# 25. Self-interest redirected: the end states of Christianity, Islam and Buddhism

Recorded 2026-11-19. This is research for a later chapter. Nothing here has been applied to the chapters.

## The idea

**The author's starting point.** Self-interest cannot be removed from man. A civilization should therefore harness it to its own ends.

**The claim to test.** Religions have long done the same thing. They do not remove self-interest; they redirect it, and what they redirect it to matters.

**The questions.** For each of three traditions, this note sets out from its primary texts:
1. what it names as the end state;
2. what it says the end state is;
3. on what conditions a man reaches it;
4. how the text itself turns the hearer's self-interest toward it.

**The texts:**
- Christianity: the Bible;
- Islam: the Qur'an; the Hadith of the Sunni and Twelver Shia collections; and the Sira;
- Buddhism: the Pali Canon, with a brief section on the Mahayana Pure Land.

Every quotation was checked by script. It was checked either against the downloaded text it cites, or against the working file drawn from that text. The renderings made for this note are marked as literal translations or glosses. Where a published translation departs from the literal sense of the original, a gloss says so.

Contents:

1. Christianity
2. Islam: the Qur'an, the Hadith and the Sira
3. Buddhism
4. Side by side
5. Observations for the later chapter
6. Open items
7. Files

---

## 1. Christianity

Bible quotations are from the King James Version, as printed in the Project Gutenberg text (ebook 10). Where the tense of the Greek matters, the Greek is from the SBL Greek New Testament, and the parsing is from MorphGNT.

### 1.1 The end state, named from the text

| Name in the text | Where | Verses in the KJV |
|---|---|---|
| "everlasting life", "eternal life", "life eternal" | John 3:16, 3:36, 17:3; Rom 6:23; Matt 25:46 | 40 in the New Testament (26, 10 and 4); Dan 12:2 in the Old |
| "the kingdom of heaven" | Matt 5:3, 7:21, 8:11 | 32, all in Matthew |
| "the kingdom of God" | John 3:3, 3:5; Mark 1:15 | 69. Also used of God's present rule. |
| "the kingdom prepared for you from the foundation of the world" | Matt 25:34 | |
| "the everlasting kingdom of our Lord and Saviour Jesus Christ" | 2 Pet 1:11 | |
| "paradise", "the paradise of God" | Luke 23:43; 2 Cor 12:4; Rev 2:7 | 3 |
| "my Father's house", "many mansions" | John 14:2 | |
| "Abraham's bosom" | Luke 16:22 | Where the beggar was carried at death |
| "the joy of thy lord" | Matt 25:21 | |
| "a rest to the people of God" | Heb 4:9 | |
| "a city which hath foundations, whose builder and maker is God"; "a better country, that is, an heavenly"; "the heavenly Jerusalem" | Heb 11:10, 11:16, 12:22 | |
| "a building of God, an house not made with hands, eternal in the heavens" | 2 Cor 5:1 | |
| "an inheritance incorruptible, and undefiled, and that fadeth not away, reserved in heaven"; "the inheritance of the saints in light" | 1 Pet 1:4; Col 1:12 | |
| "a new heaven and a new earth"; "the holy city, new Jerusalem" | Rev 21:1 to 2; 2 Pet 3:13 | |
| "the resurrection of life"; "the resurrection of the just" | John 5:29; Luke 14:14 | |
| "to be with Christ; which is far better"; "present with the Lord" | Phil 1:23; 2 Cor 5:8 | |
| the crowns: "of life", "of righteousness", "of glory that fadeth not away", "incorruptible" | Jas 1:12 and Rev 2:10; 2 Tim 4:8; 1 Pet 5:4; 1 Cor 9:25 | |

The text distinguishes two stages:

- **At death,** the believer is "with Christ" (Phil 1:23), "present with the Lord" (2 Cor 5:8), "in paradise" (Luke 23:43).
- **At the resurrection,** the body is raised and there is a new heaven and a new earth (1 Cor 15:51 to 54; Rev 21:1 to 4).

The Westminster Shorter Catechism (1647) states both in Q. 37 and Q. 38. It opens with the end itself: "Man's chief end is to glorify God, and to enjoy him forever" (Q. 1).

### 1.2 What the end state is said to be

- **God with men, and the end of loss:**
  > And I heard a great voice out of heaven saying, Behold, the tabernacle of God is with men, and he will dwell with them, and they shall be his people, and God himself shall be with them, and be their God. And God shall wipe away all tears from their eyes; and there shall be no more death, neither sorrow, nor crying, neither shall there be any more pain: for the former things are passed away. (Rev 21:3 to 4)
- **Sight of God:**
  - "And they shall see his face; and his name shall be in their foreheads." (Rev 22:4)
  - "For now we see through a glass, darkly; but then face to face: now I know in part; but then shall I know even as also I am known." (1 Cor 13:12)
  - "We know that, when he shall appear, we shall be like him; for we shall see him as he is." (1 John 3:2)
- **A raised body:**
  - "It is sown in corruption; it is raised in incorruption: It is sown in dishonour; it is raised in glory: it is sown in weakness; it is raised in power: It is sown a natural body; it is raised a spiritual body." (1 Cor 15:42 to 44)
  - "Who shall change our vile body, that it may be fashioned like unto his glorious body." (Phil 3:21)
  - "Death is swallowed up in victory." (1 Cor 15:54)
- **No marriage:** "For in the resurrection they neither marry, nor are given in marriage, but are as the angels of God in heaven." (Matt 22:30)
- **Provision:**
  - "They shall hunger no more, neither thirst any more; neither shall the sun light on them, nor any heat." (Rev 7:16)
  - "A pure river of water of life, clear as crystal ... the tree of life, which bare twelve manner of fruits, and yielded her fruit every month: and the leaves of the tree were for the healing of the nations." (Rev 22:1 to 2)
  - "Many shall come from the east and west, and shall sit down with Abraham, and Isaac, and Jacob, in the kingdom of heaven." (Matt 8:11)
- **A city without a temple or a lamp:**
  - "I saw no temple therein: for the Lord God Almighty and the Lamb are the temple of it." (Rev 21:22)
  - "There shall be no night there; and they need no candle, neither light of the sun; for the Lord God giveth them light: and they shall reign for ever and ever." (Rev 22:5)
- **Work and rule:**
  - "His servants shall serve him." (Rev 22:3)
  - "I will make thee ruler over many things: enter thou into the joy of thy lord." (Matt 25:21)
  - "Have thou authority over ten cities ... Be thou also over five cities." (Luke 19:17, 19)
- **Beyond telling:** "Eye hath not seen, nor ear heard, neither have entered into the heart of man, the things which God hath prepared for them that love him." (1 Cor 2:9)
- **Degrees:**
  - "One star differeth from another star in glory. So also is the resurrection of the dead." (1 Cor 15:41 to 42)
  - "They that turn many to righteousness as the stars for ever and ever." (Dan 12:3)
- **Excluded:**
  - "There shall in no wise enter into it any thing that defileth, neither whatsoever worketh abomination, or maketh a lie: but they which are written in the Lamb's book of life." (Rev 21:27)
  - Rev 21:8 lists "the fearful, and unbelieving, and the abominable, and murderers, and whoremongers, and sorcerers, and idolaters, and all liars."

### 1.3 The condition: one

The New Testament names one condition, faith in Christ, which is joined to repentance. It treats faith as received by grace, not earned as a wage.

- **Faith:**
  - "For God so loved the world, that he gave his only begotten Son, that whosoever believeth in him should not perish, but have everlasting life." (John 3:16)
  - "He that believeth on the Son hath everlasting life: and he that believeth not the Son shall not see life; but the wrath of God abideth on him." (John 3:36)
  - "Sirs, what must I do to be saved? And they said, Believe on the Lord Jesus Christ, and thou shalt be saved, and thy house." (Acts 16:30 to 31)
  - "That if thou shalt confess with thy mouth the Lord Jesus, and shalt believe in thine heart that God hath raised him from the dead, thou shalt be saved." (Rom 10:9)
- **One way:**
  - "I am the way, the truth, and the life: no man cometh unto the Father, but by me." (John 14:6)
  - "There is none other name under heaven given among men, whereby we must be saved." (Acts 4:12)
- **A gift, not a wage:**
  - "For by grace are ye saved through faith; and that not of yourselves: it is the gift of God: Not of works, lest any man should boast." (Eph 2:8 to 9)
  - "Now to him that worketh is the reward not reckoned of grace, but of debt. But to him that worketh not, but believeth on him that justifieth the ungodly, his faith is counted for righteousness." (Rom 4:4 to 5)
  - "For the wages of sin is death; but the gift of God is eternal life through Jesus Christ our Lord." (Rom 6:23)
  - "Not by works of righteousness which we have done, but according to his mercy he saved us." (Titus 3:5)

  Rom 4:4 and 6:23 set the wage against the gift. The wage is owed ("of debt") and is paid for sin. Eternal life is not paid; it is given.
- **Repentance, with faith:**
  - "Repent ye, and believe the gospel." (Mark 1:15)
  - "Repentance toward God, and faith toward our Lord Jesus Christ." (Acts 20:21)
  - "Except ye repent, ye shall all likewise perish." (Luke 13:3)
- **A new birth:** "Except a man be born again, he cannot see the kingdom of God." (John 3:3)
- **A faith that works.** The same texts that exclude works as the price require them as the fruit:
  - "For we are his workmanship, created in Christ Jesus unto good works, which God hath before ordained that we should walk in them." (Eph 2:10, following 2:8 to 9)
  - "Faith which worketh by love." (Gal 5:6)
  - "Faith, if it hath not works, is dead, being alone." (Jas 2:17)
  - "Not every one that saith unto me, Lord, Lord, shall enter into the kingdom of heaven; but he that doeth the will of my Father which is in heaven." (Matt 7:21)
  - In Matt 25:34 to 40 the King gives the kingdom to those who fed the hungry, gave drink to the thirsty, took in the stranger, clothed the naked, and visited the sick and the prisoner: "Inasmuch as ye have done it unto one of the least of these my brethren, ye have done it unto me."

Where the churches differ:

- **Baptism.** The text joins baptism to salvation in Mark 16:16, Acts 2:38 and 1 Pet 3:21.
- **What the reward is.** The Council of Trent (Session 6, 1547, Waterworth's translation, 1848) and the Westminster divines agree that faith is where salvation begins. They differ on the reward:
  - **Trent:** faith is "the beginning of human salvation, the foundation, and the root of all Justification" (chapter VIII). Eternal life is proposed "both as a grace mercifully promised to the sons of God through Jesus Christ, and as a reward which is according to the promise of God Himself, to be faithfully rendered to their good works and merits" (chapter XVI).
  - **Westminster:** justification is "received by faith alone" (Shorter Catechism, Q. 33).

### 1.4 Reward by works, inside the gift

Entry is given. The measure of reward within it follows the work.

- "Every man shall receive his own reward according to his own labour." (1 Cor 3:8)
- "If any man's work abide which he hath built thereupon, he shall receive a reward. If any man's work shall be burned, he shall suffer loss: but he himself shall be saved; yet so as by fire." (1 Cor 3:14 to 15)
- "For we must all appear before the judgment seat of Christ; that every one may receive the things done in his body, according to that he hath done, whether it be good or bad." (2 Cor 5:10)
- "And, behold, I come quickly; and my reward is with me, to give every man according as his work shall be." (Rev 22:12)

1 Cor 3:15 names a man who is saved and loses his reward. The text therefore keeps entry and reward apart.

### 1.5 Progressive sanctification

The Westminster Shorter Catechism, Q. 35, gives the classic definition: "Sanctification is the work of God's free grace, whereby we are renewed in the whole man after the image of God, and are enabled more and more to die unto sin, and live unto righteousness." Trent, Session 6, chapter X, describes the same growth in its own terms: "advancing from virtue to virtue, they are renewed, as the Apostle says, day by day."

The scriptural support:

- **It is God's will:** "For this is the will of God, even your sanctification." (1 Thess 4:3)
- **God works, and the believer works:** "Work out your own salvation with fear and trembling. For it is God which worketh in you both to will and to do of his good pleasure." (Phil 2:12 to 13)
- **God finishes it:** "He which hath begun a good work in you will perform it until the day of Jesus Christ." (Phil 1:6)
- **Not yet finished:** "Not as though I had already attained, either were already perfect: but I follow after ... forgetting those things which are behind, and reaching forth unto those things which are before, I press toward the mark for the prize of the high calling of God in Christ Jesus." (Phil 3:12 to 14)
- **By degrees:** "But we all, with open face beholding as in a glass the glory of the Lord, are changed into the same image from glory to glory, even as by the Spirit of the Lord." (2 Cor 3:18)
- **Day by day:** "Though our outward man perish, yet the inward man is renewed day by day." (2 Cor 4:16)
- **By a renewed mind:** "And be not conformed to this world: but be ye transformed by the renewing of your mind." (Rom 12:2)
- **Putting off and putting on:**
  - "Put off ... the old man, which is corrupt according to the deceitful lusts; And be renewed in the spirit of your mind; And ... put on the new man, which after God is created in righteousness and true holiness." (Eph 4:22 to 24)
  - "The new man, which is renewed in knowledge after the image of him that created him." (Col 3:10)
- **Toward an image:** "To be conformed to the image of his Son." (Rom 8:29)
- **Serving righteousness:** "Even so now yield your members servants to righteousness unto holiness ... ye have your fruit unto holiness, and the end everlasting life." (Rom 6:19, 22)
- **The Spirit's fruit:**
  - "Walk in the Spirit, and ye shall not fulfil the lust of the flesh." (Gal 5:16)
  - "The fruit of the Spirit is love, joy, peace, longsuffering, gentleness, goodness, faith, Meekness, temperance." (Gal 5:22 to 23)
- **Adding, step by step:** "Add to your faith virtue; and to virtue knowledge; And to knowledge temperance; and to temperance patience; and to patience godliness; And to godliness brotherly kindness; and to brotherly kindness charity ... for if ye do these things, ye shall never fall: For so an entrance shall be ministered unto you abundantly into the everlasting kingdom." (2 Pet 1:5 to 11)
- **Growing:** "But grow in grace, and in the knowledge of our Lord and Saviour Jesus Christ." (2 Pet 3:18)
- **Holiness required:** "Follow peace with all men, and holiness, without which no man shall see the Lord." (Heb 12:14)
- **Done and still in progress:** "For by one offering he hath perfected for ever them that are sanctified." (Heb 10:14)
- **Hope purifies:** "And every man that hath this hope in him purifieth himself, even as he is pure." (1 John 3:3)

The Greek shows that the KJV's "are sanctified" and "are changed" are in fact continuing tenses. Parsing is from MorphGNT on the SBLGNT text.

| Verse | KJV | Greek | Parsing | Sense |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Cor 6:11 | "ye are sanctified" | ἡγιάσθητε | aorist passive | done once: "ye were sanctified" |
| Eph 2:8 | "are ye saved" | σεσῳσμένοι | perfect passive participle | done, with a lasting result |
| Heb 10:14 | "hath perfected for ever" | τετελείωκεν | perfect active | done, with a lasting result |
| Heb 10:14 | "them that are sanctified" | ἁγιαζομένους | present passive participle | "those being sanctified" |
| 2 Cor 3:18 | "are changed" | μεταμορφούμεθα | present passive | "are being transformed" |
| 2 Cor 4:16 | "is renewed day by day" | ἀνακαινοῦται | present passive | continuing |
| Col 3:10 | "which is renewed" | ἀνακαινούμενον | present passive participle | "being renewed" |
| Rom 12:2 | "be ye transformed" | μεταμορφοῦσθε | present passive imperative | "go on being transformed" |
| Eph 4:23 | "be renewed" | ἀνανεοῦσθαι | present passive infinitive | continuing |
| 1 Cor 1:18 | "us which are saved" | σῳζομένοις | present passive participle | "us who are being saved" |
| Phil 2:12 | "work out" | κατεργάζεσθε | present middle imperative | continuing |
| 2 Pet 3:18 | "grow" | αὐξάνετε | present active imperative | continuing |
| 1 John 3:3 | "purifieth himself" | ἁγνίζει | present active | continuing |
| Phil 1:6 | "will perform it" | ἐπιτελέσει | future active | "will complete" |

Heb 10:14 holds both at once: those whom one offering "hath perfected for ever" are "being sanctified."

### 1.6 How the text redirects self-interest

The text does not tell the hearer to stop seeking gain. It names a better gain and tells him where to store it.

- **Treasure, moved:**
  > Lay not up for yourselves treasures upon earth, where moth and rust doth corrupt, and where thieves break through and steal: But lay up for yourselves treasures in heaven, where neither moth nor rust doth corrupt, and where thieves do not break through nor steal: For where your treasure is, there will your heart be also. (Matt 6:19 to 21)

  Further texts:
  - "Provide yourselves bags which wax not old, a treasure in the heavens that faileth not." (Luke 12:33)
  - Of the rich: "That they do good, that they be rich in good works, ready to distribute, willing to communicate; Laying up in store for themselves a good foundation against the time to come, that they may lay hold on eternal life." (1 Tim 6:18 to 19)
- **Profit and exchange:**
  - "For what is a man profited, if he shall gain the whole world, and lose his own soul? or what shall a man give in exchange for his soul?" (Matt 16:26)
  - "The kingdom of heaven is like unto treasure hid in a field; the which when a man hath found, he hideth, and for joy thereof goeth and selleth all that he hath, and buyeth that field." (Matt 13:44)
  - The merchant who finds "one pearl of great price ... sold all that he had, and bought it." (Matt 13:46)
- **Repayment, moved from men to God:**
  - Of alms done to be seen: "They have their reward." (Matt 6:2)
  - Of alms done in secret: "Thy Father which seeth in secret himself shall reward thee openly." (Matt 6:4)
  - "When thou makest a feast, call the poor, the maimed, the lame, the blind: And thou shalt be blessed; for they cannot recompense thee: for thou shalt be recompensed at the resurrection of the just." (Luke 14:13 to 14)
  - "Love ye your enemies, and do good, and lend, hoping for nothing again; and your reward shall be great." (Luke 6:35)
  - "For if ye love them which love you, what reward have ye?" (Matt 5:46)
- **Loss now, return now and later:** "There is no man that hath left house, or brethren ... for my sake, and the gospel's, But he shall receive an hundredfold now in this time ... with persecutions; and in the world to come eternal life." (Mark 10:29 to 30)
- **Ambition turned to service:** "Whosoever will be great among you, let him be your minister; And whosoever will be chief among you, let him be your servant." (Matt 20:26 to 27)
- **Rivalry turned to discipline:** "So run, that ye may obtain. And every man that striveth for the mastery is temperate in all things. Now they do it to obtain a corruptible crown; but we an incorruptible." (1 Cor 9:24 to 25)
- **Taking turned to labour, and labour to giving:** "Let him that stole steal no more: but rather let him labour, working with his hands the thing which is good, that he may have to give to him that needeth." (Eph 4:28)

  Further texts:
  - "Work with your own hands ... that ye may have lack of nothing." (1 Thess 4:11 to 12)
  - "If any would not work, neither should he eat." (2 Thess 3:10)
  - "Whatsoever ye do, do it heartily, as to the Lord, and not unto men; Knowing that of the Lord ye shall receive the reward of the inheritance." (Col 3:23 to 24)
- **Sowing and reaping:** "Whatsoever a man soweth, that shall he also reap ... in due season we shall reap, if we faint not." (Gal 6:7, 9)
- **Weighing the present against the end:**
  - "The sufferings of this present time are not worthy to be compared with the glory which shall be revealed in us." (Rom 8:18)
  - "Our light affliction, which is but for a moment, worketh for us a far more exceeding and eternal weight of glory." (2 Cor 4:17)
  - Moses chose "rather to suffer affliction with the people of God, than to enjoy the pleasures of sin for a season ... for he had respect unto the recompence of the reward." (Heb 11:25 to 26)
  - Christ "for the joy that was set before him endured the cross." (Heb 12:2)
- **God as one who pays:** "He that cometh to God must believe that he is, and that he is a rewarder of them that diligently seek him." (Heb 11:6)
- **The self kept by being given:** "Whosoever will save his life shall lose it: and whosoever will lose his life for my sake shall find it." (Matt 16:25)

The pattern:

1. Entry is a gift, received by faith.
2. Self-interest is then given three goals:
   - reward in proportion to the work (1 Cor 3:8 to 15);
   - likeness to Christ, reached by degrees (2 Cor 3:18);
   - the sight of God (1 John 3:2 to 3).
3. The text directs the means toward other men. It names giving, service, enemy-love, honest labour, and hospitality to those who cannot repay.

## 2. Islam: the Qur'an, the Hadith and the Sira

Sections 2.1 to 2.7 cover the Qur'an, 2.8 to 2.12 the Hadith, and 2.13 the Sira. Section 2.14 tests a claim about fear, hope and the guarantee. Section 2.15 compares, literally, what is promised to men and to women with how narrow the way in is, and 2.16 draws the pattern across them.

### Sources and conventions for the Qur'an

**The Arabic text.**
- The Arabic is the vocalized Tanzil text (`quran-simple`, the Hafs reading), from api.alquran.cloud.
- It was checked letter by letter against quran.com's `text_imlaei`. All 6,236 verses agree in their consonants.
- Arabic is given in block quotes. Verses of one passage are separated by ۝, and the rub' al-hizb ornament is dropped. Arabic is kept out of the chapters themselves, because the PDF font lacks it.

**The translations.**
- Every English rendering was made from the Arabic for this note. It follows the Arabic word order where English allows, and words in [brackets] are supplied.
- Each was checked against the quran.com word-by-word glosses and against Pickthall (1930).
- They follow the renderings of chapter 12.

**Fixed renderings:**

| Arabic | Rendering | Literal sense, where it differs |
|---|---|---|
| الله | God | |
| رب | Lord | |
| الجنة / جنات | the Garden / Gardens | "garden", from j-n-n, "to cover" |
| آمنوا | believed | |
| عملوا الصالحات | did righteous [deeds] | "did the righteous [ones]"; "deeds" is supplied |
| المتقون / اتقوا | the God-fearing / feared [God] | "those who guard [themselves]" / "guarded [themselves]", from w-q-y |
| في سبيل الله | in the way of God | |
| قاتلوا / قتلوا | fought / were killed | |
| جاهدوا | strove | "exerted", the verb of jihad |
| هاجروا | emigrated | |
| أجر | reward | "wage, hire" |
| الفوز العظيم | the great attainment | "the great winning" |
| خالدين فيها أبدا | abiding in it forever | |

The working file, with all 393 verses quoted and 21 doubts recorded, is `ext-ref/religion_ends/quran_conditions.md`.

### 2.1 The end state, named from the text

**Method.** Some form of *janna*, "garden", names the hereafter reward in 112 verses:

| Form | Verses |
|---|---|
| *al-janna*, "the Garden" | 44 |
| *janna*, "a Garden" | 10 |
| *jannat*, "Gardens" | 54 |
| *jannatan*, "two Gardens" | 3 |
| *janna-ti*, "My Garden" | 1 |

Gardens of this world, Adam's garden, and the same letters read as *jinn* or *jinna* (madness) were set aside. The table gives every other name or naming phrase found. "Name" means the text uses the phrase as a fixed designation. Where a classical exegete calls a phrase a name (*ism*), that is noted. The exegesis cited is al-Tabari, *Jami' al-bayan*, and the lexicon is Lane.

| Arabic | Transliteration | Literal sense | Verses (count) | Name or description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| الجنة | al-janna | "the garden"; from j-n-n, "to cover", a garden whose trees cover the ground (Lane) | 44, e.g. 2:82, 7:40, 9:111, 26:90 | Name; "the companions of the Garden" recurs |
| جنات / جنتان / جنتي | jannat / jannatan / janna-ti | "Gardens" / "two Gardens" / "My Garden" | 54 / 3 (55:46, 54, 62) / 1 (89:30) | Description |
| جنات الفردوس / الفردوس | jannat al-firdaws | "garden with vines" in Arabic usage, or "a garden, in Greek" (Mujahid, in al-Tabari) | 18:107, 23:11 (2) | Name; al-Tabari: "the main part of the Garden"; Qatada: "its height, middle and best part" |
| جنات عدن | jannat 'adn | "Gardens of settled residence", from 'adana, "to stay"; usually rendered "Eden" | 11: 9:72, 13:23, 16:31, 18:31, 19:61, 20:76, 35:33, 38:50, 40:8, 61:12, 98:8 | Both views in al-Tabari: "residence from which no one departs," or a name of the inner Garden, a palace, or a city |
| جنات النعيم / جنة نعيم | jannat al-na'im | "Gardens of bliss" | 10, e.g. 10:9, 56:12, 56:89 | Description |
| جنة المأوى | jannat al-ma'wa | "Garden of refuge", the place one betakes oneself to | 32:19, 53:15 (2); al-ma'wa of the Garden 79:41 | Description; "near the lote-tree of the Boundary" (53:14 to 15) |
| جنة الخلد | jannat al-khuld | "Garden of everlastingness" | 25:15 (1) | Description |
| دار السلام | dar al-salam | "abode of peace (safety)"; *al-Salam* is also a name of God (59:23) | 6:127, 10:25 (2) | Description; al-Tabari: "the abode of God ... and it is His Garden" |
| دار المقامة | dar al-muqama | "abode of residence" | 35:35 (1) | Description |
| الدار الآخرة | al-dar al-akhira | "the last abode" | 9, e.g. 28:83, 29:64 | Description |
| الحيوان | al-hayawan | "[true] life", an intensive of *hayat* | 29:64 (1) | Predicate: "the abode of the hereafter, it is the [true] life" |
| دار المتقين / دار القرار / عقبى الدار | dar al-muttaqin / dar al-qarar / 'uqba al-dar | "abode of the God-fearing" / "abode of settling" / "the outcome of the abode" | 16:30 / 40:39 / 13:22, 24, 42 | Description |
| عليون | 'illiyyun | "height above height" (al-Tabari) | 83:18 to 19 (2) | Where "the book of the righteous" is; set against *sijjin* (83:7) |
| مقعد صدق / قدم صدق / مقام أمين | maq'ad sidq / qadam sidq / maqam amin | "seat of truth" / "footing of truth" / "secure station" | 54:55 / 10:2 / 44:51 | Description |
| رضوان | ridwan | "good pleasure" (of God) | as part of the reward: 3:15, 9:21, 9:72, 57:20 (4) | Not a place; listed beside the Gardens and called "greater" (9:72) |
| الحسنى (وزيادة) | al-husna (wa-ziyada) | "the best (and more)" | 4:95, 10:26, 13:18, 21:101, 53:31, 57:10 and others (7) | Description; some early authorities in al-Tabari take the "more" as "looking at God's face" |
| حسن مآب | husn ma'ab | "a fair place of return" | 3:14, 13:29, 38:25, 38:40, 38:49 (5) | Description; 38:50 sets Gardens of 'Adn beside it |
| طوبى | tuba | "good", feminine of *atyab* | 13:29 (1) | Contested; al-Tabari reports it as "a name of the names of the Garden," or "how good for them," or "a tree in the Garden" |
| الغرفة / غرف | al-ghurfa / ghuraf | "the high chamber", "lofty rooms" | 25:75, 29:58, 34:37, 39:20 (4) | Description |
| جنة عرضها السماوات والأرض | janna 'arduha al-samawat wa-l-ard | "a Garden whose width is the heavens and the earth" | 3:133, 57:21 (2) | Description |
| رحمة الله | rahmat Allah | "the mercy of God", as the place they abide in | 3:107 (1); "admitted into His mercy" 4:175, 45:30, 76:31 | Description; al-Tabari: "that is, in His Garden" |
| الفوز العظيم | al-fawz al-'azim | "the great attainment" (literally "winning") | 18 verses with its variants, e.g. 9:111, 61:12, 64:9 | A label closing a reward verse |
| نزل | nuzul | "lodging": what is set out for an arriving guest | 3:198, 18:107, 32:19, 41:32 (4); of the Fire 56:56 | Description |
| روضات / جنة عالية / مساكن طيبة / مدخل كريم | rawdat / janna 'aliya / masakin tayyiba / mudkhal karim | "meadows" / "a lofty Garden" / "good dwellings" / "a noble entrance" | 42:22 / 69:22, 88:10 / 9:72, 61:12 / 4:31, 22:59 | Description |
| الدرجات العلى | al-darajat al-'ula | "the highest degrees" | 20:75 (1) | Description |
| يوم الخلود | yawm al-khulud | "the Day of Everlastingness" | 50:34 (1) | The time of entry, not the place |

The other end state, names only. Each is given with one verse:
- **جهنم** *jahannam*, a proper name, 77 verses (78:21);
- **النار** *al-nar*, "the Fire", 94 verses of the hereafter (3:131);
- **السعير** *al-sa'ir*, "the Blaze" (67:10);
- **سقر** *saqar* (74:26 to 27);
- **الجحيم** *al-jahim*, "the blazing fire" (79:39);
- **الحطمة** *al-hutama*, "the Crusher" (104:4 to 5);
- **هاوية** *hawiya*, "a pit" (101:9);
- **لظى** *laza*, "flame" (70:15).

al-Tabari calls *saqar*, *laza* and *al-hutama* each "a name of the names of Jahannam" or of the Fire. Between the two lies **الأعراف** *al-a'raf*, "the heights" (7:46 to 49). The book of the wicked is in **سجين** *sijjin* (83:7).

### 2.2 What the Garden is said to be

**Rivers and springs.**
- "Beneath which rivers flow" (*tajri min tahtiha al-anhar*) is said of the reward in 36 verses.
- One verse names four rivers:

**47:15**

> مَثَلُ الْجَنَّةِ الَّتِي وُعِدَ الْمُتَّقُونَ ۖ فِيهَا أَنْهَارٌ مِنْ مَاءٍ غَيْرِ آسِنٍ وَأَنْهَارٌ مِنْ لَبَنٍ لَمْ يَتَغَيَّرْ طَعْمُهُ وَأَنْهَارٌ مِنْ خَمْرٍ لَذَّةٍ لِلشَّارِبِينَ وَأَنْهَارٌ مِنْ عَسَلٍ مُصَفًّى ۖ وَلَهُمْ فِيهَا مِنْ كُلِّ الثَّمَرَاتِ وَمَغْفِرَةٌ مِنْ رَبِّهِمْ ۖ كَمَنْ هُوَ خَالِدٌ فِي النَّارِ وَسُقُوا مَاءً حَمِيمًا فَقَطَّعَ أَمْعَاءَهُمْ

The likeness of the Garden which the God-fearing were promised: in it are rivers of water not stagnant, and rivers of milk whose taste has not changed, and rivers of wine, a delight to the drinkers, and rivers of honey purified; and for them in it [is] of all the fruits, and forgiveness from their Lord. [Is that] like one who is abiding in the Fire, and they are given to drink boiling water, so that it cuts their bowels?

The springs are named in 76:5 to 6 and 76:17 to 18. They are given to drink:
- "a cup whose mixture has been kāfūr" (camphor);
- "a cup whose mixture has been zanjabīl, a spring in it called salsabīl" (*zanjabil* is ginger);
- in 83:25 to 28, "a pure wine sealed, whose seal is musk ... and its mixture is of *tasnim*, a spring from which those brought near drink."

108:1 adds: "Indeed, We have given you al-Kawthar."

**Food and drink.**

**56:17 to 21**

> يَطُوفُ عَلَيْهِمْ وِلْدَانٌ مُخَلَّدُونَ ۝ بِأَكْوَابٍ وَأَبَارِيقَ وَكَأْسٍ مِنْ مَعِينٍ ۝ لَا يُصَدَّعُونَ عَنْهَا وَلَا يُنْزِفُونَ ۝ وَفَاكِهَةٍ مِمَّا يَتَخَيَّرُونَ ۝ وَلَحْمِ طَيْرٍ مِمَّا يَشْتَهُونَ

There go round about them youths made everlasting, with goblets and ewers and a cup from a flowing spring; they get no headache from it, nor are they drained; and fruit of what they choose, and flesh of fowl of what they desire.

- In 37:45 to 47 the cup is "white, a delight to the drinkers; no *ghawl* is in it." *Ghawl* is "what seizes and carries a person off": headache, loss of reason, or harm.
- 36:57: "For them in it is fruit, and for them is whatever they call for."

**Dwellings and furnishings.**
- They have couches, cushions and carpets: "couches raised, and goblets set down, and cushions ranged, and carpets spread out" (88:13 to 16).
- They have lofty rooms (25:75, 39:20), "goodly dwellings" (9:72), pavilions (55:72), and shade:

**76:13 to 14**

> مُتَّكِئِينَ فِيهَا عَلَى الْأَرَائِكِ ۖ لَا يَرَوْنَ فِيهَا شَمْسًا وَلَا زَمْهَرِيرًا ۝ وَدَانِيَةً عَلَيْهِمْ ظِلَالُهَا وَذُلِّلَتْ قُطُوفُهَا تَذْلِيلًا

Reclining in it on the canopied couches, they see in it neither sun nor bitter cold; and near over them its shades, and its clusters made low, lowered.

**Garments and adornment.**

**18:31**

> أُولَٰئِكَ لَهُمْ جَنَّاتُ عَدْنٍ تَجْرِي مِنْ تَحْتِهِمُ الْأَنْهَارُ يُحَلَّوْنَ فِيهَا مِنْ أَسَاوِرَ مِنْ ذَهَبٍ وَيَلْبَسُونَ ثِيَابًا خُضْرًا مِنْ سُنْدُسٍ وَإِسْتَبْرَقٍ مُتَّكِئِينَ فِيهَا عَلَى الْأَرَائِكِ ۚ نِعْمَ الثَّوَابُ وَحَسُنَتْ مُرْتَفَقًا

Those, for them are Gardens of ʿAdn; there flow from beneath them the rivers; they are adorned in it with bracelets of gold, and they wear green garments of fine silk and brocade, reclining in it on the canopied couches. Excellent is the reward, and fair it is as a resting-place.

22:23 and 35:33 add pearls. 76:21 gives "bracelets of silver."

**Companions.**
- "Purified spouses" (*azwaj mutahhara*) appear in 2:25 (quoted above), 3:15 and 4:57. al-Tabari explains *mutahhara*: "purified from every harm, filth and suspicion found in the women of this world, of menstruation and childbed."
- "White-eyed, wide-eyed [ones]" (*hur 'in*) appear in 44:54, 52:20 and 56:22 to 23. *Hawar* is "intense whiteness of the white of the eye and intense blackness of the black thereof" (Lane, from the *Sihah*). *'Ayna'* is "wide in the eye."

**52:20 to 24**

> مُتَّكِئِينَ عَلَىٰ سُرُرٍ مَصْفُوفَةٍ ۖ وَزَوَّجْنَاهُمْ بِحُورٍ عِينٍ ۝ وَالَّذِينَ آمَنُوا وَاتَّبَعَتْهُمْ ذُرِّيَّتُهُمْ بِإِيمَانٍ أَلْحَقْنَا بِهِمْ ذُرِّيَّتَهُمْ وَمَا أَلَتْنَاهُمْ مِنْ عَمَلِهِمْ مِنْ شَيْءٍ ۚ كُلُّ امْرِئٍ بِمَا كَسَبَ رَهِينٌ ۝ وَأَمْدَدْنَاهُمْ بِفَاكِهَةٍ وَلَحْمٍ مِمَّا يَشْتَهُونَ ۝ يَتَنَازَعُونَ فِيهَا كَأْسًا لَا لَغْوٌ فِيهَا وَلَا تَأْثِيمٌ ۝ وَيَطُوفُ عَلَيْهِمْ غِلْمَانٌ لَهُمْ كَأَنَّهُمْ لُؤْلُؤٌ مَكْنُونٌ

Reclining on couches ranged; and We paired them with white-eyed, wide-eyed [ones]. And those who believed, and their offspring followed them in faith, We joined their offspring to them, and We did not deprive them of anything of their work; every man is pledged for what he earned. And We supplied them with fruit and meat of what they desire. They pass to one another in it a cup in which [there is] no idle talk and no sin-talk. And there go round about them lads of theirs, as if they were pearls hidden.

In 44:54 and 52:20, *zawwajnahum* may be read "We paired them with" (al-Tabari on 52:20) or "We married them to" (Mujahid).

**55:56 to 58**

> فِيهِنَّ قَاصِرَاتُ الطَّرْفِ لَمْ يَطْمِثْهُنَّ إِنْسٌ قَبْلَهُمْ وَلَا جَانٌّ ۝ فَبِأَيِّ آلَاءِ رَبِّكُمَا تُكَذِّبَانِ ۝ كَأَنَّهُنَّ الْيَاقُوتُ وَالْمَرْجَانُ

In them [are maidens] restraining [their] glances, whom no man before them has touched, nor jinn. So which of the favours of your Lord do you two deny? As if they were rubies and coral.

**56:35 to 38**

> إِنَّا أَنْشَأْنَاهُنَّ إِنْشَاءً ۝ فَجَعَلْنَاهُنَّ أَبْكَارًا ۝ عُرُبًا أَتْرَابًا ۝ لِأَصْحَابِ الْيَمِينِ

Indeed, We produced them a producing, and made them virgins, loving, age-mates [of one another], for the companions of the right.

**78:31 to 34**

> إِنَّ لِلْمُتَّقِينَ مَفَازًا ۝ حَدَائِقَ وَأَعْنَابًا ۝ وَكَوَاعِبَ أَتْرَابًا ۝ وَكَأْسًا دِهَاقًا

Indeed, for the God-fearing is a place of winning: enclosed gardens and grapevines, and swelling-breasted [maidens], age-mates [of one another], and a cup full.

Further companions:
- **Youths:** "youths made everlasting" (*wildan mukhalladun*, 56:17, 76:19), and "lads of theirs, as if they were pearls hidden" (52:24).
- **Families:** "whoever was righteous among their fathers and their spouses and their offspring" (13:23); "you and your spouses" (43:70).

**Social and inner state.**

**56:25 to 26**

> لَا يَسْمَعُونَ فِيهَا لَغْوًا وَلَا تَأْثِيمًا ۝ إِلَّا قِيلًا سَلَامًا سَلَامًا

They hear in it no idle talk and no sin-talk, only the saying "Peace, peace."

**15:45 to 48**

> إِنَّ الْمُتَّقِينَ فِي جَنَّاتٍ وَعُيُونٍ ۝ ادْخُلُوهَا بِسَلَامٍ آمِنِينَ ۝ وَنَزَعْنَا مَا فِي صُدُورِهِمْ مِنْ غِلٍّ إِخْوَانًا عَلَىٰ سُرُرٍ مُتَقَابِلِينَ ۝ لَا يَمَسُّهُمْ فِيهَا نَصَبٌ وَمَا هُمْ مِنْهَا بِمُخْرَجِينَ

Indeed, the God-fearing [are] in Gardens and springs: "Enter it in peace, secure." And We removed what was in their breasts of rancour: brothers, on couches, facing one another. No toil touches them in it, and they are not from it to be expelled.

**44:55 to 57**

> يَدْعُونَ فِيهَا بِكُلِّ فَاكِهَةٍ آمِنِينَ ۝ لَا يَذُوقُونَ فِيهَا الْمَوْتَ إِلَّا الْمَوْتَةَ الْأُولَىٰ ۖ وَوَقَاهُمْ عَذَابَ الْجَحِيمِ ۝ فَضْلًا مِنْ رَبِّكَ ۚ ذَٰلِكَ هُوَ الْفَوْزُ الْعَظِيمُ

They call in it for every fruit, secure. They do not taste death in it, except the first death; and He protected them from the punishment of al-Jaḥīm: a bounty from your Lord. That, it is the great attainment.

**Desire fulfilled.**

**43:70 to 72**

> ادْخُلُوا الْجَنَّةَ أَنْتُمْ وَأَزْوَاجُكُمْ تُحْبَرُونَ ۝ يُطَافُ عَلَيْهِمْ بِصِحَافٍ مِنْ ذَهَبٍ وَأَكْوَابٍ ۖ وَفِيهَا مَا تَشْتَهِيهِ الْأَنْفُسُ وَتَلَذُّ الْأَعْيُنُ ۖ وَأَنْتُمْ فِيهَا خَالِدُونَ ۝ وَتِلْكَ الْجَنَّةُ الَّتِي أُورِثْتُمُوهَا بِمَا كُنْتُمْ تَعْمَلُونَ

"Enter the Garden, you and your spouses, made glad." There are passed round among them plates of gold and goblets; and in it is whatever the souls desire and the eyes delight in; and you in it are abiding. And that is the Garden which you have been made to inherit for what you used to do.

**50:35**

> لَهُمْ مَا يَشَاءُونَ فِيهَا وَلَدَيْنَا مَزِيدٌ

For them is whatever they wish in it, and with Us is more.

**32:17**

> فَلَا تَعْلَمُ نَفْسٌ مَا أُخْفِيَ لَهُمْ مِنْ قُرَّةِ أَعْيُنٍ جَزَاءً بِمَا كَانُوا يَعْمَلُونَ

And no soul knows what has been hidden for them of coolness of eyes, as a recompense for what they used to do.

**God.**

**75:22 to 23**

> وُجُوهٌ يَوْمَئِذٍ نَاضِرَةٌ ۝ إِلَىٰ رَبِّهَا نَاظِرَةٌ

Faces that day [are] radiant, looking at their Lord.

In 75:23, *nazira* is "looking at," which is al-Tabari's choice. Mujahid read it as "waiting for the reward from their Lord."

**9:72**

> وَعَدَ اللَّهُ الْمُؤْمِنِينَ وَالْمُؤْمِنَاتِ جَنَّاتٍ تَجْرِي مِنْ تَحْتِهَا الْأَنْهَارُ خَالِدِينَ فِيهَا وَمَسَاكِنَ طَيِّبَةً فِي جَنَّاتِ عَدْنٍ ۚ وَرِضْوَانٌ مِنَ اللَّهِ أَكْبَرُ ۚ ذَٰلِكَ هُوَ الْفَوْزُ الْعَظِيمُ

God promised the believing men and the believing women Gardens beneath which rivers flow, abiding in it, and goodly dwellings in Gardens of ʿAdn; and good pleasure from God is greater. That, it is the great attainment.

**10:9 to 10**

> إِنَّ الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا وَعَمِلُوا الصَّالِحَاتِ يَهْدِيهِمْ رَبُّهُمْ بِإِيمَانِهِمْ ۖ تَجْرِي مِنْ تَحْتِهِمُ الْأَنْهَارُ فِي جَنَّاتِ النَّعِيمِ ۝ دَعْوَاهُمْ فِيهَا سُبْحَانَكَ اللَّهُمَّ وَتَحِيَّتُهُمْ فِيهَا سَلَامٌ ۚ وَآخِرُ دَعْوَاهُمْ أَنِ الْحَمْدُ لِلَّهِ رَبِّ الْعَالَمِينَ

Indeed, those who believed and did righteous [deeds], their Lord guides them by their faith; there flow from beneath them the rivers, in the Gardens of Bliss. Their call in it: "Glory to You, O God"; and their greeting in it: "Peace"; and the last of their call: "Praise be to God, Lord of the worlds."

98:8, 5:119, 9:100 and 58:22 add: "God is pleased with them and they are pleased with Him." The phrase "on couches, looking" (83:23, 83:35) names no object. al-Tabari supplies "what God has given them" and, in 83:35, the unbelievers in the Fire. Nearness is promised to "those brought near" (*al-muqarrabun*, 56:11, 83:28), and "in a seat of truth near a Sovereign, Powerful" (54:55).

**How the descriptions differ by group** (sura 56 and sura 55).
- **The foremost, "those brought near"** (56:12 to 26), receive:
  - woven couches;
  - youths with goblets and a cup;
  - fruit "of what they choose" and "flesh of fowl of what they desire";
  - the *hur 'in*;
  - "only the saying 'Peace, peace.'"
- **The companions of the right** (56:27 to 40) receive:
  - "lote-trees with thorns removed ... and shade extended, and water poured out, and fruit abundant";
  - raised spreads;
  - "virgins, loving, age-mates [of one another]."
- **The two pairs of Gardens.** Sura 55 gives two pairs, the second "below" or "besides" the first (55:62). al-Tabari reads it as "below them in the degrees."

### 2.3 The conditions: every way the Qur'an states

**Method.** Every verse was read in which the hereafter Garden, or another name for the hereafter reward, is promised to some person or group.
- The search covered twenty-eight terms:
  - the forms of *janna*;
  - al-Firdaws, 'Adn, al-Na'im, al-Ma'wa and al-Khuld;
  - *ridwan* and 'Illiyyin;
  - Dar al-Salam and Dar al-Muqama;
  - the companions of the right;
  - al-husna, "the best";
  - and others.
- 252 verses were hit. Of these, 88 were excluded: earthly gardens, Adam's garden, *jinna* (madness) and *jinn*, and false hits. Each exclusion is listed in the working file.
- 146 verses promise the Garden by name to someone.
- 76 more promise "a great reward", "forgiveness and a noble provision", or "the great attainment" without naming the Garden.

Each verse was classed by the condition its own words state, or the words of the verses that define its "those who." Nothing was classed by commentary. A verse often names several conditions, so the counts overlap. The counts are given as **Garden-name verses / other promise verses**.

The most frequent formula is "those who believed and did righteous [deeds]" (*alladhina amanu wa-'amilu al-salihat*). With its close variants it occurs in 69 verses.

#### The conditions, in order of frequency

**1. Belief joined to righteous deeds** (30 / 19). Further references: 2:82, 4:57, 4:122, 7:42, 10:9, 13:29, 18:107, 22:14, 22:23, 29:58, 30:15, 31:8, 32:19, 42:22, 47:12, 64:9, 65:11, 85:11, 98:7 to 8, 103:3.

**2:25**

> وَبَشِّرِ الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا وَعَمِلُوا الصَّالِحَاتِ أَنَّ لَهُمْ جَنَّاتٍ تَجْرِي مِنْ تَحْتِهَا الْأَنْهَارُ ۖ كُلَّمَا رُزِقُوا مِنْهَا مِنْ ثَمَرَةٍ رِزْقًا ۙ قَالُوا هَٰذَا الَّذِي رُزِقْنَا مِنْ قَبْلُ ۖ وَأُتُوا بِهِ مُتَشَابِهًا ۖ وَلَهُمْ فِيهَا أَزْوَاجٌ مُطَهَّرَةٌ ۖ وَهُمْ فِيهَا خَالِدُونَ

And give good news to those who believed and did righteous [deeds], that for them [are] Gardens beneath which rivers flow. Whenever they are provided from them with fruit as provision, they say, "This is what we were provided before," and they are given it in resemblance; and for them in them [are] purified spouses, and they in them [are] abiding.

**4:124**

> وَمَنْ يَعْمَلْ مِنَ الصَّالِحَاتِ مِنْ ذَكَرٍ أَوْ أُنْثَىٰ وَهُوَ مُؤْمِنٌ فَأُولَٰئِكَ يَدْخُلُونَ الْجَنَّةَ وَلَا يُظْلَمُونَ نَقِيرًا

And whoever does of the righteous [deeds], from male or female, while he is a believer, then those will enter the Garden, and they will not be wronged [by] a speck (naqir).

**2. Belief** (18 / 13), named without "righteous [deeds]" in the same clause. In most of these verses the defining context adds deeds (9:71, 9:20, 9:88, 61:11).

**57:21**

> سَابِقُوا إِلَىٰ مَغْفِرَةٍ مِنْ رَبِّكُمْ وَجَنَّةٍ عَرْضُهَا كَعَرْضِ السَّمَاءِ وَالْأَرْضِ أُعِدَّتْ لِلَّذِينَ آمَنُوا بِاللَّهِ وَرُسُلِهِ ۚ ذَٰلِكَ فَضْلُ اللَّهِ يُؤْتِيهِ مَنْ يَشَاءُ ۚ وَاللَّهُ ذُو الْفَضْلِ الْعَظِيمِ

Race to forgiveness from your Lord and a Garden whose width is like the width of the heaven and the earth, prepared for those who believed in God and His messengers. That is the bounty of God; He gives it to whom He wills; and God is the possessor of great bounty.

**3. Fear of God, *taqwa*** (33 / 8). The God-fearing are most often named as the recipients without being defined in the same passage. The definitions are in 3:133 to 135 and 51:15 to 19 (the catalogues below), and in 39:33.

**19:63**

> تِلْكَ الْجَنَّةُ الَّتِي نُورِثُ مِنْ عِبَادِنَا مَنْ كَانَ تَقِيًّا

That is the Garden which We give as inheritance, of Our servants, to whoever was God-fearing (taqiyya).

Further references: 3:15, 3:198, 13:35, 15:45, 16:30 to 31, 25:15, 26:90, 38:49, 39:73 to 74, 44:51, 47:15, 50:31, 52:17, 54:54, 68:34, 78:31.

**4. Fearing the standing before one's Lord, and restraining the soul from desire** (10 / 4). The words are *khawf* and *khashya*, distinct from *taqwa*.

**79:37 to 41**

> فَأَمَّا مَنْ طَغَىٰ ۝ وَآثَرَ الْحَيَاةَ الدُّنْيَا ۝ فَإِنَّ الْجَحِيمَ هِيَ الْمَأْوَىٰ ۝ وَأَمَّا مَنْ خَافَ مَقَامَ رَبِّهِ وَنَهَى النَّفْسَ عَنِ الْهَوَىٰ ۝ فَإِنَّ الْجَنَّةَ هِيَ الْمَأْوَىٰ

So as for whoever transgressed and preferred the life of this world, then indeed Hellfire (al-jahim), it is the refuge (al-ma'wa). And as for whoever feared the standing of his Lord and forbade the soul [its] desire (al-hawa), then indeed the Garden, it is the refuge.

**55:46**

> وَلِمَنْ خَافَ مَقَامَ رَبِّهِ جَنَّتَانِ

And for whoever feared the standing (maqam) of his Lord, two Gardens.

98:8 ends: "That is for whoever feared (*khashiya*) his Lord." See also 13:21, 50:33, 52:26, 70:27.

**5. Obedience to God and His Messenger** (4 / 7).

**4:13 to 14**

> تِلْكَ حُدُودُ اللَّهِ ۚ وَمَنْ يُطِعِ اللَّهَ وَرَسُولَهُ يُدْخِلْهُ جَنَّاتٍ تَجْرِي مِنْ تَحْتِهَا الْأَنْهَارُ خَالِدِينَ فِيهَا ۚ وَذَٰلِكَ الْفَوْزُ الْعَظِيمُ ۝ وَمَنْ يَعْصِ اللَّهَ وَرَسُولَهُ وَيَتَعَدَّ حُدُودَهُ يُدْخِلْهُ نَارًا خَالِدًا فِيهَا وَلَهُ عَذَابٌ مُهِينٌ

Those are the limits of God. And whoever obeys God and His Messenger, He will admit him to Gardens beneath which rivers flow, abiding in it; and that is the great attainment. And whoever disobeys God and His Messenger and oversteps His limits, He will admit him to a Fire, abiding in it, and for him [is] a humiliating punishment.

48:17 excuses the blind, the lame and the sick, and promises Gardens to "whoever obeys God and His Messenger." It follows 48:16, where obedience is to answer the call: "you will fight them, or they will submit." 4:69 places "whoever obeys God and the Messenger" "with those upon whom God has bestowed favor, of the prophets and the truthful and the witnesses (*al-shuhada'*) and the righteous." See also 33:71 and 72:23.

**6. Fighting, killing and being killed in the way of God** (5 / 4). The verse states the condition as a sale:

**9:111 to 112**

> إِنَّ اللَّهَ اشْتَرَىٰ مِنَ الْمُؤْمِنِينَ أَنْفُسَهُمْ وَأَمْوَالَهُمْ بِأَنَّ لَهُمُ الْجَنَّةَ ۚ يُقَاتِلُونَ فِي سَبِيلِ اللَّهِ فَيَقْتُلُونَ وَيُقْتَلُونَ ۖ وَعْدًا عَلَيْهِ حَقًّا فِي التَّوْرَاةِ وَالْإِنْجِيلِ وَالْقُرْآنِ ۚ وَمَنْ أَوْفَىٰ بِعَهْدِهِ مِنَ اللَّهِ ۚ فَاسْتَبْشِرُوا بِبَيْعِكُمُ الَّذِي بَايَعْتُمْ بِهِ ۚ وَذَٰلِكَ هُوَ الْفَوْزُ الْعَظِيمُ ۝ التَّائِبُونَ الْعَابِدُونَ الْحَامِدُونَ السَّائِحُونَ الرَّاكِعُونَ السَّاجِدُونَ الْآمِرُونَ بِالْمَعْرُوفِ وَالنَّاهُونَ عَنِ الْمُنْكَرِ وَالْحَافِظُونَ لِحُدُودِ اللَّهِ ۗ وَبَشِّرِ الْمُؤْمِنِينَ

Indeed God has bought from the believers their selves and their wealth, [in exchange] that for them [is] the Garden. They fight in the way of God, so they kill and are killed: a promise [binding] upon Him, true, in the Torah and the Gospel and the Qur'an. And who is more faithful to his covenant than God? So rejoice in your sale (bay') which you have contracted; and that, it is the great attainment. The repenters, the worshippers, the praisers, the travellers (as-sa'ihun), the bowers, the prostrators, the enjoiners of right and the forbidders of wrong, and the keepers of the limits of God. And give good news to the believers.

**3:195**

> فَاسْتَجَابَ لَهُمْ رَبُّهُمْ أَنِّي لَا أُضِيعُ عَمَلَ عَامِلٍ مِنْكُمْ مِنْ ذَكَرٍ أَوْ أُنْثَىٰ ۖ بَعْضُكُمْ مِنْ بَعْضٍ ۖ فَالَّذِينَ هَاجَرُوا وَأُخْرِجُوا مِنْ دِيَارِهِمْ وَأُوذُوا فِي سَبِيلِي وَقَاتَلُوا وَقُتِلُوا لَأُكَفِّرَنَّ عَنْهُمْ سَيِّئَاتِهِمْ وَلَأُدْخِلَنَّهُمْ جَنَّاتٍ تَجْرِي مِنْ تَحْتِهَا الْأَنْهَارُ ثَوَابًا مِنْ عِنْدِ اللَّهِ ۗ وَاللَّهُ عِنْدَهُ حُسْنُ الثَّوَابِ

So their Lord answered them: "Indeed I do not let go to waste the work of any worker among you, from male or female; you are of one another. So those who emigrated, and were driven out from their homes, and were harmed in My way, and fought and were killed, I will surely remove from them their evil deeds, and I will surely admit them to Gardens beneath which rivers flow, as a reward (thawab) from God; and God, with Him is the best reward."

**47:4 to 6**

> فَإِذَا لَقِيتُمُ الَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا فَضَرْبَ الرِّقَابِ حَتَّىٰ إِذَا أَثْخَنْتُمُوهُمْ فَشُدُّوا الْوَثَاقَ فَإِمَّا مَنًّا بَعْدُ وَإِمَّا فِدَاءً حَتَّىٰ تَضَعَ الْحَرْبُ أَوْزَارَهَا ۚ ذَٰلِكَ وَلَوْ يَشَاءُ اللَّهُ لَانْتَصَرَ مِنْهُمْ وَلَٰكِنْ لِيَبْلُوَ بَعْضَكُمْ بِبَعْضٍ ۗ وَالَّذِينَ قُتِلُوا فِي سَبِيلِ اللَّهِ فَلَنْ يُضِلَّ أَعْمَالَهُمْ ۝ سَيَهْدِيهِمْ وَيُصْلِحُ بَالَهُمْ ۝ وَيُدْخِلُهُمُ الْجَنَّةَ عَرَّفَهَا لَهُمْ

So when you meet those who disbelieved, [then] striking of the necks, until, when you have subdued them, then bind fast the bond; then either a favor afterward or a ransom, until the war lays down its burdens. That [is so]. And if God willed, He would have taken vengeance on them, but [it is] to test some of you by others. And those who were killed in the way of God, He will never let their deeds go astray. He will guide them and set right their state, and admit them to the Garden, which He has made known to them.

In 47:4 the Hafs text reads *qutilu*, "were killed." Another canonical reading has *qatalu*, "fought."

**3:169 to 171**

> وَلَا تَحْسَبَنَّ الَّذِينَ قُتِلُوا فِي سَبِيلِ اللَّهِ أَمْوَاتًا ۚ بَلْ أَحْيَاءٌ عِنْدَ رَبِّهِمْ يُرْزَقُونَ ۝ فَرِحِينَ بِمَا آتَاهُمُ اللَّهُ مِنْ فَضْلِهِ وَيَسْتَبْشِرُونَ بِالَّذِينَ لَمْ يَلْحَقُوا بِهِمْ مِنْ خَلْفِهِمْ أَلَّا خَوْفٌ عَلَيْهِمْ وَلَا هُمْ يَحْزَنُونَ ۝ يَسْتَبْشِرُونَ بِنِعْمَةٍ مِنَ اللَّهِ وَفَضْلٍ وَأَنَّ اللَّهَ لَا يُضِيعُ أَجْرَ الْمُؤْمِنِينَ

And do not think of those who were killed in the way of God as dead. Rather, [they are] alive with their Lord, being provided, rejoicing in what God has given them of His bounty, and they receive good news about those who have not yet joined them from behind them: that no fear [will be] upon them, nor will they grieve. They receive good news of favor from God and bounty, and that God does not let go to waste the reward of the believers.

See also 2:154, 4:74 (under exchange, below), 22:58 to 59 (those who emigrated "then were killed or died"), and 57:10 and 4:95 (under degrees, below).

**7. Striving with wealth and selves** (5 / 2). The verse calls it a trade:

**61:10 to 12**

> يَا أَيُّهَا الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا هَلْ أَدُلُّكُمْ عَلَىٰ تِجَارَةٍ تُنْجِيكُمْ مِنْ عَذَابٍ أَلِيمٍ ۝ تُؤْمِنُونَ بِاللَّهِ وَرَسُولِهِ وَتُجَاهِدُونَ فِي سَبِيلِ اللَّهِ بِأَمْوَالِكُمْ وَأَنْفُسِكُمْ ۚ ذَٰلِكُمْ خَيْرٌ لَكُمْ إِنْ كُنْتُمْ تَعْلَمُونَ ۝ يَغْفِرْ لَكُمْ ذُنُوبَكُمْ وَيُدْخِلْكُمْ جَنَّاتٍ تَجْرِي مِنْ تَحْتِهَا الْأَنْهَارُ وَمَسَاكِنَ طَيِّبَةً فِي جَنَّاتِ عَدْنٍ ۚ ذَٰلِكَ الْفَوْزُ الْعَظِيمُ

O you who believed, shall I direct you to a trade (tijara) that will save you from a painful punishment? You believe in God and His Messenger and strive in the way of God with your wealth and your selves; that is better for you, if you know. He will forgive you your sins and admit you to Gardens beneath which rivers flow, and good dwellings in Gardens of Eden. That is the great attainment.

3:142 makes striving and patience a test that must come first: "Or did you think that you would enter the Garden while God has not yet known those who strove among you, and known the patient?" See also 9:88 to 89, 4:95, 8:74, 9:120.

**8. Emigration** (4 / 3). It is joined to fighting in 3:195 and 22:58 to 59, and to striving in 9:20 and 8:74.

**4:100**

> وَمَنْ يُهَاجِرْ فِي سَبِيلِ اللَّهِ يَجِدْ فِي الْأَرْضِ مُرَاغَمًا كَثِيرًا وَسَعَةً ۚ وَمَنْ يَخْرُجْ مِنْ بَيْتِهِ مُهَاجِرًا إِلَى اللَّهِ وَرَسُولِهِ ثُمَّ يُدْرِكْهُ الْمَوْتُ فَقَدْ وَقَعَ أَجْرُهُ عَلَى اللَّهِ ۗ وَكَانَ اللَّهُ غَفُورًا رَحِيمًا

And whoever emigrates in the way of God will find in the earth many places of refuge and abundance. And whoever goes out from his house as an emigrant to God and His Messenger, then death overtakes him, his reward has fallen upon God; and God is Forgiving, Merciful.

See also 9:100 and 16:41.

**9. Patience** (10 / 4). "For what they were patient" (*bima sabaru*) closes 13:24, 25:75 and 76:12.

**39:10**

> قُلْ يَا عِبَادِ الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا اتَّقُوا رَبَّكُمْ ۚ لِلَّذِينَ أَحْسَنُوا فِي هَٰذِهِ الدُّنْيَا حَسَنَةٌ ۗ وَأَرْضُ اللَّهِ وَاسِعَةٌ ۗ إِنَّمَا يُوَفَّى الصَّابِرُونَ أَجْرَهُمْ بِغَيْرِ حِسَابٍ

Say: "O My servants who believed, fear your Lord. For those who did good in this world [is] good; and the earth of God is wide. Only the patient will be paid their reward in full without reckoning."

2:214 also states that a trial must come first: "Or did you think that you would enter the Garden while there has not yet come to you the like of [what came to] those who passed away before you?" See also 3:142, 29:58 to 59.

**10. Repentance, seeking forgiveness, turning back** (13 / 1).

**19:60**

> إِلَّا مَنْ تَابَ وَآمَنَ وَعَمِلَ صَالِحًا فَأُولَٰئِكَ يَدْخُلُونَ الْجَنَّةَ وَلَا يُظْلَمُونَ شَيْئًا

Except whoever repented and believed and did righteousness; then those will enter the Garden, and they will not be wronged in anything.

66:8: "repent to God [with] a sincere repentance. It may be that your Lord will remove from you your evil deeds and admit you to Gardens." 25:70: the one who called on another god, killed or fornicated is spared "except whoever repented and believed and did a righteous deed; for those, God will replace their evil deeds with good deeds." See also 3:135 to 136, 40:7 to 8, 50:32.

**11. Spending, charity, feeding the poor, the orphan and the captive, and freeing slaves** (12 / 12).

**90:11 to 18**

> فَلَا اقْتَحَمَ الْعَقَبَةَ ۝ وَمَا أَدْرَاكَ مَا الْعَقَبَةُ ۝ فَكُّ رَقَبَةٍ ۝ أَوْ إِطْعَامٌ فِي يَوْمٍ ذِي مَسْغَبَةٍ ۝ يَتِيمًا ذَا مَقْرَبَةٍ ۝ أَوْ مِسْكِينًا ذَا مَتْرَبَةٍ ۝ ثُمَّ كَانَ مِنَ الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا وَتَوَاصَوْا بِالصَّبْرِ وَتَوَاصَوْا بِالْمَرْحَمَةِ ۝ أُولَٰئِكَ أَصْحَابُ الْمَيْمَنَةِ

But he has not attempted the steep ascent (al-'aqaba). And what will make you know what the steep ascent is? The freeing of a neck, or feeding on a day of hunger an orphan of near kinship or a poor one in misery (lying in the dust), then being of those who believed and enjoined one another to patience and enjoined one another to compassion. Those are the companions of the right (ashab al-maymana).

**76:7 to 12**

> يُوفُونَ بِالنَّذْرِ وَيَخَافُونَ يَوْمًا كَانَ شَرُّهُ مُسْتَطِيرًا ۝ وَيُطْعِمُونَ الطَّعَامَ عَلَىٰ حُبِّهِ مِسْكِينًا وَيَتِيمًا وَأَسِيرًا ۝ إِنَّمَا نُطْعِمُكُمْ لِوَجْهِ اللَّهِ لَا نُرِيدُ مِنْكُمْ جَزَاءً وَلَا شُكُورًا ۝ إِنَّا نَخَافُ مِنْ رَبِّنَا يَوْمًا عَبُوسًا قَمْطَرِيرًا ۝ فَوَقَاهُمُ اللَّهُ شَرَّ ذَٰلِكَ الْيَوْمِ وَلَقَّاهُمْ نَضْرَةً وَسُرُورًا ۝ وَجَزَاهُمْ بِمَا صَبَرُوا جَنَّةً وَحَرِيرًا

They fulfill the vow, and fear a day whose evil is widespread, and they give food, despite love of it, to a poor one and an orphan and a captive: "We feed you only for the face of God; we do not want from you recompense or thanks. Indeed we fear from our Lord a day, frowning, distressful." So God protected them from the evil of that day, and gave them radiance and joy, and rewarded them, for what they were patient, a Garden and silk.

In 76:8, *'ala hubbihi* is "despite love of it," that is, of the food, or "for love of Him." See also 2:261 to 262 (the grain that grew seven ears), 2:274, 3:134, 51:19, 57:7, 57:10, 70:24 to 25, and the loan verses below.

**12. Prayer and the prescribed alms** (9 / 9). Prayer opens and closes two of the catalogues (23:2 and 23:9; 70:23 and 70:34). In 5:12 the covenant with Israel promises the Gardens for prayer, the zakah, belief in the messengers, supporting them, and "a goodly loan" to God. In 74:38 to 47, those in the Gardens ask those in Saqar what brought them there: "We were not of those who prayed, nor did we feed the poor." See also 2:277, 8:2 to 4, 87:14 to 15.

**13. Guarding chastity** (3 / 1). This appears only inside the catalogues: 23:5 to 7, 70:29 to 31 and 33:35, and, as "do not commit fornication", 25:68.

**14. Keeping trusts, covenants, vows and ties** (6 / 2).
- 23:8 and 70:32: "those who, of their trusts and their covenant, are keepers."
- 13:20 to 21: "those who fulfill the covenant of God and do not break the pledge, and those who join what God has commanded to be joined."
- 76:7: "they fulfill the vow."
- 48:10: "whoever fulfills what he has covenanted with God, He will give him a great reward."

**15. Submitting one's face to God while doing good** (2 / 2). This answers the claim that only Jews or Christians will enter:

**2:111 to 112**

> وَقَالُوا لَنْ يَدْخُلَ الْجَنَّةَ إِلَّا مَنْ كَانَ هُودًا أَوْ نَصَارَىٰ ۗ تِلْكَ أَمَانِيُّهُمْ ۗ قُلْ هَاتُوا بُرْهَانَكُمْ إِنْ كُنْتُمْ صَادِقِينَ ۝ بَلَىٰ مَنْ أَسْلَمَ وَجْهَهُ لِلَّهِ وَهُوَ مُحْسِنٌ فَلَهُ أَجْرُهُ عِنْدَ رَبِّهِ وَلَا خَوْفٌ عَلَيْهِمْ وَلَا هُمْ يَحْزَنُونَ

And they said, "None will enter the Garden except whoever is a Jew or Christians." Those are their wishes. Say: "Bring your proof, if you are truthful." Rather, whoever submits (aslama) his face to God while he is a doer of good (muhsin), for him [is] his reward with his Lord, and no fear upon them, nor will they grieve.

4:123 to 125 makes the same answer: "[It is] not by your wishes, nor the wishes of the People of the Book." See also 31:22, 43:69 to 70, 46:15 to 16.

**16. Doing good, *ihsan*** (11 / 5).
- 10:26: "For those who did good [is] the best (*al-husna*) and more (*ziyada*)."
- 5:83 to 85: those who weep at what was sent down and believe.
- See also 3:134, 16:30, 39:34, 51:16, 53:31.

**17. Avoiding the great sins** (2 / 0).

**4:31**

> إِنْ تَجْتَنِبُوا كَبَائِرَ مَا تُنْهَوْنَ عَنْهُ نُكَفِّرْ عَنْكُمْ سَيِّئَاتِكُمْ وَنُدْخِلْكُمْ مُدْخَلًا كَرِيمًا

If you avoid the great [sins] of what you are forbidden, We will remove from you your evil deeds and admit you to a noble entrance (mudkhal karim).

53:32 defines the doers of good as "those who avoid the great [sins] of wrongdoing and the indecencies, except the slight [faults] (*al-lamam*)."

**18. Purification, *tazakka*** (1 / 2).

**20:75 to 76**

> وَمَنْ يَأْتِهِ مُؤْمِنًا قَدْ عَمِلَ الصَّالِحَاتِ فَأُولَٰئِكَ لَهُمُ الدَّرَجَاتُ الْعُلَىٰ ۝ جَنَّاتُ عَدْنٍ تَجْرِي مِنْ تَحْتِهَا الْأَنْهَارُ خَالِدِينَ فِيهَا ۚ وَذَٰلِكَ جَزَاءُ مَنْ تَزَكَّىٰ

And whoever comes to Him a believer, having done the righteous [deeds], then those, for them [are] the highest degrees (ad-darajat al-'ula): Gardens of Eden beneath which rivers flow, abiding in it; and that is the recompense of whoever purified himself (tazakka).

87:14: "Indeed he has succeeded who purified himself." 92:17 to 21: "the most God-fearing ... who gives his wealth, purifying himself ... only seeking the face of his Lord." 24:21 states that no one would be pure without God's bounty (see 31 below).

**19. Steadfastness after saying "our Lord is God"** (2 / 0).

**41:30 to 32**

> إِنَّ الَّذِينَ قَالُوا رَبُّنَا اللَّهُ ثُمَّ اسْتَقَامُوا تَتَنَزَّلُ عَلَيْهِمُ الْمَلَائِكَةُ أَلَّا تَخَافُوا وَلَا تَحْزَنُوا وَأَبْشِرُوا بِالْجَنَّةِ الَّتِي كُنْتُمْ تُوعَدُونَ ۝ نَحْنُ أَوْلِيَاؤُكُمْ فِي الْحَيَاةِ الدُّنْيَا وَفِي الْآخِرَةِ ۖ وَلَكُمْ فِيهَا مَا تَشْتَهِي أَنْفُسُكُمْ وَلَكُمْ فِيهَا مَا تَدَّعُونَ ۝ نُزُلًا مِنْ غَفُورٍ رَحِيمٍ

Indeed those who said, "Our Lord is God," then were steadfast (istaqamu): the angels descend upon them: "Do not fear and do not grieve, and receive good news of the Garden which you were promised. We are your allies in the life of this world and in the hereafter; and for you in it [is] whatever your souls desire, and for you in it [is] whatever you call for, a lodging (nuzul) from One Forgiving, Merciful."

See also 46:13 to 14.

**20. Following the first emigrants and helpers** (1 / 0).

**9:100**

> وَالسَّابِقُونَ الْأَوَّلُونَ مِنَ الْمُهَاجِرِينَ وَالْأَنْصَارِ وَالَّذِينَ اتَّبَعُوهُمْ بِإِحْسَانٍ رَضِيَ اللَّهُ عَنْهُمْ وَرَضُوا عَنْهُ وَأَعَدَّ لَهُمْ جَنَّاتٍ تَجْرِي تَحْتَهَا الْأَنْهَارُ خَالِدِينَ فِيهَا أَبَدًا ۚ ذَٰلِكَ الْفَوْزُ الْعَظِيمُ

And the foremost, the first, of the emigrants (al-muhajirin) and the helpers (al-ansar), and those who followed them with excellence (bi-ihsan): God is pleased with them and they are pleased with Him, and He has prepared for them Gardens beneath which rivers flow, abiding in it forever. That is the great attainment.

**21. Refusing affection to those who oppose God and His Messenger, even one's own kin** (1 / 0).

**58:22**

> لَا تَجِدُ قَوْمًا يُؤْمِنُونَ بِاللَّهِ وَالْيَوْمِ الْآخِرِ يُوَادُّونَ مَنْ حَادَّ اللَّهَ وَرَسُولَهُ وَلَوْ كَانُوا آبَاءَهُمْ أَوْ أَبْنَاءَهُمْ أَوْ إِخْوَانَهُمْ أَوْ عَشِيرَتَهُمْ ۚ أُولَٰئِكَ كَتَبَ فِي قُلُوبِهِمُ الْإِيمَانَ وَأَيَّدَهُمْ بِرُوحٍ مِنْهُ ۖ وَيُدْخِلُهُمْ جَنَّاتٍ تَجْرِي مِنْ تَحْتِهَا الْأَنْهَارُ خَالِدِينَ فِيهَا ۚ رَضِيَ اللَّهُ عَنْهُمْ وَرَضُوا عَنْهُ ۚ أُولَٰئِكَ حِزْبُ اللَّهِ ۚ أَلَا إِنَّ حِزْبَ اللَّهِ هُمُ الْمُفْلِحُونَ

You will not find a people who believe in God and the Last Day loving (yuwaddun) whoever opposes God and His Messenger, even if they were their fathers or their sons or their brothers or their kin. Those, He has written in their hearts faith and supported them with a spirit from Him, and He will admit them to Gardens beneath which rivers flow, abiding in it. God is pleased with them and they are pleased with Him. Those are the party of God; indeed the party of God, they are the successful.

**22. Truthfulness** (4 / 3).

**5:119**

> قَالَ اللَّهُ هَٰذَا يَوْمُ يَنْفَعُ الصَّادِقِينَ صِدْقُهُمْ ۚ لَهُمْ جَنَّاتٌ تَجْرِي مِنْ تَحْتِهَا الْأَنْهَارُ خَالِدِينَ فِيهَا أَبَدًا ۚ رَضِيَ اللَّهُ عَنْهُمْ وَرَضُوا عَنْهُ ۚ ذَٰلِكَ الْفَوْزُ الْعَظِيمُ

God will say: "This is the day [when] their truthfulness benefits the truthful. For them [are] Gardens beneath which rivers flow, abiding in it forever. God is pleased with them and they are pleased with Him. That is the great attainment."

33:23 to 24: "Among the believers are men who were true to what they covenanted with God; so among them is one who has fulfilled his vow (*qada nahbahu*), and among them is one who waits." *Qada nahbahu* is commonly read as "met his death [in the fighting]." See also 39:33 to 35, 33:35, 70:33.

**23. Humility, and not seeking exaltation in the land** (4 / 3).
- 28:83: "That abode of the hereafter, We assign it to those who do not want exaltation (*'uluww*) in the land, nor corruption."
- 11:23: "those who believed and did righteous [deeds] and humbled themselves before their Lord."
- See also 23:2, 25:63.

**24. Enjoining right and forbidding wrong** (2 / 1). This appears in 9:71 to 72 and 9:112. 3:104 ties it to success.

**25. Kindness to parents, and gratitude** (1 / 0).
- 46:15 to 16: the son who "reached forty years" and prays to be grateful "for Your favor which You bestowed on me and on my parents ... Indeed I have repented to You, and indeed I am of the submitters."
- Then: "Those are the ones from whom We accept the best of what they did and pass over their evil deeds, among the companions of the Garden."

**26. A sound heart, a tranquil soul** (5 / 1). 26:88 to 89: "the day wealth will not benefit, nor sons, except whoever comes to God with a sound heart (*qalb salim*)." Also:

**89:27 to 30**

> يَا أَيَّتُهَا النَّفْسُ الْمُطْمَئِنَّةُ ۝ ارْجِعِي إِلَىٰ رَبِّكِ رَاضِيَةً مَرْضِيَّةً ۝ فَادْخُلِي فِي عِبَادِي ۝ وَادْخُلِي جَنَّتِي

"O tranquil soul (an-nafs al-mutma'inna), return to your Lord, pleased, pleasing; so enter among My servants, and enter My Garden."

**27. Righteous kin joined to the saved** (3 / 0).
- 52:21: "those who believed and whose offspring followed them in faith, We will join to them their offspring ... Every man, for what he earned, [is] held in pledge."
- 13:23 and 40:8: "whoever was righteous among their fathers and their spouses and their offspring."

**28. The People of the Book, and others who believe in God and the Last Day** (3 / 3). The text sets two statements side by side. These verses do not say how they relate.

**2:62**

> إِنَّ الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا وَالَّذِينَ هَادُوا وَالنَّصَارَىٰ وَالصَّابِئِينَ مَنْ آمَنَ بِاللَّهِ وَالْيَوْمِ الْآخِرِ وَعَمِلَ صَالِحًا فَلَهُمْ أَجْرُهُمْ عِنْدَ رَبِّهِمْ وَلَا خَوْفٌ عَلَيْهِمْ وَلَا هُمْ يَحْزَنُونَ

Indeed those who believed, and those who were Jews (hadu), and the Christians, and the Sabians: whoever believed in God and the Last Day and did righteousness, for them [is] their reward with their Lord, and no fear upon them, nor will they grieve.

5:69 repeats 2:62, with the Sabians placed before the Christians.

**3:85**

> وَمَنْ يَبْتَغِ غَيْرَ الْإِسْلَامِ دِينًا فَلَنْ يُقْبَلَ مِنْهُ وَهُوَ فِي الْآخِرَةِ مِنَ الْخَاسِرِينَ

And whoever seeks other than submission (al-islam) as a religion, it will never be accepted from him, and he in the hereafter [will be] among the losers.

5:65: "if the People of the Book had believed and feared [God], We would surely have removed from them their evil deeds and admitted them to Gardens of Bliss."

**29. The signs at the judgment: heavy scales, and the book given in the right hand** (4 / 4). These name the sign, not the deed behind it. The deed is given in 69:24, "for what you sent ahead."

**101:6 to 9**

> فَأَمَّا مَنْ ثَقُلَتْ مَوَازِينُهُ ۝ فَهُوَ فِي عِيشَةٍ رَاضِيَةٍ ۝ وَأَمَّا مَنْ خَفَّتْ مَوَازِينُهُ ۝ فَأُمُّهُ هَاوِيَةٌ

So as for whoever's scales are heavy, then he [will be] in a pleasing life. And as for whoever's scales are light, then his mother [will be] an abyss (hawiya).

**69:19 to 24**

> فَأَمَّا مَنْ أُوتِيَ كِتَابَهُ بِيَمِينِهِ فَيَقُولُ هَاؤُمُ اقْرَءُوا كِتَابِيَهْ ۝ إِنِّي ظَنَنْتُ أَنِّي مُلَاقٍ حِسَابِيَهْ ۝ فَهُوَ فِي عِيشَةٍ رَاضِيَةٍ ۝ فِي جَنَّةٍ عَالِيَةٍ ۝ قُطُوفُهَا دَانِيَةٌ ۝ كُلُوا وَاشْرَبُوا هَنِيئًا بِمَا أَسْلَفْتُمْ فِي الْأَيَّامِ الْخَالِيَةِ

So as for whoever is given his book in his right hand, he will say, "Here, read my book! Indeed I thought (zanantu) that I would meet my reckoning." So he [will be] in a pleasing life, in a lofty Garden, its clusters [of fruit] hanging near. "Eat and drink with good cheer for what you sent ahead (aslaftum) in the days gone by."

See also 7:8 to 9, 23:102 to 103, 84:7 to 9.

**30. Status names** (15 / 0):
- "the sincere servants of God" (37:40 to 43);
- "the righteous" (*al-abrar*: 76:5, 82:13, 83:18 to 28);
- "the foremost" and "those brought near" (56:10 to 12).

The deeds of the righteous are given in 76:7 to 10 (condition 11). 83:26, on the sealed wine of the righteous: "And for that, let the competitors compete" (*fal-yatanafas al-mutanafisun*).

**31. The stated ground: deeds, and God's bounty.**
- **Deeds** (10 / 4). "Enter the Garden for what you used to do" (16:32); "the Garden which you have been made to inherit for what you used to do" (43:72). See also 7:43, 32:17, 32:19, 46:14, 52:19, 69:24.
- **God's bounty, mercy or prior decree** (11 / 8). Further references: 35:34 to 35, 44:56 to 57 ("a bounty from your Lord"), 21:101.

One verse gives both grounds at once:

**7:43**

> وَنَزَعْنَا مَا فِي صُدُورِهِمْ مِنْ غِلٍّ تَجْرِي مِنْ تَحْتِهِمُ الْأَنْهَارُ ۖ وَقَالُوا الْحَمْدُ لِلَّهِ الَّذِي هَدَانَا لِهَٰذَا وَمَا كُنَّا لِنَهْتَدِيَ لَوْلَا أَنْ هَدَانَا اللَّهُ ۖ لَقَدْ جَاءَتْ رُسُلُ رَبِّنَا بِالْحَقِّ ۖ وَنُودُوا أَنْ تِلْكُمُ الْجَنَّةُ أُورِثْتُمُوهَا بِمَا كُنْتُمْ تَعْمَلُونَ

And We will remove whatever is in their breasts of rancor; beneath them rivers flow. And they will say, "Praise be to God, who guided us to this; and we would not have been guided had God not guided us. The messengers of our Lord certainly came with the truth." And they will be called: "That is the Garden; you have been made to inherit it for what you used to do."

**24:21**

> يَا أَيُّهَا الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا لَا تَتَّبِعُوا خُطُوَاتِ الشَّيْطَانِ ۚ وَمَنْ يَتَّبِعْ خُطُوَاتِ الشَّيْطَانِ فَإِنَّهُ يَأْمُرُ بِالْفَحْشَاءِ وَالْمُنْكَرِ ۚ وَلَوْلَا فَضْلُ اللَّهِ عَلَيْكُمْ وَرَحْمَتُهُ مَا زَكَىٰ مِنْكُمْ مِنْ أَحَدٍ أَبَدًا وَلَٰكِنَّ اللَّهَ يُزَكِّي مَنْ يَشَاءُ ۗ وَاللَّهُ سَمِيعٌ عَلِيمٌ

O you who believed, do not follow the footsteps of Satan; and whoever follows the footsteps of Satan, then indeed he commands indecency and wrong. And were it not for the bounty of God upon you and His mercy, not one of you would have been pure (zaka) ever; but God purifies whom He wills; and God is Hearing, Knowing.

**Not a condition: the claims the text rejects.** The text turns away claims to the Garden made by membership or by wishing:
- 2:94 and 62:6: "wish for death, if you are truthful";
- 5:18: "We are the sons of God and His beloved";
- 2:80 to 81 and 3:24: "the Fire will not touch us except for numbered days";
- 70:38: "Does every man of them hope to be admitted to a Garden of Bliss? No!"

#### The catalogue passages

The Qur'an gathers several conditions into lists. Each is promised the Garden, or "forgiveness and a great reward."

**23:1 to 11, al-Mu'minun**:

**23:1 to 11**

> قَدْ أَفْلَحَ الْمُؤْمِنُونَ ۝ الَّذِينَ هُمْ فِي صَلَاتِهِمْ خَاشِعُونَ ۝ وَالَّذِينَ هُمْ عَنِ اللَّغْوِ مُعْرِضُونَ ۝ وَالَّذِينَ هُمْ لِلزَّكَاةِ فَاعِلُونَ ۝ وَالَّذِينَ هُمْ لِفُرُوجِهِمْ حَافِظُونَ ۝ إِلَّا عَلَىٰ أَزْوَاجِهِمْ أَوْ مَا مَلَكَتْ أَيْمَانُهُمْ فَإِنَّهُمْ غَيْرُ مَلُومِينَ ۝ فَمَنِ ابْتَغَىٰ وَرَاءَ ذَٰلِكَ فَأُولَٰئِكَ هُمُ الْعَادُونَ ۝ وَالَّذِينَ هُمْ لِأَمَانَاتِهِمْ وَعَهْدِهِمْ رَاعُونَ ۝ وَالَّذِينَ هُمْ عَلَىٰ صَلَوَاتِهِمْ يُحَافِظُونَ ۝ أُولَٰئِكَ هُمُ الْوَارِثُونَ ۝ الَّذِينَ يَرِثُونَ الْفِرْدَوْسَ هُمْ فِيهَا خَالِدُونَ

Indeed the believers have succeeded, those who, in their prayer, are humble (khashi'un), and those who, from vain talk, are turning away, and those who, for the zakah, are doers, and those who, of their private parts (furuj), are guardians, except from their spouses or what their right hands possess, for indeed they are not blameworthy; so whoever seeks beyond that, then those, they are the transgressors; and those who, of their trusts and their covenant, are keepers (ra'un), and those who, over their prayers, keep guard: those, they are the inheritors, who will inherit al-Firdaws; they [will be] abiding in it.

The list:
1. humble in prayer;
2. turning from vain talk;
3. giving the zakah;
4. guarding the private parts;
5. keeping trusts and the covenant;
6. guarding the prayers.

The reward is to "inherit al-Firdaws."

**70:22 to 35, al-Ma'arij** (al-Ma'arij): except those who pray, those who, at their prayer, are constant, and those in whose wealth [is] a known right for the one who asks and the deprived, and those who hold true the Day of Judgment (yawm al-din), and those who, of the punishment of their Lord, are fearful, indeed the punishment of their Lord is not [a thing] to be felt secure from, and those who, of their private parts, are guardians, except from their spouses or what their right hands possess, for indeed they are not blameworthy; so whoever seeks beyond that, then those, they are the transgressors; and those who, of their trusts and their covenant, are keepers, and those who, in their testimonies, are upright (qa'imun, "standing"), and those who, over their prayer, keep guard: those [will be] in Gardens, honored.

**13:19 to 24, al-Ra'd**: Is then he who knows that what has been sent down to you from your Lord is the truth like one who is blind? Only those of understanding (uli l-albab) take heed: those who fulfill the covenant of God and do not break the pledge, and those who join what God has commanded to be joined, and fear (yakhshawn) their Lord, and are afraid of the evil of the reckoning, and those who were patient, seeking the face of their Lord, and established the prayer, and spent from what We provided them, secretly and openly, and repel the evil deed with the good deed: those, for them [is] the outcome of the abode (uqba d-dar): Gardens of Eden, which they will enter, and whoever was righteous among their fathers and their spouses and their offspring; and the angels will enter upon them from every gate, "Peace upon you, for what you were patient." So excellent is the outcome of the abode.

**25:63 to 76, al-Furqan, "the servants of the Merciful"**: And the servants of the Merciful (ar-Rahman) are those who walk on the earth gently, and when the ignorant address them, they say, "Peace"; and those who spend the night for their Lord, prostrating and standing; and those who say, "Our Lord, turn away from us the punishment of Hell; indeed its punishment is a lasting torment (gharam); indeed it is evil as a settlement and a residence"; and those who, when they spend, are not extravagant and are not stingy, and [their spending] is between that, moderate; and those who do not call with God upon another god, and do not kill the soul which God has forbidden except by right, and do not commit fornication (yaznun); and whoever does that will meet a penalty (athaman): the punishment will be doubled for him on the Day of Resurrection, and he will abide in it humiliated, except whoever repented and believed and did a righteous deed; for those, God will replace their evil deeds with good deeds, and God is ever Forgiving, Merciful; and whoever repented and did righteousness, then indeed he turns to God with a [true] turning; and those who do not witness falsehood (az-zur), and when they pass by vain talk, pass by with dignity; and those who, when they are reminded of the signs of their Lord, do not fall upon them deaf and blind; and those who say, "Our Lord, grant us from our spouses and our offspring comfort of eyes, and make us for the God-fearing a leader (imam)": those will be rewarded the high chamber (al-ghurfa) for what they were patient, and they will be met in it with greeting and peace, abiding in it; good [it is] as a settlement and a residence.

**33:35, al-Ahzab**: Indeed the submitting men (al-muslimin) and the submitting women, and the believing men and the believing women, and the devout men and the devout women, and the truthful men and the truthful women, and the patient men and the patient women, and the humble men and the humble women, and the men who give charity and the women who give charity, and the fasting men and the fasting women, and the men who guard their private parts and the women who guard [them], and the men who remember God much and the women who remember: God has prepared for them forgiveness and a great reward.

**51:15 to 19, al-Dhariyat**: Indeed the God-fearing [will be] in Gardens and springs, taking what their Lord has given them; indeed they were, before that, doers of good (muhsinin). Little of the night was it that they slept, and in the hours before dawn they would seek forgiveness, and in their wealth [was] a right for the one who asks and the deprived.

**3:133 to 136, Al 'Imran**: And hasten to forgiveness from your Lord and a Garden whose width is the heavens and the earth, prepared for the God-fearing, those who spend in ease and hardship, and those who restrain anger, and those who pardon people; and God loves the doers of good; and those who, when they did an indecency or wronged themselves, remembered God and sought forgiveness for their sins (and who forgives sins except God?), and did not persist in what they did while they know: those, their recompense [is] forgiveness from their Lord and Gardens beneath which rivers flow, abiding in it; and excellent is the reward of the workers.

### 2.4 What bars the Garden

Only three verses say in so many words that the Garden is closed to someone:

**5:72**

> لَقَدْ كَفَرَ الَّذِينَ قَالُوا إِنَّ اللَّهَ هُوَ الْمَسِيحُ ابْنُ مَرْيَمَ ۖ وَقَالَ الْمَسِيحُ يَا بَنِي إِسْرَائِيلَ اعْبُدُوا اللَّهَ رَبِّي وَرَبَّكُمْ ۖ إِنَّهُ مَنْ يُشْرِكْ بِاللَّهِ فَقَدْ حَرَّمَ اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ الْجَنَّةَ وَمَأْوَاهُ النَّارُ ۖ وَمَا لِلظَّالِمِينَ مِنْ أَنْصَارٍ

Those have certainly disbelieved who said, "Indeed God, He is the Messiah, son of Mary," while the Messiah said, "O Children of Israel, worship God, my Lord and your Lord." Indeed whoever associates [partners] with God (yushrik), God has forbidden him the Garden, and his refuge is the Fire; and for the wrongdoers there are no helpers.

**7:40**

> إِنَّ الَّذِينَ كَذَّبُوا بِآيَاتِنَا وَاسْتَكْبَرُوا عَنْهَا لَا تُفَتَّحُ لَهُمْ أَبْوَابُ السَّمَاءِ وَلَا يَدْخُلُونَ الْجَنَّةَ حَتَّىٰ يَلِجَ الْجَمَلُ فِي سَمِّ الْخِيَاطِ ۚ وَكَذَٰلِكَ نَجْزِي الْمُجْرِمِينَ

Indeed those who denied Our signs and were arrogant toward them, the gates of heaven will not be opened for them, and they will not enter the Garden until the camel passes through the eye of the needle. And thus We recompense the criminals.

The third is 7:50. The people of the Fire ask the people of the Garden for water "or of what God has provided you," and are answered: "Indeed God has forbidden both to the unbelievers."

Association of partners with God is the one sin stated as unforgiven:

**4:48**

> إِنَّ اللَّهَ لَا يَغْفِرُ أَنْ يُشْرَكَ بِهِ وَيَغْفِرُ مَا دُونَ ذَٰلِكَ لِمَنْ يَشَاءُ ۚ وَمَنْ يُشْرِكْ بِاللَّهِ فَقَدِ افْتَرَىٰ إِثْمًا عَظِيمًا

Indeed God does not forgive that [anything] be associated with Him, and He forgives what is below that for whom He wills. And whoever associates [partners] with God has fabricated a great sin.

4:116 repeats 4:48. Every other bar is stated as the Fire, or as abiding in it:

| Verse | The act | The wording |
|---|---|---|
| 2:81 | earning an evil deed, "his sin has encompassed him" | "companions of the Fire; they in it [are] abiding" |
| 2:217 | turning from one's religion and dying an unbeliever | deeds "worthless in this world and the hereafter"; companions of the Fire, abiding |
| 2:275 | returning to interest after admonition | companions of the Fire, abiding |
| 3:91 | dying an unbeliever | "the earth's fill of gold" not accepted as ransom |
| 4:10 | consuming the wealth of orphans wrongfully | "into their bellies fire" |
| 4:14 | disobeying God and His Messenger, overstepping His limits | "a Fire, abiding in it" |
| 4:93 | killing a believer intentionally | "Hell, abiding in it", without "forever" |
| 9:34 to 35 | hoarding gold and silver, not spending in the way of God | branded with it |
| 11:15 to 16 | wanting only this world and its adornment | "not, in the hereafter, [anything] except the Fire" |
| 25:68 to 69 | another god, unlawful killing, fornication | punishment doubled, "except whoever repented" (25:70) |
| 69:33 to 34 | not believing in God the Great; not urging the feeding of the poor | Hellfire and the chain |
| 72:23 | disobeying God and His Messenger | "the Fire of Hell, abiding in it forever" |
| 98:6 | disbelief among the People of the Book and the associators | "the Fire of Hell, abiding in it" |

### 2.5 The language of exchange

The Qur'an states the hereafter as a sale, a trade, a loan and a wage. This is the plainest place where it addresses the hearer's self-interest and redirects it.

**A sale.**
- 9:111 is given in full above: "God has bought from the believers their selves and their wealth ... So rejoice in your sale (*bay'*)."
- The preposition *bi-* in *bi-anna lahum al-janna* marks the price.
- Two further verses:

**2:207**

> وَمِنَ النَّاسِ مَنْ يَشْرِي نَفْسَهُ ابْتِغَاءَ مَرْضَاتِ اللَّهِ ۗ وَاللَّهُ رَءُوفٌ بِالْعِبَادِ

And of the people is one who sells (yashri) himself, seeking the pleasure of God; and God is Kind to the servants.

**4:74**

> فَلْيُقَاتِلْ فِي سَبِيلِ اللَّهِ الَّذِينَ يَشْرُونَ الْحَيَاةَ الدُّنْيَا بِالْآخِرَةِ ۚ وَمَنْ يُقَاتِلْ فِي سَبِيلِ اللَّهِ فَيُقْتَلْ أَوْ يَغْلِبْ فَسَوْفَ نُؤْتِيهِ أَجْرًا عَظِيمًا

So let those fight in the way of God who sell the life of this world for the hereafter. And whoever fights in the way of God and is killed or overcomes, We will give him a great reward.

The same verb runs the other way, for those "who bought the life of this world for the hereafter" (2:86), and "who bought error for guidance, so their trade did not profit" (2:16).

**A trade.**
- 61:10 to 12, given above: "shall I direct you to a trade (*tijara*) that will save you from a painful punishment?"
- 35:29 to 30, with the "wages" of 35:30 (*ujurahum*):

**35:29 to 30**

> إِنَّ الَّذِينَ يَتْلُونَ كِتَابَ اللَّهِ وَأَقَامُوا الصَّلَاةَ وَأَنْفَقُوا مِمَّا رَزَقْنَاهُمْ سِرًّا وَعَلَانِيَةً يَرْجُونَ تِجَارَةً لَنْ تَبُورَ ۝ لِيُوَفِّيَهُمْ أُجُورَهُمْ وَيَزِيدَهُمْ مِنْ فَضْلِهِ ۚ إِنَّهُ غَفُورٌ شَكُورٌ
