Indeed those who recite the Book of God and established the prayer and spent from what We provided them, secretly and openly, hope for a trade that will never perish (lan tabur), that He may pay them in full their rewards and increase them out of His bounty; indeed He is Forgiving, Appreciative.

**A loan.**

**2:245**

> مَنْ ذَا الَّذِي يُقْرِضُ اللَّهَ قَرْضًا حَسَنًا فَيُضَاعِفَهُ لَهُ أَضْعَافًا كَثِيرَةً ۚ وَاللَّهُ يَقْبِضُ وَيَبْسُطُ وَإِلَيْهِ تُرْجَعُونَ

Who is it that will lend God a goodly loan (qard hasan), so that He multiplies it for him many times over? And God withholds and extends, and to Him you will be returned.

See also 5:12, 57:11, 57:18, 64:17, 73:20.

**A wage.**
- The reward is "the wage of the workers" (*ajr al-'amilin*) in 3:136, 29:58 and 39:74.
- Wages are "paid in full" in 3:185, 4:173, 35:30 and 39:10.
- The emigrant's wage "has fallen due upon God" (4:100).
- "Every soul, for what it has earned, [is] held in pledge (*rahina*)" (74:38). *Rahina* is a security held for a debt.
- 53:39: "for man there is nothing except what he strove for."
- The Day of Judgment is "the Day of Mutual Loss" (64:9). *Taghabun* comes from *ghabn*, being cheated in a sale.

**64:9**

> يَوْمَ يَجْمَعُكُمْ لِيَوْمِ الْجَمْعِ ۖ ذَٰلِكَ يَوْمُ التَّغَابُنِ ۗ وَمَنْ يُؤْمِنْ بِاللَّهِ وَيَعْمَلْ صَالِحًا يُكَفِّرْ عَنْهُ سَيِّئَاتِهِ وَيُدْخِلْهُ جَنَّاتٍ تَجْرِي مِنْ تَحْتِهَا الْأَنْهَارُ خَالِدِينَ فِيهَا أَبَدًا ۚ ذَٰلِكَ الْفَوْزُ الْعَظِيمُ

The day He will gather you for the Day of Gathering, that is the Day of Mutual Loss (at-taghabun). And whoever believes in God and does righteousness, He will remove from him his evil deeds and admit him to Gardens beneath which rivers flow, abiding in it forever. That is the great attainment.

**37:58 to 61**

> أَفَمَا نَحْنُ بِمَيِّتِينَ ۝ إِلَّا مَوْتَتَنَا الْأُولَىٰ وَمَا نَحْنُ بِمُعَذَّبِينَ ۝ إِنَّ هَٰذَا لَهُوَ الْفَوْزُ الْعَظِيمُ ۝ لِمِثْلِ هَٰذَا فَلْيَعْمَلِ الْعَامِلُونَ

"Are we then not to die, except our first death, and we will not be punished? Indeed this, it is the great attainment." For the like of this let the workers work.

**This world weighed against the hereafter.**

**3:14 to 15**

> زُيِّنَ لِلنَّاسِ حُبُّ الشَّهَوَاتِ مِنَ النِّسَاءِ وَالْبَنِينَ وَالْقَنَاطِيرِ الْمُقَنْطَرَةِ مِنَ الذَّهَبِ وَالْفِضَّةِ وَالْخَيْلِ الْمُسَوَّمَةِ وَالْأَنْعَامِ وَالْحَرْثِ ۗ ذَٰلِكَ مَتَاعُ الْحَيَاةِ الدُّنْيَا ۖ وَاللَّهُ عِنْدَهُ حُسْنُ الْمَآبِ ۝ قُلْ أَؤُنَبِّئُكُمْ بِخَيْرٍ مِنْ ذَٰلِكُمْ ۚ لِلَّذِينَ اتَّقَوْا عِنْدَ رَبِّهِمْ جَنَّاتٌ تَجْرِي مِنْ تَحْتِهَا الْأَنْهَارُ خَالِدِينَ فِيهَا وَأَزْوَاجٌ مُطَهَّرَةٌ وَرِضْوَانٌ مِنَ اللَّهِ ۗ وَاللَّهُ بَصِيرٌ بِالْعِبَادِ

Made attractive to people is the love of desires: of women and sons and heaped-up hoards of gold and silver and branded horses and cattle and tillage. That is the enjoyment of the life of this world, and God, with Him is the good return (husn al-ma'ab). Say: "Shall I inform you of [what is] better than that? For those who feared [God], with their Lord [are] Gardens beneath which rivers flow, abiding in it, and purified spouses, and good pleasure (ridwan) from God; and God is Seeing of the servants,

**9:38**

> يَا أَيُّهَا الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا مَا لَكُمْ إِذَا قِيلَ لَكُمُ انْفِرُوا فِي سَبِيلِ اللَّهِ اثَّاقَلْتُمْ إِلَى الْأَرْضِ ۚ أَرَضِيتُمْ بِالْحَيَاةِ الدُّنْيَا مِنَ الْآخِرَةِ ۚ فَمَا مَتَاعُ الْحَيَاةِ الدُّنْيَا فِي الْآخِرَةِ إِلَّا قَلِيلٌ

O you who believed, what is [the matter] with you that, when it is said to you, "Go forth in the way of God," you cling heavily to the earth? Are you pleased with the life of this world rather than the hereafter? But the enjoyment of the life of this world, in the hereafter, is nothing but little.

**87:16 to 17**

> بَلْ تُؤْثِرُونَ الْحَيَاةَ الدُّنْيَا ۝ وَالْآخِرَةُ خَيْرٌ وَأَبْقَىٰ

But you prefer the life of this world, and the hereafter is better and more lasting.

**29:64**

> وَمَا هَٰذِهِ الْحَيَاةُ الدُّنْيَا إِلَّا لَهْوٌ وَلَعِبٌ ۚ وَإِنَّ الدَّارَ الْآخِرَةَ لَهِيَ الْحَيَوَانُ ۚ لَوْ كَانُوا يَعْلَمُونَ

And this life of this world is nothing but amusement and play; and indeed the abode of the hereafter, it is the [true] life (al-hayawan), if they knew.

See also 4:77, 16:96, 17:18 to 19, 18:46, 28:60, 42:20, 57:20, 93:4.

### 2.6 Degrees within the Garden

The text grades the reward by the deed.

**4:95 to 96**

> لَا يَسْتَوِي الْقَاعِدُونَ مِنَ الْمُؤْمِنِينَ غَيْرُ أُولِي الضَّرَرِ وَالْمُجَاهِدُونَ فِي سَبِيلِ اللَّهِ بِأَمْوَالِهِمْ وَأَنْفُسِهِمْ ۚ فَضَّلَ اللَّهُ الْمُجَاهِدِينَ بِأَمْوَالِهِمْ وَأَنْفُسِهِمْ عَلَى الْقَاعِدِينَ دَرَجَةً ۚ وَكُلًّا وَعَدَ اللَّهُ الْحُسْنَىٰ ۚ وَفَضَّلَ اللَّهُ الْمُجَاهِدِينَ عَلَى الْقَاعِدِينَ أَجْرًا عَظِيمًا ۝ دَرَجَاتٍ مِنْهُ وَمَغْفِرَةً وَرَحْمَةً ۚ وَكَانَ اللَّهُ غَفُورًا رَحِيمًا

Not equal are those of the believers who sit [at home], other than those with disability, and those who strive in the way of God with their wealth and their selves. God has preferred those who strive with their wealth and their selves over those who sit, by a degree; and to each God has promised the best (al-husna); and God has preferred those who strive over those who sit with a great reward, degrees from Him, and forgiveness, and mercy; and God is Forgiving, Merciful.

**9:20 to 22**

> الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا وَهَاجَرُوا وَجَاهَدُوا فِي سَبِيلِ اللَّهِ بِأَمْوَالِهِمْ وَأَنْفُسِهِمْ أَعْظَمُ دَرَجَةً عِنْدَ اللَّهِ ۚ وَأُولَٰئِكَ هُمُ الْفَائِزُونَ ۝ يُبَشِّرُهُمْ رَبُّهُمْ بِرَحْمَةٍ مِنْهُ وَرِضْوَانٍ وَجَنَّاتٍ لَهُمْ فِيهَا نَعِيمٌ مُقِيمٌ ۝ خَالِدِينَ فِيهَا أَبَدًا ۚ إِنَّ اللَّهَ عِنْدَهُ أَجْرٌ عَظِيمٌ

Those who believed and emigrated and strove in the way of God with their wealth and their selves are greater in degree with God; and those, they are the attainers (al-fa'izun). Their Lord gives them good news of mercy from Him and good pleasure (ridwan) and Gardens for them, in which [is] lasting bliss (na'im), abiding in it forever. Indeed God, with Him is a great reward.

57:10: "Not equal among you [is] whoever spent before the conquest (*al-fath*) and fought: those are greater in degree than those who spent afterward and fought. And to each God has promised the best." 17:21: "the hereafter is greater in degrees and greater in preference." 46:19 and 6:132: "for each [are] degrees from what they did."

56:7 to 14 divides men into three kinds at the judgment:

**56:7 to 14**

> وَكُنْتُمْ أَزْوَاجًا ثَلَاثَةً ۝ فَأَصْحَابُ الْمَيْمَنَةِ مَا أَصْحَابُ الْمَيْمَنَةِ ۝ وَأَصْحَابُ الْمَشْأَمَةِ مَا أَصْحَابُ الْمَشْأَمَةِ ۝ وَالسَّابِقُونَ السَّابِقُونَ ۝ أُولَٰئِكَ الْمُقَرَّبُونَ ۝ فِي جَنَّاتِ النَّعِيمِ ۝ ثُلَّةٌ مِنَ الْأَوَّلِينَ ۝ وَقَلِيلٌ مِنَ الْآخِرِينَ

and you will be three kinds: so the companions of the right (al-maymana): what are the companions of the right? And the companions of the left (al-mash'ama): what are the companions of the left? And the foremost, the foremost (as-sabiqun as-sabiqun): those are the ones brought near (al-muqarrabun), in Gardens of Bliss, a multitude of the former [peoples], and a few of the later [peoples].

Further gradings:
- **The three kinds, at death** (56:88 to 91). "Rest and fragrance and a Garden of Bliss" go to those brought near. "'Peace for you' from the companions of the right" goes to the companions of the right.
- **The four Gardens.** 55:46 gives two Gardens to "whoever feared the standing of his Lord." 55:62 adds "And besides (min dunihima) those two, two Gardens." *Min dunihima* can also mean "below those two."
- **The springs.** In 83:27 to 28, the righteous are given drink mixed from the spring of Tasnim, "a spring from which those brought near drink" directly.

### 2.7 The pattern in the Qur'an

**The hearer's own good.** The Qur'an addresses self-interest directly, in the words of the market. God "has bought" the believers' selves and wealth (9:111), and offers "a trade that will save you" (61:10) and "a goodly loan" He "multiplies" (2:245). Its reward is a wage (*ajr*) that the patient "will be paid ... in full without reckoning" (39:10), and the judgment is "the Day of Mutual Loss" (64:9).

**Grounds and conditions.**
- Entry is promised on stated conditions, chiefly belief joined to deeds. The text gives as its grounds both "what you used to do" and "the bounty of God" (7:43, 57:21).
- The conditions toward others are:
  - giving and feeding the poor, the orphan and the captive;
  - freeing slaves;
  - keeping trusts and covenants;
  - truthfulness and chastity;
  - restraining anger and pardoning (3:134).
- The conditions toward the community and its enemies are:
  - obedience to God and the Messenger;
  - emigration;
  - striving with wealth and selves;
  - fighting, killing and being killed in the way of God;
  - refusing affection to kin who oppose God and His Messenger.
- The text gives the highest degree to the second group (4:95, 9:20, 57:10). It states the second group's reward as an explicit purchase, and says the purchase is promised "in the Torah and the Gospel and the Qur'an" (9:111).

**What the self-interest is turned toward.** The Garden:
- rivers, fruit, shade, silk and gold;
- "purified spouses" and *hur 'in*;
- "whatever your souls desire" (41:31);
- God's good pleasure, "greater" than the Gardens (9:72).

### 2.8 The Hadith: sources and conventions

Islam rests on three sources:
- the Qur'an;
- the Hadith, the reports of what Muhammad said, did and approved;
- the Sira, the account of his life.

The Hadith add many specific paths to the Garden that the Qur'an does not name, and they are the part in which the branches of Islam differ most. Sections 2.8 to 2.12 sweep the Hadith, and 2.13 sweeps the Sira. Section 2.14 tests a claim the author has heard about fear, hope and the guarantee.

**What was swept:**
- the two collections Sunni Muslims hold sound: Sahih al-Bukhari and Sahih Muslim;
- the four Sunan of Abu Dawud, al-Tirmidhi, al-Nasa'i and Ibn Majah, and Malik's Muwatta, which together with the two Sahihs are the canonical books of Sunni Islam;
- the Twelver Shia collections, chiefly al-Kulayni's *al-Kafi* and Ibn Babawayh's *Thawab al-a'mal wa-'iqab al-a'mal*.

**The Sunni texts.**
- **Source.** They are the open hadith-api editions (github.com/fawazahmed0/hadith-api), fetched 2026-11-19, in vocalized Arabic. The published English in the same dataset was used only to check the translations.
- **Numbering.**
  - Bukhari is cited by the Fath al-Bari numbers, as on sunnah.com.
  - Muslim is cited by Fu'ad 'Abd al-Baqi's numbers.
  - The Sunan follow sunnah.com.
  - The Muwatta follows the edition's book and hadith reference.
- **Grades.**
  - For Bukhari and Muslim, Sunni scholarship holds the contents sound (*sahih*) as a body.
  - For the other books, each report carries the grades in the dataset: al-Albani, Zubair Ali Zai, Ahmad Shakir and Bashar 'Awad Ma'ruf. Where the Arabic contains the collector's own judgement, chiefly al-Tirmidhi's, that is given too.
  - Reports that a grader calls weak (*da'if*) or fabricated (*mawdu'*) are kept and marked, not dropped.

**The Shia texts.** Their sources and gradings are given in 2.12.

**The translations.** Every translation is made from the Arabic for this note, by the same rules as the Qur'an section:
- The matn is rendered literally, and supplied words are in [brackets].
- The narrating Companion or Imam is named, and the chain is not translated.
- The formulas of blessing after the names of the Prophet and the Companions are left out.

**The check.** Every Arabic excerpt below was checked by script, as an exact substring of its source text. The tool is `ext-ref/religion_ends/hadith/tools/expand_hadith.py`. Two cases needed care:
- Muslim 2565 is unnumbered in this edition, and was found under the edition's own entries 6544 to 6545.
- The edition lacks a few of Muslim's numbers (2635, 2971 to 2979), so those reports are not quoted.

**The working files** are in `ext-ref/religion_ends/`. Each lists every report kept, with its numbers and classification, and every report excluded, with the reason:
- `hadith_bm_conditions.md`;
- `hadith_bm_descriptions.md`;
- `hadith_sunan.md`;
- `hadith_shia.md`.

### 2.9 Sahih al-Bukhari and Sahih Muslim: conditions and bars

#### Method in brief

What was searched:
- in the whole text of both collections, the Garden words, the entry and bar formulas, and the Fire's mirror forms;
- further terms for martyrs, the seventy thousand and deprivations in the hereafter.

941 entries were read and 580 kept, in 179 distinct reports, 86 of them in both collections. Bukhari is cited by the Fath al-Bari number of sunnah.com, and Muslim by Fu'ad 'Abd al-Baqi's number, which in the edition is the field `arabicnumber`. The check report, "His deed will never admit any one of you to the Garden", is 2816 there. Every Arabic excerpt was checked by machine as an exact substring of the vocalized edition. The master table and the exclusions are in `ext-ref/religion_ends/hadith_bm_conditions.md`.

#### The conditions, in order of frequency

The counts are of distinct reports. A report can sit in more than one category, and each is placed by its own words.

**1. Jihad, martyrdom and the battles** (23). Going out, fighting and being killed "in the way of God" admit to the Garden.

**Bukhari 2790** (from Abu Hurayra; also bukhari:7423)

> مَنْ آمَنَ بِاللَّهِ وَبِرَسُولِهِ وَأَقَامَ الصَّلاَةَ وَصَامَ رَمَضَانَ، كَانَ حَقًّا عَلَى اللَّهِ أَنْ يُدْخِلَهُ الْجَنَّةَ جَاهَدَ فِي سَبِيلِ اللَّهِ، أَوْ جَلَسَ فِي أَرْضِهِ الَّتِي وُلِدَ فِيهَا ‏"‏‏.‏ فَقَالُوا يَا رَسُولَ اللَّهِ أَفَلاَ نُبَشِّرُ النَّاسَ‏.‏ قَالَ ‏"‏ إِنَّ فِي الْجَنَّةِ مِائَةَ دَرَجَةٍ أَعَدَّهَا اللَّهُ لِلْمُجَاهِدِينَ فِي سَبِيلِ اللَّهِ، مَا بَيْنَ الدَّرَجَتَيْنِ كَمَا بَيْنَ السَّمَاءِ وَالأَرْضِ، فَإِذَا سَأَلْتُمُ اللَّهَ فَاسْأَلُوهُ الْفِرْدَوْسَ، فَإِنَّهُ أَوْسَطُ الْجَنَّةِ وَأَعْلَى الْجَنَّةِ، أُرَاهُ فَوْقَهُ عَرْشُ الرَّحْمَنِ، وَمِنْهُ تَفَجَّرُ أَنْهَارُ الْجَنَّةِ

"Whoever believed in God and His Messenger, and established the prayer, and fasted Ramadan, it was a right upon God that He admit him to the Garden, [whether] he strove in the way of God or sat in his land in which he was born." They said: "O Messenger of God, shall we not give the people the glad tidings?" He said: "In the Garden are a hundred degrees which God prepared for those who strive in the way of God; what is between the two degrees is as what is between the heaven and the earth. So when you ask God, ask Him al-Firdaws, for it is the middle of the Garden and the highest of the Garden" (I think [he said]:) "above it is the Throne of the Merciful, and from it the rivers of the Garden gush forth."

**Bukhari 2818** (from 'Abd Allah b. Abi Awfa; also bukhari:2965, 2966, 3024, 3025; muslim:1742)

> وَاعْلَمُوا أَنَّ الْجَنَّةَ تَحْتَ ظِلاَلِ السُّيُوفِ

"And know that the Garden is beneath the shades of the swords."

Further references:
- **The guarantee**: Bukhari 36, 3123; Muslim 1876. It is quoted under the language of exchange.
- **The dust and the fast**: dusty feet (Bukhari 2811); a fast in the way of God (Bukhari 2840; Muslim 1153).
- **A hundred degrees**: Muslim 1884.
- **Badr and the tree**: Bukhari 3983; Muslim 2496.
- **Single fighters**:
  - Uhud (Bukhari 2805, 4046; Muslim 1789);
  - Badr (Bukhari 2809; Muslim 1901).
- **The martyrs**: the martyrs' spirits (Muslim 1887); the martyr's wish to return (Bukhari 2817).
- **Other battle reports**: Bukhari 2826, 3159, 3182; Muslim 1807, 1902, 2897; and the deeds nearest the Garden (Muslim 85).
- **The negative case**: "Whoever died and did not raid, and did not tell himself of it, died on a branch of hypocrisy" (Muslim 1910).

**2. Conduct toward others** (20). Particular acts toward daughters, orphans, the sick, animals, debtors, slaves and kin lead to the Garden or screen from the Fire.

**Muslim 2630** (from 'A'isha)

> جَاءَتْنِي مِسْكِينَةٌ تَحْمِلُ ابْنَتَيْنِ لَهَا فَأَطْعَمْتُهَا ثَلاَثَ تَمَرَاتٍ فَأَعْطَتْ كُلَّ وَاحِدَةٍ مِنْهُمَا تَمْرَةً وَرَفَعَتْ إِلَى فِيهَا تَمْرَةً لِتَأْكُلَهَا فَاسْتَطْعَمَتْهَا ابْنَتَاهَا فَشَقَّتِ التَّمْرَةَ الَّتِي كَانَتْ تُرِيدُ أَنْ تَأْكُلَهَا بَيْنَهُمَا فَأَعْجَبَنِي شَأْنُهَا فَذَكَرْتُ الَّذِي صَنَعَتْ لِرَسُولِ اللَّهِ صلى الله عليه وسلم فَقَالَ ‏ "‏ إِنَّ اللَّهَ قَدْ أَوْجَبَ لَهَا بِهَا الْجَنَّةَ أَوْ أَعْتَقَهَا بِهَا مِنَ النَّارِ

"A poor woman came to me carrying two daughters of hers, and I fed her three dates. She gave each one of them a date, and raised a date to her mouth to eat it; then her daughters asked her for it to eat, so she split the date which she wanted to eat between them. Her affair pleased me, and I mentioned what she did to the Messenger of God, and he said: 'God has made the Garden obligatory for her by it, or freed her by it from the Fire.'"

**Bukhari 173** (from Abu Hurayra; also bukhari:2363, 2466, 6009; muslim:2244)

> أَنَّ رَجُلاً رَأَى كَلْبًا يَأْكُلُ الثَّرَى مِنَ الْعَطَشِ، فَأَخَذَ الرَّجُلُ خُفَّهُ فَجَعَلَ يَغْرِفُ لَهُ بِهِ حَتَّى أَرْوَاهُ، فَشَكَرَ اللَّهُ لَهُ فَأَدْخَلَهُ الْجَنَّةَ

"A man saw a dog eating the moist earth from thirst; so the man took his shoe and began to scoop [water] for it with it until he quenched its thirst; so God thanked him and admitted him to the Garden."

**Bukhari 6094** (from Ibn Mas'ud; also muslim:2607)

> إِنَّ الصِّدْقَ يَهْدِي إِلَى الْبِرِّ، وَإِنَّ الْبِرَّ يَهْدِي إِلَى الْجَنَّةِ، وَإِنَّ الرَّجُلَ لَيَصْدُقُ حَتَّى يَكُونَ صِدِّيقًا، وَإِنَّ الْكَذِبَ يَهْدِي إِلَى الْفُجُورِ، وَإِنَّ الْفُجُورَ يَهْدِي إِلَى النَّارِ، وَإِنَّ الرَّجُلَ لَيَكْذِبُ، حَتَّى يُكْتَبَ عِنْدَ اللَّهِ كَذَّابًا

"Truthfulness guides to righteousness, and righteousness guides to the Garden; and a man is truthful until he becomes a siddiq. And lying guides to wickedness, and wickedness guides to the Fire; and a man lies until he is written with God a liar."

Further references:
- **Daughters**:
  - daughters "are for him a screen from the Fire" (Bukhari 1418; Muslim 2629);
  - two girls supported until they come of age (Muslim 2631), which names no Garden.
- **Orphans and the sick**:
  - the orphan's guardian (Bukhari 5304; Muslim 2983);
  - "Whoever visits a sick person does not cease to be in the khurfa of the Garden" (Muslim 2568).
- **The road**: removing a branch, "And he was admitted to the Garden" (Muslim 1914). Bukhari has "so God thanked him, and forgave him" (Bukhari 652).
- **Animals**: the dog report has "forgave him" in its other chains (Bukhari 2363; Muslim 2244); the prostitute "was forgiven by it" (Bukhari 3467; Muslim 2245).
- **Love, the greeting, the tongue**:
  - love and the greeting (Muslim 54);
  - guarding "what is between his two jaws and what is between his two legs" (Bukhari 6474).
- **Slaves, debtors, kin and parents**: freeing a slave (Bukhari 6715; Muslim 1509); leniency with debtors (Bukhari 3450; Muslim 1560); kin (Bukhari 1396); parents (Muslim 2551).
- **Several deeds together**: Bukhari 2631; Muslim 1028, 1844, 2865.

**3. The testimony; dying without association** (19). Saying, knowing or dying on "there is no god but God" admits to the Garden or forbids the Fire.

**Bukhari 5827** (from Abu Dharr; also bukhari:1237, 2388, 3222, 6268, 6443, 6444, 7487; muslim:94)

> مَا مِنْ عَبْدٍ قَالَ لاَ إِلَهَ إِلاَّ اللَّهُ‏.‏ ثُمَّ مَاتَ عَلَى ذَلِكَ، إِلاَّ دَخَلَ الْجَنَّةَ ‏"‏‏.‏ قُلْتُ وَإِنْ زَنَى وَإِنْ سَرَقَ قَالَ ‏"‏ وَإِنْ زَنَى وَإِنْ سَرَقَ ‏"‏‏.‏ قُلْتُ وَإِنْ زَنَى وَإِنْ سَرَقَ قَالَ ‏"‏ وَإِنْ زَنَى وَإِنْ سَرَقَ ‏"‏‏.‏ قُلْتُ وَإِنْ زَنَى وَإِنْ سَرَقَ قَالَ ‏"‏ وَإِنْ زَنَى وَإِنْ سَرَقَ عَلَى رَغْمِ أَنْفِ أَبِي ذَرٍّ

"There is no servant who said 'there is no god but God', then died upon that, but he entered the Garden." I said: "And if he committed adultery and if he stole?" He said: "And if he committed adultery and if he stole." I said: "And if he committed adultery and if he stole?" He said: "And if he committed adultery and if he stole." I said: "And if he committed adultery and if he stole?" He said: "And if he committed adultery and if he stole, despite the nose of Abu Dharr."

**Muslim 93** (from Jabir)

> مَا الْمُوجِبَتَانِ فَقَالَ ‏ "‏ مَنْ مَاتَ لاَ يُشْرِكُ بِاللَّهِ شَيْئًا دَخَلَ الْجَنَّةَ وَمَنْ مَاتَ يُشْرِكُ بِاللَّهِ شَيْئًا دَخَلَ النَّارَ

"What are the two that make necessary?" He said: "Whoever died not associating anything with God entered the Garden, and whoever died associating anything with God entered the Fire."

Further references:
- **Knowing it**: Muslim 26.
- **"seeking by that the face of God"**: Bukhari 425; Muslim 33.
- **"his heart certain of it"**: Muslim 31. There 'Umar answers: "for I fear that the people will rely on it; so leave them to work."
- **Other testimony reports**: Bukhari 128, 1238, 1338, 3435; Muslim 27, 28, 29, 1884.

**4. The pillars and the obligatory duties** (11). Worship without association, the prayer, the zakah, the fast and the pilgrimage are stated as enough.

**Bukhari 1397** (from Abu Hurayra; also muslim:14 (entry 14.02))

> دُلَّنِي عَلَى عَمَلٍ إِذَا عَمِلْتُهُ دَخَلْتُ الْجَنَّةَ‏.‏ قَالَ ‏"‏ تَعْبُدُ اللَّهَ لاَ تُشْرِكُ بِهِ شَيْئًا، وَتُقِيمُ الصَّلاَةَ الْمَكْتُوبَةَ، وَتُؤَدِّي الزَّكَاةَ الْمَفْرُوضَةَ، وَتَصُومُ رَمَضَانَ ‏"‏‏.‏ قَالَ وَالَّذِي نَفْسِي بِيَدِهِ لاَ أَزِيدُ عَلَى هَذَا‏.‏ فَلَمَّا وَلَّى قَالَ النَّبِيُّ صلى الله عليه وسلم ‏"‏ مَنْ سَرَّهُ أَنْ يَنْظُرَ إِلَى رَجُلٍ مِنْ أَهْلِ الْجَنَّةِ فَلْيَنْظُرْ إِلَى هَذَا

"Direct me to a deed which, when I do it, I enter the Garden." He said: "You worship God, not associating anything with Him, and you establish the prescribed prayer, and you pay the obligatory zakah, and you fast Ramadan." He said: "By Him in whose hand is my soul, I will not add to this." When he turned away, the Prophet said: "Whoever it pleases to look at a man of the people of the Garden, let him look at this [one]."

**Bukhari 1773** (from Abu Hurayra; also muslim:1349)

> الْعُمْرَةُ إِلَى الْعُمْرَةِ كَفَّارَةٌ لِمَا بَيْنَهُمَا، وَالْحَجُّ الْمَبْرُورُ لَيْسَ لَهُ جَزَاءٌ إِلاَّ الْجَنَّةُ

"The 'umra to the 'umra is an expiation for what is between them, and the hajj mabrur has no recompense except the Garden."

Further references:
- **The Bedouin's questions**: "He entered the Garden, if he is truthful" (Bukhari 1891; Muslim 11); also Bukhari 1396 and Muslim 12, 13, 15.
- **'Abd al-Qays**: Bukhari 53; Muslim 17.
- **The gates**: al-Rayyan for the fasters (Bukhari 1896); spending a pair (Bukhari 1897).

**5. Particular prayers, ablution, prostration, the mosque** (11).

**Bukhari 574** (from Abu Musa al-Ash'ari; also muslim:635)

> مَنْ صَلَّى الْبَرْدَيْنِ دَخَلَ الْجَنَّةَ

"Whoever prays the two cool [prayers] entered the Garden."

**Muslim 234** (from 'Uqba b. 'Amir; the second saying through 'Umar)

> مَا مِنْ مُسْلِمٍ يَتَوَضَّأُ فَيُحْسِنُ وُضُوءَهُ ثُمَّ يَقُومُ فَيُصَلِّي رَكْعَتَيْنِ مُقْبِلٌ عَلَيْهِمَا بِقَلْبِهِ وَوَجْهِهِ إِلاَّ وَجَبَتْ لَهُ الْجَنَّةُ ‏"‏ ‏.‏ قَالَ فَقُلْتُ مَا أَجْوَدَ هَذِهِ ‏.‏ فَإِذَا قَائِلٌ بَيْنَ يَدَىَّ يَقُولُ الَّتِي قَبْلَهَا أَجْوَدُ ‏.‏ فَنَظَرْتُ فَإِذَا عُمَرُ قَالَ إِنِّي قَدْ رَأَيْتُكَ جِئْتَ آنِفًا قَالَ ‏"‏ مَا مِنْكُمْ مِنْ أَحَدٍ يَتَوَضَّأُ فَيُبْلِغُ - أَوْ فَيُسْبِغُ - الْوُضُوءَ ثُمَّ يَقُولُ أَشْهَدُ أَنْ لاَ إِلَهَ إِلاَّ اللَّهُ وَأَنَّ مُحَمَّدًا عَبْدُ اللَّهِ وَرَسُولُهُ إِلاَّ فُتِحَتْ لَهُ أَبْوَابُ الْجَنَّةِ الثَّمَانِيَةُ يَدْخُلُ مِنْ أَيِّهَا شَاءَ

"There is no Muslim who ablutes and does his ablution well, then stands and prays two rak'as, turning to them with his heart and his face, but the Garden became obligatory for him." He said: So I said: "How fine is this!" And there was a speaker in front of me saying: "The one before it is finer." I looked, and it was 'Umar. He said: "I saw you came just now. He said: 'There is none of you who ablutes and completes (or: perfects) the ablution, then says: I testify that there is no god but God and that Muhammad is the servant of God and His Messenger, but the eight gates of the Garden are opened for him; he enters by whichever of them he wills.'"

Further references:
- **The dawn and afternoon prayers**: "Never will one enter the Fire who prayed before the rising of the sun and before its setting" (Muslim 634).
- **Other prayers**:
  - the twelve rak'as (Muslim 728);
  - building a mosque (Bukhari 450; Muslim 533) and going to it (Bukhari 662);
  - Bilal's prayer (Bukhari 1149).
- **Prostration**: Muslim 81, 488, 489.

**6. Words of remembrance and the call to prayer** (8).

**Bukhari 6306** (from Shaddad b. Aws; also bukhari:6323)

> سَيِّدُ الاِسْتِغْفَارِ أَنْ تَقُولَ اللَّهُمَّ أَنْتَ رَبِّي، لاَ إِلَهَ إِلاَّ أَنْتَ، خَلَقْتَنِي وَأَنَا عَبْدُكَ، وَأَنَا عَلَى عَهْدِكَ وَوَعْدِكَ مَا اسْتَطَعْتُ، أَعُوذُ بِكَ مِنْ شَرِّ مَا صَنَعْتُ، أَبُوءُ لَكَ بِنِعْمَتِكَ عَلَىَّ وَأَبُوءُ لَكَ بِذَنْبِي، فَاغْفِرْ لِي، فَإِنَّهُ لاَ يَغْفِرُ الذُّنُوبَ إِلاَّ أَنْتَ ‏"‏‏.‏ قَالَ ‏"‏ وَمَنْ قَالَهَا مِنَ النَّهَارِ مُوقِنًا بِهَا، فَمَاتَ مِنْ يَوْمِهِ قَبْلَ أَنْ يُمْسِيَ، فَهُوَ مِنْ أَهْلِ الْجَنَّةِ، وَمَنْ قَالَهَا مِنَ اللَّيْلِ وَهْوَ مُوقِنٌ بِهَا، فَمَاتَ قَبْلَ أَنْ يُصْبِحَ، فَهْوَ مِنْ أَهْلِ الْجَنَّةِ

"The master of the prayers for forgiveness is that you say: 'O God, You are my Lord, there is no god but You; You created me and I am Your servant, and I am upon Your covenant and Your promise as far as I am able; I seek refuge in You from the evil of what I did; I acknowledge to You Your favour upon me, and I acknowledge to You my sin; so forgive me, for none forgives the sins but You.'" He said: "And whoever says it in the day, certain of it, and dies on his day before he reaches evening, he is of the people of the Garden; and whoever says it in the night while he is certain of it, and dies before he reaches morning, he is of the people of the Garden."

Further references:
- **The ninety-nine names**: "whoever enumerates them entered the Garden" (Bukhari 2736; Muslim 2677).
- **"There is no might and no power except by God"**: Muslim 2704.
- **The call to prayer**: answering it (Muslim 385); and Bukhari 614.
- **Other words**: the gatherings of remembrance (Bukhari 6408); love of al-Ikhlas (Bukhari 774.2, a ta'liq).

**7. Patience at loss and affliction** (8).

**Bukhari 5653** (from Anas)

> إِنَّ اللَّهَ قَالَ إِذَا ابْتَلَيْتُ عَبْدِي بِحَبِيبَتَيْهِ فَصَبَرَ عَوَّضْتُهُ مِنْهُمَا الْجَنَّةَ

"God said: 'When I try My servant in his two beloveds and he is patient, I compensate him for them with the Garden.'"

Further references:
- **Loss of children**: three reports with three wordings.
  - "God admits him to the Garden by the bounty of His mercy for them" (Bukhari 1248);
  - "a screen from the Fire" (Bukhari 101);
  - "she entered the Garden" (Muslim 2632).
- **The chosen one**: "My believing servant has no recompense with Me" (Bukhari 6424).
- **Other affliction**: the epileptic woman (Bukhari 5652); the plague (Bukhari 3474); Medina (Muslim 1363).

**8. The kinds of martyr** (7). These reports name who counts as a martyr. They do not state the reward.

**Bukhari 2829** (from Abu Hurayra; also bukhari:652, 653, 654, 720, 721, 5733; muslim:1914)

> الشُّهَدَاءُ خَمْسَةٌ الْمَطْعُونُ، وَالْمَبْطُونُ، وَالْغَرِقُ وَصَاحِبُ الْهَدْمِ، وَالشَّهِيدُ فِي سَبِيلِ اللَّهِ

"The martyrs are five: the one struck by plague, and the one [who dies] of the belly, and the drowned, and the one under the collapse, and the martyr in the way of God."

Further references:
- **Muslim's list**: Muslim 1915 adds "whoever dies in the way of God".
- **Defending property**: "Whoever is killed short of his property, he is a martyr" (Bukhari 2480).
- **Asking for martyrdom**: Muslim 1908, 1909.
- **The exception of debt**: "For the martyr every sin is forgiven, except the debt" (Muslim 1886).

**9. Deeds that earn the Prophet's intercession** (6).

**Muslim 199** (from Abu Hurayra; also bukhari:6304; muslim:198)

> لِكُلِّ نَبِيٍّ دَعْوَةٌ مُسْتَجَابَةٌ فَتَعَجَّلَ كُلُّ نَبِيٍّ دَعْوَتَهُ وَإِنِّي اخْتَبَأْتُ دَعْوَتِي شَفَاعَةً لأُمَّتِي يَوْمَ الْقِيَامَةِ فَهِيَ نَائِلَةٌ إِنْ شَاءَ اللَّهُ مَنْ مَاتَ مِنْ أُمَّتِي لاَ يُشْرِكُ بِاللَّهِ شَيْئًا

"For every prophet is a prayer that is answered; every prophet hastened his prayer, and I have hidden my prayer as intercession for my community on the Day of Resurrection; and it will reach, if God wills, whoever of my community died not associating anything with God."

Further references:
- **Sincerity**: "pure from his heart" (Bukhari 99).
- **The call to prayer**: Bukhari 614; Muslim 384.
- **Medina**: Muslim 1363.
- **Abu Talib**: Bukhari 3885, among the bars.

**10. Who the people of the Garden are** (6). These name kinds of people rather than deeds.

**Bukhari 4918** (from Haritha b. Wahb al-Khuza'i; also bukhari:6071, 6657; muslim:2853)

> أَلاَ أُخْبِرُكُمْ بِأَهْلِ الْجَنَّةِ كُلُّ ضَعِيفٍ مُتَضَعِّفٍ لَوْ أَقْسَمَ عَلَى اللَّهِ لأَبَرَّهُ، أَلاَ أُخْبِرُكُمْ بِأَهْلِ النَّارِ كُلُّ عُتُلٍّ جَوَّاظٍ مُسْتَكْبِرٍ

"Shall I not tell you of the people of the Garden? Every weak one, deemed weak, who if he swore by God, He would fulfil it. Shall I not tell you of the people of the Fire? Every coarse, greedy, arrogant one."

Further references:
- **The poor**: "most of its people the poor" (Bukhari 3241; Muslim 2737), and Bukhari 5196.
- **The weak and the lowly**: "none enters me but the weak of the people and their lowly" (Bukhari 4850).
- **Birds' hearts**: Muslim 2840.
- **Women**: Muslim 2738.

**11. The seventy thousand** (5).

**Bukhari 6541** (from Ibn 'Abbas; also bukhari:5705, 5752, 6472; muslim:218 ('Imran b. Husayn), 220)

> هَؤُلاَءِ أُمَّتُكَ، وَهَؤُلاَءِ سَبْعُونَ أَلْفًا قُدَّامَهُمْ، لاَ حِسَابَ عَلَيْهِمْ وَلاَ عَذَابَ‏.‏ قُلْتُ وَلِمَ قَالَ كَانُوا لاَ يَكْتَوُونَ، وَلاَ يَسْتَرْقُونَ، وَلاَ يَتَطَيَّرُونَ، وَعَلَى رَبِّهِمْ يَتَوَكَّلُونَ

[Gabriel said:] "These are your community, and these are seventy thousand in front of them; no reckoning on them and no punishment." I said: "And why?" He said: "They used not to cauterize, and not to seek incantation, and not to take omens, and on their Lord they rely."

Further references:
- **Other chains with the qualities**: Bukhari 5705, 6472; Muslim 218, 220. The chains differ in which qualities they list.
- **Chains with no deed**: Abu Hurayra's and Sahl's (Bukhari 5811, 6543; Muslim 216, 219).

**12. Smaller categories.**
- **Spending in the way of God** (2): Bukhari 1897, 2778.
- **Obedience to the Messenger** (2):

**Bukhari 7280** (from Abu Hurayra)

> كُلُّ أُمَّتِي يَدْخُلُونَ الْجَنَّةَ، إِلاَّ مَنْ أَبَى ‏"‏‏.‏ قَالُوا يَا رَسُولَ اللَّهِ وَمَنْ يَأْبَى قَالَ ‏"‏ مَنْ أَطَاعَنِي دَخَلَ الْجَنَّةَ، وَمَنْ عَصَانِي فَقَدْ أَبَى

"All my community enter the Garden, except whoever refuses." They said: "O Messenger of God, and who refuses?" He said: "Whoever obeys me entered the Garden, and whoever disobeys me has refused."

  - Bukhari 7281: "So the house is the Garden, and the inviter is Muhammad".
- **The witness of the believers** (2): "so the Garden became obligatory for him" (Bukhari 1367; also Bukhari 1368).
- **Entering Islam, and the killer who repents** (2): Bukhari 3010, 2826.
- **Statements of the way** (2): "for the sake of that God promised the Garden" (Bukhari 7416); and:

**Bukhari 6487** (from Abu Hurayra; also muslim:2822 (Anas))

> حُجِبَتِ النَّارُ بِالشَّهَوَاتِ، وَحُجِبَتِ الْجَنَّةُ بِالْمَكَارِهِ

"The Fire was veiled with the desires, and the Garden was veiled with the things disliked."

- **Knowledge** (1): "And whoever treads a path seeking in it knowledge, God eases for him by it a path to the Garden." (Muslim 2699).
- **The pledge to avoid the great sins** (1): Bukhari 3893, under the language of exchange.

#### What bars the Garden, or makes the Fire certain

Thirty-three reports have a bar as their primary category. They fall into four forms of words.

**Will not enter the Garden**

**Muslim 91** (from Ibn Mas'ud)

> لاَ يَدْخُلُ الْجَنَّةَ مَنْ كَانَ فِي قَلْبِهِ مِثْقَالُ ذَرَّةٍ مِنْ كِبْرٍ ‏"‏ ‏.‏ قَالَ رَجُلٌ إِنَّ الرَّجُلَ يُحِبُّ أَنْ يَكُونَ ثَوْبُهُ حَسَنًا وَنَعْلُهُ حَسَنَةً ‏.‏ قَالَ ‏"‏ إِنَّ اللَّهَ جَمِيلٌ يُحِبُّ الْجَمَالَ الْكِبْرُ بَطَرُ الْحَقِّ وَغَمْطُ النَّاسِ

"He will not enter the Garden in whose heart is an atom's weight of pride." A man said: "A man loves that his garment be fine and his sandal fine." He said: "God is beautiful and loves beauty. Pride is disdaining the truth and despising the people."

Further references:
- **The talebearer and the severer**:
  - "A talebearer (qattat) will not enter the Garden" (Bukhari 6056; Muslim 105);
  - "A severer will not enter the Garden" (Bukhari 5984; Muslim 2556).
- **The neighbour**: "He will not enter the Garden whose neighbour is not safe from his calamities" (Muslim 46).
- **The commander and the hypocrites**: the commander who does not counsel (Muslim 142); the twelve hypocrites (Muslim 2779).
- **Only believers enter**: four occasions, among them "None enters the Garden but a Muslim soul" (Bukhari 3062; Muslim 111, 114, 221, 1142).

**Will not smell its scent**

**Bukhari 3166** (from 'Abd Allah b. 'Amr; also bukhari:6914)

> مَنْ قَتَلَ مُعَاهَدًا لَمْ يَرَحْ رَائِحَةَ الْجَنَّةِ، وَإِنَّ رِيحَهَا تُوجَدُ مِنْ مَسِيرَةِ أَرْبَعِينَ عَامًا

"Whoever killed one under covenant will not smell the scent of the Garden; and its scent is found from a distance of forty years."

Further references:
- **The ruler**: who "did not surround it with good counsel" (Bukhari 7150).
- **The whip-men and the women**: of the whip-men and the women "clothed, naked", "they will not enter the Garden, and will not find its scent" (Muslim 2128).

**Forbidden**

**Bukhari 1364** (from Jundub; also bukhari:1363, 3463; muslim:113)

> كَانَ بِرَجُلٍ جِرَاحٌ فَقَتَلَ نَفْسَهُ فَقَالَ اللَّهُ بَدَرَنِي عَبْدِي بِنَفْسِهِ حَرَّمْتُ عَلَيْهِ الْجَنَّةَ

"There was in a man a wound, and he killed himself; so God said: 'My servant hastened ahead of Me with himself; I have forbidden him the Garden.'"

Further references:
- **The deceiving ruler**: "dies while he is deceiving them, but God has forbidden him the Garden" (Bukhari 7151).
- **False descent**: "the Garden is forbidden to him" (Bukhari 6766; Muslim 63).
- **The false oath**: "forbidden him the Garden", "Even if a twig of arak" (Muslim 137).
- **The unbelievers**: "I have forbidden the Garden to the unbelievers" (Bukhari 4769).

**The Fire made certain**

**Bukhari 3318** (from Ibn 'Umar; also bukhari:745, 2364 (Asma'), 2365, 3482; muslim:904 (Jabir), 2242, 2243, 2619 (Abu Hurayra))

> دَخَلَتِ امْرَأَةٌ النَّارَ فِي هِرَّةٍ رَبَطَتْهَا، فَلَمْ تُطْعِمْهَا، وَلَمْ تَدَعْهَا تَأْكُلُ مِنْ خِشَاشِ الأَرْضِ

"A woman entered the Fire for a cat she tied up, and did not feed it, and did not leave it to eat of the creeping things of the earth."

Further references:
- **Association**: "Whoever died associating anything with God entered the Fire" (Bukhari 1238; Muslim 93).
- **False descent and lying**: claiming a people not one's own (Bukhari 3508); lying about the Prophet (Bukhari 110 and seven other chains).
- **The stolen cloak**: "is blazing upon him as fire" (Bukhari 6707).
- **Women in the Fire**: "most of its people were women; they are ungrateful" (Bukhari 29).

Two further forms do not name the Garden:
- **Deprivation in the hereafter**: "is deprived of it in the hereafter" (Bukhari 5575, wine); "will not wear it in the hereafter" (Bukhari 5832, silk).
- **Withheld zakah**: the punishment is followed by "either to the Garden or to the Fire" (Muslim 987).

#### Grace and deeds

**No one is admitted by his deed.** Three Companions give the saying: Abu Hurayra, 'A'isha (Bukhari 6467; Muslim 2818) and Jabir. Bukhari 6463 has "will never save" where others have "will never admit".

**Bukhari 6463** (from Abu Hurayra; also bukhari:5673; muslim:2816)

> لَنْ يُنَجِّيَ أَحَدًا مِنْكُمْ عَمَلُهُ ‏"‏‏.‏ قَالُوا وَلاَ أَنْتَ يَا رَسُولَ اللَّهِ قَالَ ‏"‏ وَلاَ أَنَا، إِلاَّ أَنْ يَتَغَمَّدَنِي اللَّهُ بِرَحْمَةٍ، سَدِّدُوا وَقَارِبُوا، وَاغْدُوا وَرُوحُوا، وَشَىْءٌ مِنَ الدُّلْجَةِ‏.‏ وَالْقَصْدَ الْقَصْدَ تَبْلُغُوا

"His deed will never save any one of you." They said: "Not even you, O Messenger of God?" He said: "Not even I, unless God covers me with mercy. Aim right, and come near; and go in the morning, and go in the evening, and something of the night; and the middle course, the middle course: you will arrive."

**Bukhari 5673** (from Abu Hurayra; also bukhari:6463; muslim:2816)

> لَنْ يُدْخِلَ أَحَدًا عَمَلُهُ الْجَنَّةَ ‏"‏‏.‏ قَالُوا وَلاَ أَنْتَ يَا رَسُولَ اللَّهِ قَالَ ‏"‏ لاَ، وَلاَ أَنَا إِلاَّ أَنْ يَتَغَمَّدَنِي اللَّهُ بِفَضْلٍ وَرَحْمَةٍ فَسَدِّدُوا وَقَارِبُوا

"His deed will never admit anyone to the Garden." They said: "Not even you, O Messenger of God?" He said: "No, not even I, unless God covers me with bounty and mercy. So aim right and come near."

**Muslim 2817** (from Jabir)

> لاَ يُدْخِلُ أَحَدًا مِنْكُمْ عَمَلُهُ الْجَنَّةَ وَلاَ يُجِيرُهُ مِنَ النَّارِ وَلاَ أَنَا إِلاَّ بِرَحْمَةٍ مِنَ اللَّهِ

"His deed does not admit any one of you to the Garden, and does not protect him from the Fire, and not I, except by mercy from God."

**Mercy in a hundred parts.** "God created mercy, on the day He created it, a hundred mercies; He kept with Himself ninety-nine mercies, and sent among His creation, all of them, one mercy. If the unbeliever knew all that is with God of mercy, he would not despair of the Garden; and if the believer knew all that is with God of punishment, he would not feel safe from the Fire." (Bukhari 6469; also Bukhari 6000; Muslim 2752, 2755).

**Exit from the Fire.** The measure for leaving the Fire narrows:
- a mustard seed of faith;
- then a grain of good with the testimony;
- then a handful taken out "without a deed they did" (Bukhari 7439).

**Bukhari 22** (from Abu Sa'id al-Khudri; also bukhari:6560; muslim:184)

> يَدْخُلُ أَهْلُ الْجَنَّةِ الْجَنَّةَ، وَأَهْلُ النَّارِ النَّارَ، ثُمَّ يَقُولُ اللَّهُ تَعَالَى أَخْرِجُوا مَنْ كَانَ فِي قَلْبِهِ مِثْقَالُ حَبَّةٍ مِنْ خَرْدَلٍ مِنْ إِيمَانٍ‏.‏ فَيُخْرَجُونَ مِنْهَا قَدِ اسْوَدُّوا فَيُلْقَوْنَ فِي نَهَرِ الْحَيَا ـ أَوِ الْحَيَاةِ، شَكَّ مَالِكٌ ـ فَيَنْبُتُونَ كَمَا تَنْبُتُ الْحِبَّةُ فِي جَانِبِ السَّيْلِ، أَلَمْ تَرَ أَنَّهَا تَخْرُجُ صَفْرَاءَ مُلْتَوِيَةً

"The people of the Garden enter the Garden, and the people of the Fire the Fire; then God the Exalted says: 'Bring out whoever has in his heart a mustard seed's weight of faith.' So they are brought out of it, having turned black, and are cast into the river of rain (or: of life; Malik doubted), and they sprout as the seed sprouts beside the torrent. Have you not seen that it comes out yellow, twisted?"

**Bukhari 44** (from Anas; also bukhari:7410; muslim:193)

> يَخْرُجُ مِنَ النَّارِ مَنْ قَالَ لاَ إِلَهَ إِلاَّ اللَّهُ، وَفِي قَلْبِهِ وَزْنُ شَعِيرَةٍ مِنْ خَيْرٍ، وَيَخْرُجُ مِنَ النَّارِ مَنْ قَالَ لاَ إِلَهَ إِلاَّ اللَّهُ، وَفِي قَلْبِهِ وَزْنُ بُرَّةٍ مِنْ خَيْرٍ، وَيَخْرُجُ مِنَ النَّارِ مَنْ قَالَ لاَ إِلَهَ إِلاَّ اللَّهُ، وَفِي قَلْبِهِ وَزْنُ ذَرَّةٍ مِنْ خَيْرٍ

"There comes out of the Fire whoever said 'there is no god but God' and in his heart is a barley grain's weight of good; and there comes out of the Fire whoever said 'there is no god but God' and in his heart is a wheat grain's weight of good; and there comes out of the Fire whoever said 'there is no god but God' and in his heart is an atom's weight of good."

**Bukhari 7439** (from Abu Sa'id al-Khudri; also muslim:183)

> فَيَقُولُ الْجَبَّارُ بَقِيَتْ شَفَاعَتِي‏.‏ فَيَقْبِضُ قَبْضَةً مِنَ النَّارِ فَيُخْرِجُ أَقْوَامًا قَدِ امْتُحِشُوا، فَيُلْقَوْنَ فِي نَهَرٍ بِأَفْوَاهِ الْجَنَّةِ يُقَالُ لَهُ مَاءُ الْحَيَاةِ، فَيَنْبُتُونَ فِي حَافَتَيْهِ كَمَا تَنْبُتُ الْحِبَّةُ فِي حَمِيلِ السَّيْلِ، قَدْ رَأَيْتُمُوهَا إِلَى جَانِبِ الصَّخْرَةِ إِلَى جَانِبِ الشَّجَرَةِ، فَمَا كَانَ إِلَى الشَّمْسِ مِنْهَا كَانَ أَخْضَرَ، وَمَا كَانَ مِنْهَا إِلَى الظِّلِّ كَانَ أَبْيَضَ، فَيَخْرُجُونَ كَأَنَّهُمُ اللُّؤْلُؤُ، فَيُجْعَلُ فِي رِقَابِهِمُ الْخَوَاتِيمُ فَيَدْخُلُونَ الْجَنَّةَ فَيَقُولُ أَهْلُ الْجَنَّةِ هَؤُلاَءِ عُتَقَاءُ الرَّحْمَنِ أَدْخَلَهُمُ الْجَنَّةَ بِغَيْرِ عَمَلٍ عَمِلُوهُ وَلاَ خَيْرٍ قَدَّمُوهُ‏.‏ فَيُقَالُ لَهُمْ لَكُمْ مَا رَأَيْتُمْ وَمِثْلُهُ مَعَهُ

And the Almighty says: "My intercession remains." And He takes a handful from the Fire, and brings out peoples who have been burnt black, and they are cast into a river at the mouths of the Garden called the water of life; and they sprout on its two banks as the seed sprouts in the silt of the torrent. You have seen it beside the rock, beside the tree: what of it was toward the sun was green, and what of it was toward the shade was white. And they come out as if they were pearls, and seals are put on their necks, and they enter the Garden. And the people of the Garden say: "These are the freed ones of the Merciful; He admitted them to the Garden without a deed they did or a good they sent ahead." And it is said to them: "For you is what you saw, and its like with it."

Further references:
- **The intercession's last round**: in Bukhari 7510 the intercession goes down to "the least, least, least of a mustard seed's weight of faith". At the fourth round God says: "I will bring out of it whoever said there is no god but God."
- **The last man**:
  - those brought out are known "by the mark of prostration" (Bukhari 7437);
  - the last man is given "the like of the world and ten times its like" (Muslim 186);
  - "his Lord excuses him" (Muslim 187).

**Predestination.** Each person "is made easy for what he was created for", and the command is still "Work".

**Bukhari 4949** (from 'Ali; also bukhari:1362, 4945, 4946, 4947, 4948, 6217, 6605, 7552; muslim:2647)

> مَا مِنْكُمْ مِنْ أَحَدٍ إِلاَّ وَقَدْ كُتِبَ مَقْعَدُهُ مِنَ النَّارِ وَمَقْعَدُهُ مِنَ الْجَنَّةِ ‏"‏‏.‏ قَالُوا يَا رَسُولَ اللَّهِ أَفَلاَ نَتَّكِلُ عَلَى كِتَابِنَا وَنَدَعُ الْعَمَلَ قَالَ ‏"‏ اعْمَلُوا فَكُلٌّ مُيَسَّرٌ لِمَا خُلِقَ لَهُ، أَمَّا مَنْ كَانَ مِنْ أَهْلِ السَّعَادَةِ فَيُيَسَّرُ لِعَمَلِ أَهْلِ السَّعَادَةِ، وَأَمَّا مَنْ كَانَ مِنْ أَهْلِ الشَّقَاءِ فَيُيَسَّرُ لِعَمَلِ أَهْلِ الشَّقَاوَةِ ‏"‏‏.‏ ثُمَّ قَرَأَ ‏{‏فَأَمَّا مَنْ أَعْطَى وَاتَّقَى * وَصَدَّقَ بِالْحُسْنَى‏

"There is none of you but his seat in the Fire and his seat in the Garden have been written." They said: "O Messenger of God, shall we not rely on our writing and leave the work?" He said: "Work, for each is made easy for what he was created for: as for whoever is of the people of happiness, he is made easy for the work of the people of happiness; and as for whoever is of the people of wretchedness, he is made easy for the work of the people of wretchedness." Then he recited: "So as for him who gave and feared [God], and affirmed the best" (92:5 to 6).

**Muslim 2643** (from Ibn Mas'ud; also bukhari:3208, 3332, 6594, 7454)

> فَوَالَّذِي لاَ إِلَهَ غَيْرُهُ إِنَّ أَحَدَكُمْ لَيَعْمَلُ بِعَمَلِ أَهْلِ الْجَنَّةِ حَتَّى مَا يَكُونَ بَيْنَهُ وَبَيْنَهَا إِلاَّ ذِرَاعٌ فَيَسْبِقُ عَلَيْهِ الْكِتَابُ فَيَعْمَلُ بِعَمَلِ أَهْلِ النَّارِ فَيَدْخُلُهَا وَإِنَّ أَحَدَكُمْ لَيَعْمَلُ بِعَمَلِ أَهْلِ النَّارِ حَتَّى مَا يَكُونَ بَيْنَهُ وَبَيْنَهَا إِلاَّ ذِرَاعٌ فَيَسْبِقُ عَلَيْهِ الْكِتَابُ فَيَعْمَلُ بِعَمَلِ أَهْلِ الْجَنَّةِ فَيَدْخُلُهَا

"By Him besides whom there is no god, one of you does the deed of the people of the Garden until there is not between him and it but an arm's length, and the writing outstrips him, and he does the deed of the people of the Fire, and enters it; and one of you does the deed of the people of the Fire until there is not between him and it but an arm's length, and the writing outstrips him, and he does the deed of the people of the Garden, and enters it."

Further references: "deeds are only by their endings" (Bukhari 6493); Muslim 2662; Bukhari 6596.

#### The language of exchange

The words used are:
- guarantee and surety;
- "a right upon God";
- recompense and compensation;
- the pledge "for the Garden".

No report in either collection uses price, purchase, trade or loan of the Garden itself.

**Muslim 1876** (from Abu Hurayra; also bukhari:36, 2787, 3123, 7457, 7463)

> تَضَمَّنَ اللَّهُ لِمَنْ خَرَجَ فِي سَبِيلِهِ لاَ يُخْرِجُهُ إِلاَّ جِهَادًا فِي سَبِيلِي وَإِيمَانًا بِي وَتَصْدِيقًا بِرُسُلِي فَهُوَ عَلَىَّ ضَامِنٌ أَنْ أُدْخِلَهُ الْجَنَّةَ أَوْ أَرْجِعَهُ إِلَى مَسْكَنِهِ الَّذِي خَرَجَ مِنْهُ نَائِلاً مَا نَالَ مِنْ أَجْرٍ أَوْ غَنِيمَةٍ

"God has guaranteed, for whoever went out in His way, nothing bringing him out but jihad in My way and faith in Me and belief in My messengers: he is upon Me a guarantee, that I admit him to the Garden, or return him to his dwelling from which he went out, obtaining what he obtained of reward or booty."

**Muslim 1901** (from Anas)

> فَانْطَلَقَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صلى الله عليه وسلم وَأَصْحَابُهُ حَتَّى سَبَقُوا الْمُشْرِكِينَ إِلَى بَدْرٍ وَجَاءَ الْمُشْرِكُونَ فَقَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صلى الله عليه وسلم ‏"‏ لاَ يُقَدِّمَنَّ أَحَدٌ مِنْكُمْ إِلَى شَىْءٍ حَتَّى أَكُونَ أَنَا دُونَهُ ‏"‏ ‏.‏ فَدَنَا الْمُشْرِكُونَ فَقَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صلى الله عليه وسلم ‏"‏ قُومُوا إِلَى جَنَّةٍ عَرْضُهَا السَّمَوَاتُ وَالأَرْضُ ‏"‏ ‏.‏ قَالَ يَقُولُ عُمَيْرُ بْنُ الْحُمَامِ الأَنْصَارِيُّ يَا رَسُولَ اللَّهِ جَنَّةٌ عَرْضُهَا السَّمَوَاتُ وَالأَرْضُ قَالَ ‏"‏ نَعَمْ ‏"‏ ‏.‏ قَالَ بَخٍ بَخٍ ‏.‏ فَقَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صلى الله عليه وسلم ‏"‏ مَا يَحْمِلُكَ عَلَى قَوْلِكَ بَخٍ بَخٍ ‏"‏ ‏.‏ قَالَ لاَ وَاللَّهِ يَا رَسُولَ اللَّهِ إِلاَّ رَجَاءَةَ أَنْ أَكُونَ مِنْ أَهْلِهَا ‏.‏ قَالَ ‏"‏ فَإِنَّكَ مِنْ أَهْلِهَا ‏"‏ ‏.‏ فَأَخْرَجَ تَمَرَاتٍ مِنْ قَرْنِهِ فَجَعَلَ يَأْكُلُ مِنْهُنَّ ثُمَّ قَالَ لَئِنْ أَنَا حَيِيتُ حَتَّى آكُلَ تَمَرَاتِي هَذِهِ إِنَّهَا لَحَيَاةٌ طَوِيلَةٌ - قَالَ - فَرَمَى بِمَا كَانَ مَعَهُ مِنَ التَّمْرِ ‏.‏ ثُمَّ قَاتَلَهُمْ حَتَّى قُتِلَ

Then the Messenger of God and his companions set out until they reached Badr before the associators, and the associators came. The Messenger of God said: "Let none of you go forward to anything until I am before him." The associators came near, and the Messenger of God said: "Rise to a Garden whose width is the heavens and the earth." He said: 'Umayr b. al-Humam al-Ansari said: "O Messenger of God, a Garden whose width is the heavens and the earth?" He said: "Yes." He said: "Bakh, bakh!" The Messenger of God said: "What brings you to say 'bakh, bakh'?" He said: "No, by God, O Messenger of God, [nothing] but the hope that I be of its people." He said: "Then you are of its people." He brought out dates from his quiver and began to eat of them; then he said: "If I live until I eat these dates of mine, it is a long life." He said: So he threw away what he had with him of the dates; then he fought them until he was killed.

**Bukhari 3893** (from 'Ubada b. al-Samit; also bukhari:6873; muslim:1709)

> بَايَعْنَاهُ عَلَى أَنْ لاَ نُشْرِكَ بِاللَّهِ شَيْئًا، وَلاَ نَسْرِقَ، وَلاَ نَزْنِيَ، وَلاَ نَقْتُلَ النَّفْسَ الَّتِي حَرَّمَ اللَّهُ، وَلاَ نَنْتَهِبَ، وَلاَ نَعْصِيَ بِالْجَنَّةِ إِنْ فَعَلْنَا ذَلِكَ، فَإِنْ غَشِينَا مِنْ ذَلِكَ شَيْئًا كَانَ قَضَاءُ ذَلِكَ إِلَى اللَّهِ‏

"We pledged to him that we would not associate anything with God, nor steal, nor commit adultery, nor kill the soul which God made sacred, nor plunder, nor disobey, for the Garden, if we did that; and if we committed any of that, its judgment was with God."

Further references:
- **The right of the servants**: "the right of the servants upon God is that He not punish whoever does not associate anything with Him" (Bukhari 2856).
- **Guarantees**: "I guarantee to him the Garden" (Bukhari 6474).
- **Recompense and compensation**:
  - "no recompense except the Garden" (Bukhari 1773);
  - "I compensate him for them with the Garden" (Bukhari 5653);
  - "hoping for its reward and believing its promise" (Bukhari 2631).
- **Valuations against the world**:
  - "A bow's length in the Garden is better than what the sun rises upon and sets" (Bukhari 2793);
  - silk and gold and silver vessels "are theirs in the world and ours in the hereafter" (Bukhari 5426).

### 2.10 Bukhari and Muslim: what the Garden is

#### Method in brief

A search of the fawazahmed0 edition for Garden terms hit 991 entries (Bukhari 551, Muslim 440), or 562 reports once repeats are merged. Of these, 191 describe the Garden or its people (41 in both collections), and 116 that state only a condition are left to the conditions section. Bukhari is cited by the Fath al-Bari numbering and Muslim by Fu'ad 'Abd al-Baqi's, without sub-letters; the edition has no text for Muslim 2635 or 2971 to 2979, so the report on the poor Emigrants preceding the rich (2979) is not used. Every Arabic excerpt below was checked by machine as an exact substring of the edition's vocalized text, and the working file is `ext-ref/religion_ends/hadith_bm_descriptions.md`.

#### Names and places

**Al-Firdaws.** The hundred degrees and al-Firdaws come in one report.

**Bukhari 2790** (from Abu Hurayra; also bukhari:7423)

> إِنَّ فِي الْجَنَّةِ مِائَةَ دَرَجَةٍ أَعَدَّهَا اللَّهُ لِلْمُجَاهِدِينَ فِي سَبِيلِ اللَّهِ، مَا بَيْنَ الدَّرَجَتَيْنِ كَمَا بَيْنَ السَّمَاءِ وَالأَرْضِ، فَإِذَا سَأَلْتُمُ اللَّهَ فَاسْأَلُوهُ الْفِرْدَوْسَ، فَإِنَّهُ أَوْسَطُ الْجَنَّةِ وَأَعْلَى الْجَنَّةِ، أُرَاهُ فَوْقَهُ عَرْشُ الرَّحْمَنِ، وَمِنْهُ تَفَجَّرُ أَنْهَارُ الْجَنَّةِ

Indeed, in the Garden are a hundred degrees which God has prepared for those who strive (al-mujahidin) in the way of God; what is between the two degrees is as what is between the heaven and the earth. So when you ask God, ask Him for al-Firdaws, for it is the middle of the Garden and the highest of the Garden — I think [he said]: above it is the Throne of the Merciful — and from it the rivers of the Garden burst forth.

- In 2790 the clause on the Throne carries a transmitter's "I think [he said]". Bukhari 7423 has it without the hedge.
- Al-Firdaws is named in 8 Bukhari entries and none of Muslim.
- To Haritha's mother: "Is it one Garden? It is many gardens, and he is in the Garden of al-Firdaws" (Bukhari 3982; also 2809, 6550).

**The gates.**

**Bukhari 3257** (from Sahl b. Sa'd; also bukhari:1896; muslim:1152)

> فِي الْجَنَّةِ ثَمَانِيَةُ أَبْوَابٍ، فِيهَا باب يُسَمَّى الرَّيَّانَ لاَ يَدْخُلُهُ إِلاَّ الصَّائِمُونَ

In the Garden are eight gates; among them is a gate called al-Rayyan, which none enters except those who fast.

- Bukhari 1897 (Muslim 1027) names further gates by the deed: "the gate of prayer", "the gate of jihad", "the gate of al-Rayyan" and "the gate of charity".
- Abu Bakr asks: "is anyone called from those gates, all of them?" The answer is: "Yes, and I hope that you will be among them."

**'Adn.**

**Bukhari 4878** (from Abu Musa al-Ash'ari ('Abd Allah b. Qays); also bukhari:4880, bukhari:7444; muslim:180)

> جَنَّتَانِ مِنْ فِضَّةٍ، آنِيَتُهُمَا وَمَا فِيهِمَا وَجَنَّتَانِ مِنْ ذَهَبٍ آنِيَتُهُمَا وَمَا فِيهِمَا، وَمَا بَيْنَ الْقَوْمِ وَبَيْنَ أَنْ يَنْظُرُوا إِلَى رَبِّهِمْ إِلاَّ رِدَاءُ الْكِبْرِ عَلَى وَجْهِهِ فِي جَنَّةِ عَدْنٍ

Two Gardens of silver, their vessels and what is in them; and two Gardens of gold, their vessels and what is in them. And there is nothing between the people and their looking at their Lord except the cloak of grandeur upon His face, in the Garden of 'Adn.

- In a dream, a city "built of bricks of gold and bricks of silver" is called "the Garden of 'Adn". In it is "a palace like the white cloud" (Bukhari 7047; also 4674).

**Other names and places.**
- *Al-wasila*: "a rank in the Garden which is fitting only for one servant of the servants of God" (Muslim 384).
- *Rawda*: "a meadow of the meadows of the Garden", between the Prophet's house and pulpit (Bukhari 1196; Muslim 1391).
- A market: "a market which they come to every Friday" (Muslim 2833). The published English renders *suq* "street".
- God's name for the Garden: "You are My mercy" (Bukhari 4850).
- The Prophet's last words: "the highest companionship (al-rafiq al-a'la)" (Bukhari 4463; Muslim 2444).

#### Degrees and ranks

**Bukhari 3256** (from Abu Sa'id al-Khudri; also bukhari:6555 (Sahl b. Sa'd); muslim:2830 to 2831 (Sahl, then Abu Sa'id))

> إِنَّ أَهْلَ الْجَنَّةِ يَتَرَاءَيُونَ أَهْلَ الْغُرَفِ مِنْ فَوْقِهِمْ كَمَا يَتَرَاءَيُونَ الْكَوْكَبَ الدُّرِّيَّ الْغَابِرَ فِي الأُفُقِ مِنَ الْمَشْرِقِ أَوِ الْمَغْرِبِ، لِتَفَاضُلِ مَا بَيْنَهُمْ ‏"‏‏.‏ قَالُوا يَا رَسُولَ اللَّهِ، تِلْكَ مَنَازِلُ الأَنْبِيَاءِ لاَ يَبْلُغُهَا غَيْرُهُمْ قَالَ ‏"‏ بَلَى وَالَّذِي نَفْسِي بِيَدِهِ، رِجَالٌ آمَنُوا بِاللَّهِ وَصَدَّقُوا الْمُرْسَلِينَ

Indeed, the people of the Garden see the people of the rooms above them as they see the brilliant star lingering on the horizon, from the east or the west, because of the excellence [of some over others] between them. They said: "O Messenger of God, those are the dwellings of the prophets; none other than they reach them." He said: "Yes indeed — by Him in whose hand is my soul — men who believed in God and believed the messengers."

**Muslim 189** (from al-Mughira b. Shu'ba)

> سَأَلَ مُوسَى رَبَّهُ مَا أَدْنَى أَهْلِ الْجَنَّةِ مَنْزِلَةً قَالَ هُوَ رَجُلٌ يَجِيءُ بَعْدَ مَا أُدْخِلَ أَهْلُ الْجَنَّةِ الْجَنَّةَ فَيُقَالُ لَهُ ادْخُلِ الْجَنَّةَ ‏.‏ فَيَقُولُ أَىْ رَبِّ كَيْفَ وَقَدْ نَزَلَ النَّاسُ مَنَازِلَهُمْ وَأَخَذُوا أَخَذَاتِهِمْ فَيُقَالُ لَهُ أَتَرْضَى أَنْ يَكُونَ لَكَ مِثْلُ مُلْكِ مَلِكٍ مِنْ مُلُوكِ الدُّنْيَا فَيَقُولُ رَضِيتُ رَبِّ ‏.‏ فَيَقُولُ لَكَ ذَلِكَ وَمِثْلُهُ وَمِثْلُهُ وَمِثْلُهُ وَمِثْلُهُ ‏.‏ فَقَالَ فِي الْخَامِسَةِ رَضِيتُ رَبِّ ‏.‏ فَيَقُولُ هَذَا لَكَ وَعَشَرَةُ أَمْثَالِهِ وَلَكَ مَا اشْتَهَتْ نَفْسُكَ وَلَذَّتْ عَيْنُكَ ‏.‏ فَيَقُولُ رَضِيتُ رَبِّ ‏.‏ قَالَ رَبِّ فَأَعْلاَهُمْ مَنْزِلَةً قَالَ أُولَئِكَ الَّذِينَ أَرَدْتُ غَرَسْتُ كَرَامَتَهُمْ بِيَدِي وَخَتَمْتُ عَلَيْهَا فَلَمْ تَرَ عَيْنٌ وَلَمْ تَسْمَعْ أُذُنٌ وَلَمْ يَخْطُرْ عَلَى قَلْبِ بَشَرٍ

Moses asked his Lord: "What is the lowest of the people of the Garden in rank?" He said: "He is a man who comes after the people of the Garden have been entered into the Garden, and it is said to him: Enter the Garden. He says: O Lord, how, when the people have settled in their dwellings and taken their takings? It is said to him: Would you be pleased to have the like of the kingdom of a king of the kings of the world? He says: I am pleased, Lord. He says: Yours is that, and its like, and its like, and its like, and its like. He says at the fifth: I am pleased, Lord. He says: This is yours and ten times its like, and yours is what your soul desires and your eye delights in. He says: I am pleased, Lord." He said: "Lord, and the highest of them in rank?" He said: "Those are the ones whom I wanted: I planted their honour with My hand and set a seal upon it; so no eye has seen, and no ear has heard, and it has not occurred to the heart of a man."

- **The lowest in rank.** The last man out of the Fire is told: "yours is the like of the world and ten times its like" (Bukhari 6571; Muslim 186).
- **Its like, or ten times its like.** In Bukhari 7437, Abu Hurayra retained "That is yours, and its like with it". Abu Sa'id retained "That is yours, and ten times its like".
- **The lowest man's wives.** In Muslim 188 his "two wives of the white-eyed, wide-eyed [ones] (hur 'in)" greet him: "Praise be to God, who brought you to life for us and brought us to life for you."
- **The first to enter.**
  - The first group is "in the form of the moon on the night of the full moon" (Bukhari 3245, 3246, 3254, 3327; Muslim 2834).
  - This community says: "we are the first to enter the Garden" (Muslim 855).
- **The poor first.**
  - To the question "who is the first of people to cross?", the answer is "The poor of the Emigrants" (Muslim 315).
  - At the gate, "the people of fortune were held back" (Bukhari 5196).

#### Descriptions, grouped as in 2.2

**Rivers and springs.**

**Bukhari 6581** (from Anas b. Malik; also bukhari:4964)

> بَيْنَمَا أَنَا أَسِيرُ فِي الْجَنَّةِ إِذَا أَنَا بِنَهَرٍ حَافَتَاهُ قِبَابُ الدُّرِّ الْمُجَوَّفِ قُلْتُ مَا هَذَا يَا جِبْرِيلُ قَالَ هَذَا الْكَوْثَرُ الَّذِي أَعْطَاكَ رَبُّكَ‏.‏ فَإِذَا طِينُهُ ـ أَوْ طِيبُهُ ـ مِسْكٌ أَذْفَرُ

While I was walking in the Garden, behold, I [came] upon a river whose two banks are domes of hollowed pearl. I said: What is this, O Gabriel? He said: This is al-Kawthar, which your Lord has given you. And behold, its mud — or its scent — was pungent musk.

- Elsewhere al-Kawthar is "a river which my Lord has promised me" and also "a basin (hawd) to which my community comes down on the Day of Resurrection" (Muslim 400).
- At the Lote-tree of the Boundary are "two rivers hidden and two rivers manifest". "As for the two hidden, they are in the Garden" (Bukhari 3207; also 3887, 5610; Muslim 164).
- Of earthly rivers: "Sayhan and Jayhan and the Euphrates and the Nile: each is of the rivers of the Garden" (Muslim 2839).
- A spring is named in Muslim 315: "a spring in it called Salsabil".

**Food and drink.**

**Muslim 2835** (from Jabir b. 'Abd Allah)

> إِنَّ أَهْلَ الْجَنَّةِ يَأْكُلُونَ فِيهَا وَيَشْرَبُونَ وَلاَ يَتْفُلُونَ وَلاَ يَبُولُونَ وَلاَ يَتَغَوَّطُونَ وَلاَ يَمْتَخِطُونَ ‏"‏ ‏.‏ قَالُوا فَمَا بَالُ الطَّعَامِ قَالَ ‏"‏ جُشَاءٌ وَرَشْحٌ كَرَشْحِ الْمِسْكِ يُلْهَمُونَ التَّسْبِيحَ وَالتَّحْمِيدَ كَمَا يُلْهَمُونَ النَّفَسَ

Indeed, the people of the Garden eat in it and drink, and they do not spit, nor urinate, nor relieve themselves, nor blow their noses. They said: "Then what of the food?" He said: "A belch, and sweat like the sweat of musk. They are inspired with glorification and praise as they are inspired with breath."

- The first meal: the earth becomes "one loaf ... a lodging for the people of the Garden" (Bukhari 6520).
- "The extra lobe of the liver of the fish (al-nun)" comes first, then "the bull of the Garden" (Muslim 315).

**Bodies, garments and adornment.**

**Bukhari 3245** (from Abu Hurayra; also bukhari:3246, bukhari:3254, bukhari:3327; muslim:2834)

> أَوَّلُ زُمْرَةٍ تَلِجُ الْجَنَّةَ صُورَتُهُمْ عَلَى صُورَةِ الْقَمَرِ لَيْلَةَ الْبَدْرِ، لاَ يَبْصُقُونَ فِيهَا وَلاَ يَمْتَخِطُونَ وَلاَ يَتَغَوَّطُونَ، آنِيَتُهُمْ فِيهَا الذَّهَبُ، أَمْشَاطُهُمْ مِنَ الذَّهَبِ وَالْفِضَّةِ، وَمَجَامِرُهُمُ الأَلُوَّةُ، وَرَشْحُهُمُ الْمِسْكُ، وَلِكُلِّ وَاحِدٍ مِنْهُمْ زَوْجَتَانِ، يُرَى مُخُّ سُوقِهِمَا مِنْ وَرَاءِ اللَّحْمِ، مِنَ الْحُسْنِ، لاَ اخْتِلاَفَ بَيْنَهُمْ وَلاَ تَبَاغُضَ، قُلُوبُهُمْ قَلْبٌ وَاحِدٌ، يُسَبِّحُونَ اللَّهَ بُكْرَةً وَعَشِيًّا

The first group to enter the Garden: their form is in the form of the moon on the night of the full moon. They do not spit in it, nor blow their noses, nor relieve themselves. Their vessels in it are gold; their combs are of gold and silver; their censers [burn] aloeswood; and their sweat is musk. Each one of them has two wives, the marrow of whose shins is seen from beyond the flesh, from beauty. There is no disagreement among them and no mutual hatred; their hearts are one heart; they glorify God morning and evening.

**Muslim 2837** (from Abu Sa'id al-Khudri and Abu Hurayra)

> يُنَادِي مُنَادٍ إِنَّ لَكُمْ أَنْ تَصِحُّوا فَلاَ تَسْقَمُوا أَبَدًا وَإِنَّ لَكُمْ أَنْ تَحْيَوْا فَلاَ تَمُوتُوا أَبَدًا وَإِنَّ لَكُمْ أَنْ تَشِبُّوا فَلاَ تَهْرَمُوا أَبَدًا وَإِنَّ لَكُمْ أَنْ تَنْعَمُوا فَلاَ تَبْتَئِسُوا أَبَدًا ‏"‏ ‏.‏ فَذَلِكَ قَوْلُهُ عَزَّ وَجَلَّ ‏{‏ وَنُودُوا أَنْ تِلْكُمُ الْجَنَّةُ أُورِثْتُمُوهَا بِمَا كُنْتُمْ تَعْمَلُونَ

A caller calls: "It is for you to be healthy, and you will never be ill; it is for you to live, and you will never die; it is for you to be young, and you will never grow old; and it is for you to be in bliss, and you will never suffer misery." And that is His saying: {And they will be called: That is the Garden; you have been made to inherit it for what you used to do} (7:43).

- Stature is "in the form of their father Adam, sixty cubits in the heaven" (Bukhari 3327; also 3326; Muslim 2841).
- Whoever enters "is in bliss and does not suffer misery; his clothes do not wear out and his youth does not pass away" (Muslim 2836).

**Dwellings and furnishings.**

**Bukhari 4879** (from Abu Musa al-Ash'ari ('Abd Allah b. Qays); also bukhari:3243, bukhari:4880; muslim:2838)

> إِنَّ فِي الْجَنَّةِ خَيْمَةً مِنْ لُؤْلُؤَةٍ مُجَوَّفَةٍ، عَرْضُهَا سِتُّونَ مِيلاً، فِي كُلِّ زَاوِيَةٍ مِنْهَا أَهْلٌ، مَا يَرَوْنَ الآخَرِينَ يَطُوفُ عَلَيْهِمُ الْمُؤْمِنُونَ

Indeed, in the Garden is a tent of a hollowed pearl; its width is sixty miles; in every corner of it is a family who do not see the others; the believers go round to them.

**Bukhari 3252** (from Abu Hurayra; also bukhari:3251 (Anas), bukhari:3253, bukhari:4881, bukhari:6552 (Sahl b. Sa'd), bukhari:6553 (Abu Sa'id); muslim:2826, muslim:2827 to 2828 (Sahl, then Abu Sa'id))

> إِنَّ فِي الْجَنَّةِ لَشَجَرَةً يَسِيرُ الرَّاكِبُ فِي ظِلِّهَا مِائَةَ سَنَةٍ، وَاقْرَءُوا إِنْ شِئْتُمْ ‏{‏وَظِلٍّ مَمْدُودٍ

Indeed, in the Garden is a tree in whose shade the rider travels a hundred years. And recite, if you wish: {and shade extended} (56:30).

- The published English renders *ahl* in the tent as "wives". The Arabic says "family".
- Other dwellings are "domes of pearl" with soil that "is musk" (Muslim 163; Bukhari 3342), and a house "of qasab, in which is no clamour and no toil" (Bukhari 3820; Muslim 2432).

**Companions.**

**Bukhari 2796** (from Anas b. Malik; also bukhari:6567, bukhari:6568)

> وَلَوْ أَنَّ امْرَأَةً مِنْ أَهْلِ الْجَنَّةِ اطَّلَعَتْ إِلَى أَهْلِ الأَرْضِ لأَضَاءَتْ مَا بَيْنَهُمَا وَلَمَلأَتْهُ رِيحًا، وَلَنَصِيفُهَا عَلَى رَأْسِهَا خَيْرٌ مِنَ الدُّنْيَا وَمَا فِيهَا

And if a woman of the people of the Garden looked down upon the people of the earth, she would light up what is between them and fill it with fragrance; and her veil upon her head is better than the world and what is in it.

**Muslim 2834** (from Abu Hurayra (through Muhammad b. Sirin))

> إِمَّا تَفَاخَرُوا وَإِمَّا تَذَاكَرُوا الرِّجَالُ فِي الْجَنَّةِ أَكْثَرُ أَمِ النِّسَاءُ فَقَالَ أَبُو هُرَيْرَةَ أَوَلَمْ يَقُلْ أَبُو الْقَاسِمِ صلى الله عليه وسلم ‏ "‏ إِنَّ أَوَّلَ زُمْرَةٍ تَدْخُلُ الْجَنَّةَ عَلَى صُورَةِ الْقَمَرِ لَيْلَةَ الْبَدْرِ وَالَّتِي تَلِيهَا عَلَى أَضْوَإِ كَوْكَبٍ دُرِّيٍّ فِي السَّمَاءِ لِكُلِّ امْرِئٍ مِنْهُمْ زَوْجَتَانِ اثْنَتَانِ يُرَى مُخُّ سُوقِهِمَا مِنْ وَرَاءِ اللَّحْمِ وَمَا فِي الْجَنَّةِ أَعْزَبُ

Either they boasted or they discussed: are men more [numerous] in the Garden, or women? Abu Hurayra said: Did not Abu al-Qasim say: "Indeed, the first group to enter the Garden are in the form of the moon on the night of the full moon, and the one following it upon [the form of] the brightest brilliant star in the heaven. Each man of them has two wives, two, the marrow of whose shins is seen from beyond the flesh; and there is not in the Garden anyone unmarried."

- The published English of Bukhari 2796 has "a houri from Paradise". The Arabic says "a woman of the people of the Garden".
- In Bukhari 3254 and 3327 the wives are hur. In Bukhari 3245 and Muslim 2834 they are not so named.

**Social and inner state.**

**Bukhari 6535** (from Abu Sa'id al-Khudri; also bukhari:2440)

> يَخْلُصُ الْمُؤْمِنُونَ مِنَ النَّارِ، فَيُحْبَسُونَ عَلَى قَنْطَرَةٍ بَيْنَ الْجَنَّةِ وَالنَّارِ، فَيُقَصُّ لِبَعْضِهِمْ مِنْ بَعْضٍ، مَظَالِمُ كَانَتْ بَيْنَهُمْ فِي الدُّنْيَا، حَتَّى إِذَا هُذِّبُوا وَنُقُّوا أُذِنَ لَهُمْ فِي دُخُولِ الْجَنَّةِ، فَوَالَّذِي نَفْسُ مُحَمَّدٍ بِيَدِهِ لأَحَدُهُمْ أَهْدَى بِمَنْزِلِهِ فِي الْجَنَّةِ مِنْهُ بِمَنْزِلِهِ كَانَ فِي الدُّنْيَا

The believers are delivered from the Fire, and they are held on a bridge between the Garden and the Fire, and retaliation is made for some of them from others for wrongs that were between them in the world; until, when they have been refined and purified, they are given leave to enter the Garden. By Him in whose hand is the soul of Muhammad, one of them is better guided to his dwelling in the Garden than he was to his dwelling in the world.

- In Bukhari 3245, "their hearts are one heart".
- In Bukhari 3254, "there is no mutual hatred among them and no mutual envy".
- "They are inspired with glorification and praise as they are inspired with breath" (Muslim 2835).

**Desire fulfilled.**

**Bukhari 4780** (from Abu Hurayra; also bukhari:3244, bukhari:4779, bukhari:7498; muslim:2824; muslim:2825 (Sahl b. Sa'd: "in it is what no eye has seen ..."))

> يَقُولُ اللَّهُ تَعَالَى أَعْدَدْتُ لِعِبَادِي الصَّالِحِينَ مَا لاَ عَيْنٌ رَأَتْ، وَلاَ أُذُنٌ سَمِعَتْ، وَلاَ خَطَرَ عَلَى قَلْبِ بَشَرٍ، ذُخْرًا، بَلْهَ مَا أُطْلِعْتُمْ عَلَيْهِ ‏"‏‏.‏ ثُمَّ قَرَأَ ‏{‏فَلاَ تَعْلَمُ نَفْسٌ مَا أُخْفِيَ لَهُمْ مِنْ قُرَّةِ أَعْيُنٍ جَزَاءً بِمَا كَانُوا يَعْمَلُونَ

God says: "I have prepared for My righteous servants what no eye has seen, and no ear has heard, and has not occurred to the heart of a man — as a store; leave aside what you have been shown of it." Then he recited: {So no soul knows what has been hidden for them of coolness of eyes, as a recompense for what they used to do} (32:17).

- The man who sows in the Garden is told: "Take it, son of Adam, for nothing satisfies you" (Bukhari 2348; also 7519).
- "The place of a whip in the Garden is better than the world and what is in it" (Bukhari 3250).
- Each one who enters "is shown his seat in the Fire, had he done ill, that he may increase in thankfulness" (Bukhari 6569). The published English has "if he had rejected faith".

**Death slaughtered.**

**Bukhari 4730** (from Abu Sa'id al-Khudri; also muslim:2849)

> يُؤْتَى بِالْمَوْتِ كَهَيْئَةِ كَبْشٍ أَمْلَحَ فَيُنَادِي مُنَادٍ يَا أَهْلَ الْجَنَّةِ، فَيَشْرَئِبُّونَ وَيَنْظُرُونَ فَيَقُولُ هَلْ تَعْرِفُونَ هَذَا فَيَقُولُونَ نَعَمْ هَذَا الْمَوْتُ، وَكُلُّهُمْ قَدْ رَآهُ، ثُمَّ يُنَادِي يَا أَهْلَ النَّارِ، فَيَشْرَئِبُّونَ وَيَنْظُرُونَ، فَيَقُولُ هَلْ تَعْرِفُونَ هَذَا فَيَقُولُونَ نَعَمْ هَذَا الْمَوْتُ، وَكُلُّهُمْ قَدْ رَآهُ، فَيُذْبَحُ ثُمَّ يَقُولُ يَا أَهْلَ الْجَنَّةِ، خُلُودٌ فَلاَ مَوْتَ، وَيَا أَهْلَ النَّارِ، خُلُودٌ فَلاَ مَوْتَ ثُمَّ قَرَأَ ‏{‏وَأَنْذِرْهُمْ يَوْمَ الْحَسْرَةِ إِذْ قُضِيَ الأَمْرُ وَهُمْ فِي غَفْلَةٍ‏}‏ وَهَؤُلاَءِ فِي غَفْلَةٍ أَهْلُ الدُّنْيَا ‏{‏وَهُمْ لاَ يُؤْمِنُونَ

Death is brought in the shape of a black-and-white ram, and a caller calls: "O people of the Garden!" They crane their necks and look. He says: "Do you know this?" They say: "Yes, this is death" — and all of them have seen it. Then he calls: "O people of the Fire!" They crane their necks and look. He says: "Do you know this?" They say: "Yes, this is death" — and all of them have seen it. Then it is slaughtered. Then he says: "O people of the Garden, everlastingness, so no death; and O people of the Fire, everlastingness, so no death." Then he recited: {And warn them of the Day of Regret, when the matter is decided, while they are in heedlessness} (19:39) — and these in heedlessness are the people of the world — {and they do not believe}.

- Ibn 'Umar's version adds: "So the people of the Garden increase in joy upon their joy" (Bukhari 6548; Muslim 2850).

#### God

**Bukhari 554** (from Jarir b. 'Abd Allah; also bukhari:573, bukhari:4851, bukhari:7434, bukhari:7436; muslim:633)

> إِنَّكُمْ سَتَرَوْنَ رَبَّكُمْ كَمَا تَرَوْنَ هَذَا الْقَمَرَ لاَ تُضَامُّونَ فِي رُؤْيَتِهِ، فَإِنِ اسْتَطَعْتُمْ أَنْ لاَ تُغْلَبُوا عَلَى صَلاَةٍ قَبْلَ طُلُوعِ الشَّمْسِ وَقَبْلَ غُرُوبِهَا فَافْعَلُوا ‏"‏‏.‏ ثُمَّ قَرَأَ ‏{‏وَسَبِّحْ بِحَمْدِ رَبِّكَ قَبْلَ طُلُوعِ الشَّمْسِ وَقَبْلَ الْغُرُوبِ

"Indeed, you will see your Lord as you see this moon; you will not crowd one another in seeing Him. So if you are able not to be overcome [and miss] a prayer before the rising of the sun and before its setting, do so." Then he recited: {And glorify with the praise of your Lord before the rising of the sun and before the setting} (50:39).

**Muslim 181** (from Suhayb)

> إِذَا دَخَلَ أَهْلُ الْجَنَّةِ الْجَنَّةَ - قَالَ - يَقُولُ اللَّهُ تَبَارَكَ وَتَعَالَى تُرِيدُونَ شَيْئًا أَزِيدُكُمْ فَيَقُولُونَ أَلَمْ تُبَيِّضْ وُجُوهَنَا أَلَمْ تُدْخِلْنَا الْجَنَّةَ وَتُنَجِّنَا مِنَ النَّارِ - قَالَ - فَيَكْشِفُ الْحِجَابَ فَمَا أُعْطُوا شَيْئًا أَحَبَّ إِلَيْهِمْ مِنَ النَّظَرِ إِلَى رَبِّهِمْ

When the people of the Garden have entered the Garden — he said — God says: "Do you want anything, that I may increase you?" They say: "Have You not whitened our faces? Have You not entered us into the Garden and saved us from the Fire?" He said: Then He removes the veil, and they have not been given anything more beloved to them than looking at their Lord.

**Bukhari 6549** (from Abu Sa'id al-Khudri; also bukhari:7518; muslim:2829)

> إِنَّ اللَّهَ يَقُولُ لأَهْلِ الْجَنَّةِ يَا أَهْلَ الْجَنَّةِ‏.‏ يَقُولُونَ لَبَّيْكَ رَبَّنَا وَسَعْدَيْكَ‏.‏ فَيَقُولُ هَلْ رَضِيتُمْ فَيَقُولُونَ وَمَا لَنَا لاَ نَرْضَى وَقَدْ أَعْطَيْتَنَا مَا لَمْ تُعْطِ أَحَدًا مِنْ خَلْقِكَ‏.‏ فَيَقُولُ أَنَا أُعْطِيكُمْ أَفْضَلَ مِنْ ذَلِكَ‏.‏ قَالُوا يَا رَبِّ وَأَىُّ شَىْءٍ أَفْضَلُ مِنْ ذَلِكَ فَيَقُولُ أُحِلُّ عَلَيْكُمْ رِضْوَانِي فَلاَ أَسْخَطُ عَلَيْكُمْ بَعْدَهُ أَبَدًا

Indeed, God says to the people of the Garden: "O people of the Garden!" They say: "Here we are, our Lord, at Your service!" He says: "Are you pleased?" They say: "And why should we not be pleased, when You have given us what You have not given anyone of Your creation?" He says: "I give you better than that." They say: "O Lord, and what thing is better than that?" He says: "I make My good pleasure alight upon you, and I will not be displeased with you after it, ever."

- In the Garden of 'Adn only "the cloak of grandeur upon His face" stands between them and their Lord (Bukhari 4878, above). The published English has "curtain".
- Bukhari 7437 (Muslim 182) places a vision at the gathering, before the bridge: "Then you will see Him so."
- At death the believer "is given glad tidings of the good pleasure of God and His honour" (Bukhari 6507).

#### Who is in the Garden, and in what proportion

**Bukhari 3241** (from 'Imran b. Husayn; also bukhari:5198, bukhari:6449, bukhari:6546; muslim:2737 (Ibn 'Abbas, and 'Imran))

> اطَّلَعْتُ فِي الْجَنَّةِ فَرَأَيْتُ أَكْثَرَ أَهْلِهَا الْفُقَرَاءَ، وَاطَّلَعْتُ فِي النَّارِ فَرَأَيْتُ أَكْثَرَ أَهْلِهَا النِّسَاءَ

I looked into the Garden, and I saw most of its people [were] the poor; and I looked into the Fire, and I saw most of its people [were] women.

**Bukhari 4850** (from Abu Hurayra; also bukhari:7449; muslim:2846)

> تَحَاجَّتِ الْجَنَّةُ وَالنَّارُ فَقَالَتِ النَّارُ أُوثِرْتُ بِالْمُتَكَبِّرِينَ وَالْمُتَجَبِّرِينَ‏.‏ وَقَالَتِ الْجَنَّةُ مَا لِي لاَ يَدْخُلُنِي إِلاَّ ضُعَفَاءُ النَّاسِ وَسَقَطُهُمْ‏.‏ قَالَ اللَّهُ تَبَارَكَ وَتَعَالَى لِلْجَنَّةِ أَنْتِ رَحْمَتِي أَرْحَمُ بِكِ مَنْ أَشَاءُ مِنْ عِبَادِي‏.‏ وَقَالَ لِلنَّارِ إِنَّمَا أَنْتِ عَذَابٌ أُعَذِّبُ بِكِ مَنْ أَشَاءُ مِنْ عِبَادِي‏.‏ وَلِكُلِّ وَاحِدَةٍ مِنْهُمَا مِلْؤُهَا، فَأَمَّا النَّارُ فَلاَ تَمْتَلِئُ حَتَّى يَضَعَ رِجْلَهُ فَتَقُولُ قَطٍ قَطٍ قَطٍ‏.‏ فَهُنَالِكَ تَمْتَلِئُ وَيُزْوَى بَعْضُهَا إِلَى بَعْضٍ، وَلاَ يَظْلِمُ اللَّهُ ـ عَزَّ وَجَلَّ ـ مِنْ خَلْقِهِ أَحَدًا، وَأَمَّا الْجَنَّةُ فَإِنَّ اللَّهَ عَزَّ وَجَلَّ يُنْشِئُ لَهَا خَلْقًا

The Garden and the Fire disputed. The Fire said: "I have been favoured with the arrogant and the tyrants." And the Garden said: "What is it with me, that none enters me except the weak of the people and their lowly?" God said to the Garden: "You are My mercy; I show mercy by you to whom I will of My servants." And He said to the Fire: "You are only punishment; I punish by you whom I will of My servants. And for each one of them is its fill." As for the Fire, it is not filled until He places His foot, and it says: "Enough, enough, enough." There it is filled, and its parts are drawn together; and God does not wrong anyone of His creation. And as for the Garden, God produces for it a creation.

**Bukhari 6528** (from 'Abd Allah b. Mas'ud; also bukhari:6642; muslim:221; the same sequence from Abu Sa'id: bukhari:3348, bukhari:4741, bukhari:6530; muslim:222)

> أَتَرْضَوْنَ أَنْ تَكُونُوا رُبُعَ أَهْلِ الْجَنَّةِ ‏"‏‏.‏ قُلْنَا نَعَمْ‏.‏ قَالَ ‏"‏ تَرْضَوْنَ أَنْ تَكُونُوا ثُلُثَ أَهْلِ الْجَنَّةِ ‏"‏‏.‏ قُلْنَا نَعَمْ‏.‏ قَالَ ‏"‏ أَتَرْضَوْنَ أَنْ تَكُونُوا شَطْرَ أَهْلِ الْجَنَّةِ ‏"‏‏.‏ قُلْنَا نَعَمْ‏.‏ قَالَ ‏"‏ وَالَّذِي نَفْسُ مُحَمَّدٍ بِيَدِهِ إِنِّي لأَرْجُو أَنْ تَكُونُوا نِصْفَ أَهْلِ الْجَنَّةِ، وَذَلِكَ أَنَّ الْجَنَّةَ لاَ يَدْخُلُهَا إِلاَّ نَفْسٌ مُسْلِمَةٌ، وَمَا أَنْتُمْ فِي أَهْلِ الشِّرْكِ إِلاَّ كَالشَّعْرَةِ الْبَيْضَاءِ فِي جِلْدِ الثَّوْرِ الأَسْوَدِ أَوْ كَالشَّعْرَةِ السَّوْدَاءِ فِي جِلْدِ الثَّوْرِ الأَحْمَرِ

"Are you pleased to be a quarter of the people of the Garden?" We said: "Yes." He said: "Are you pleased to be a third of the people of the Garden?" We said: "Yes." He said: "Are you pleased to be half of the people of the Garden?" We said: "Yes." He said: "By Him in whose hand is the soul of Muhammad, I hope that you will be half of the people of the Garden; and that is because none enters the Garden but a Muslim soul, and you among the people of association (shirk) are only like the white hair on the hide of the black bull, or like the black hair on the hide of the red bull."

- **Women.** In Muslim 2738: "Indeed, the fewest of the inhabitants of the Garden are women."
  - Yet each man of the first group has "two wives", and "there is not in the Garden anyone unmarried" (Muslim 2834).
  - Al-Nawawi (vol. 17, p. 171) gives al-Qadi 'Iyad's reconciliation: women are the majority of mankind, and the hur add to each man's share.
  - The Fire's majority of women is explained in the words "They are ungrateful to the companion, and ungrateful for kindness" (Bukhari 29).
- **The weak.**
  - "Every weak one, humbling himself" (Bukhari 4918; Muslim 2853). Muslim's vowels read "deemed weak".
  - Bukhari 7384 adds that God "produces for it a creation and settles them in the surplus of the Garden".
- **Seventy thousand** enter "holding together, one taking hold of another" (Bukhari 6543; Muslim 219).
- **From the Fire.**
  - Some are "spread on the rivers of the Garden" (Muslim 185).
  - Others are named by the people of the Garden "the Jahannamiyyun" (Bukhari 6559).
- **Children.** Around Abraham is "every child born who died upon the natural disposition (fitra)", including the children of the polytheists (Bukhari 7047).

#### The martyrs

**Muslim 1887** (from 'Abd Allah b. Mas'ud (asked by Masruq about 3:169))

> أَرْوَاحُهُمْ فِي جَوْفِ طَيْرٍ خُضْرٍ لَهَا قَنَادِيلُ مُعَلَّقَةٌ بِالْعَرْشِ تَسْرَحُ مِنَ الْجَنَّةِ حَيْثُ شَاءَتْ ثُمَّ تَأْوِي إِلَى تِلْكَ الْقَنَادِيلِ فَاطَّلَعَ إِلَيْهِمْ رَبُّهُمُ اطِّلاَعَةً فَقَالَ هَلْ تَشْتَهُونَ شَيْئًا قَالُوا أَىَّ شَىْءٍ نَشْتَهِي وَنَحْنُ نَسْرَحُ مِنَ الْجَنَّةِ حَيْثُ شِئْنَا فَفَعَلَ ذَلِكَ بِهِمْ ثَلاَثَ مَرَّاتٍ فَلَمَّا رَأَوْا أَنَّهُمْ لَنْ يُتْرَكُوا مِنْ أَنْ يُسْأَلُوا قَالُوا يَا رَبِّ نُرِيدُ أَنْ تَرُدَّ أَرْوَاحَنَا فِي أَجْسَادِنَا حَتَّى نُقْتَلَ فِي سَبِيلِكَ مَرَّةً أُخْرَى ‏.‏ فَلَمَّا رَأَى أَنْ لَيْسَ لَهُمْ حَاجَةٌ تُرِكُوا

Their souls are in the bellies of green birds, which have lamps hung from the Throne; they roam from the Garden wherever they wish, then betake themselves to those lamps. Their Lord looked upon them once and said: "Do you desire anything?" They said: "What should we desire, when we roam from the Garden wherever we wish?" He did that with them three times. When they saw that they would not be left without being asked, they said: "O Lord, we want You to return our souls into our bodies, so that we may be killed in Your way another time." When He saw that they had no need, they were left.

**Bukhari 2817** (from Anas b. Malik; also muslim:1877)

> مَا أَحَدٌ يَدْخُلُ الْجَنَّةَ يُحِبُّ أَنْ يَرْجِعَ إِلَى الدُّنْيَا وَلَهُ مَا عَلَى الأَرْضِ مِنْ شَىْءٍ، إِلاَّ الشَّهِيدُ، يَتَمَنَّى أَنْ يَرْجِعَ إِلَى الدُّنْيَا فَيُقْتَلَ عَشْرَ مَرَّاتٍ، لِمَا يَرَى مِنَ الْكَرَامَةِ

No one who enters the Garden loves to return to the world, though he had whatever is on the earth, except the martyr: he wishes to return to the world and be killed ten times, for what he sees of the honour.

- The published English of Bukhari 2817 has "a Mujahid" for "the martyr".
- The English of Muslim 1887 has "eat the fruits of Paradise" for the souls' roaming "from the Garden wherever they wish".
- Bukhari 2795 gives the same wish "for what he sees of the merit of martyrdom".
- In a dream, after "the house of the generality of the believers", comes "the house of the martyrs" (Bukhari 1386).
- Haritha, killed at Badr, "has attained the highest al-Firdaws" (Bukhari 2809).
- At Badr, 'Umayr b. al-Humam hears "Rise to a Garden whose width is the heavens and the earth" and is told "Then you are of its people" (Muslim 1901).

### 2.11 The four Sunan and the Muwatta

#### Method in brief

- **Sweep.** The vocalized Arabic of Abu Dawud, al-Tirmidhi, al-Nasa'i, Ibn Majah and the Muwatta (fawazahmed0 hadith-api, sunnah.com numbering) was searched for the Garden words, the entry and bar formulas and entry into the Fire; 1,261 entries were read and 903 kept, carrying 553 distinct sayings.
- **Weight.** Of the kept rows, 351 are not in Bukhari or Muslim, 237 are there only in part or with an addition, and 315 are the same report; the counts below are rows / distinct sayings.
- **Grades.** Grades are those the edition lists (al-Albani, Zubair Ali Zai, Shakir, Bashar, al-Arna'ut, 'Abd al-Hamid, Abu Ghudda, 'Abd al-Baqi, and al-Hilali for the Muwatta), with Tirmidhi's own judgement, and a report any grader calls Da'if or Mawdu' is marked weak or fabricated, not dropped.
- **Files.** Translations follow 2.3, and every row is in `ext-ref/religion_ends/hadith_sunan.md`.

#### The conditions, in order of frequency

**1. Prayer and the other pillars** (prayer 94 / 54; fasting 32 / 20; alms 31 / 21; pilgrimage 15 / 7). The five prayers are a covenant. Bukhari and Muslim do not carry it.

**Abu Dawud 1420** (from 'Ubada b. al-Samit; also malik:267; nasai:461; ibnmajah:1401; grade: Al-Albani: Sahih; Muhammad Muhyi Al-Din Abdul Hamid: Sahih; Zubair Ali Zai: Isnaad Hasan)

> خَمْسُ صَلَوَاتٍ كَتَبَهُنَّ اللَّهُ عَلَى الْعِبَادِ فَمَنْ جَاءَ بِهِنَّ لَمْ يُضَيِّعْ مِنْهُنَّ شَيْئًا اسْتِخْفَافًا بِحَقِّهِنَّ كَانَ لَهُ عِنْدَ اللَّهِ عَهْدٌ أَنْ يُدْخِلَهُ الْجَنَّةَ وَمَنْ لَمْ يَأْتِ بِهِنَّ فَلَيْسَ لَهُ عِنْدَ اللَّهِ عَهْدٌ إِنْ شَاءَ عَذَّبَهُ وَإِنْ شَاءَ أَدْخَلَهُ الْجَنَّةَ

"Five prayers God has written upon the servants. Whoever comes with them, not having lost anything of them from making light of their due, has with God a covenant that He will admit him to the Garden. And whoever does not come with them has no covenant with God: if He wills, He punishes him, and if He wills, He admits him to the Garden."

Also Nasa'i 461 (Sahih), Ibn Majah 1401 (al-Albani Sahih, Zai Hasan) and Muwatta 267 (al-Hilali Sahih). The twelve rak'as that build a house are also in Muslim 728.
- **Noon.** "Whoever prays before the noon [prayer] four and after it four, God forbids him to the Fire" (Tirmidhi 427; Shakir, al-Albani and Zai Sahih).
- **The call to prayer.** Seven years of it bring "a release (bara'a) from the Fire" (Tirmidhi 206; weak, Da'if by all four graders). Twelve years: "the Garden is due to him" (Ibn Majah 728; al-Albani Sahih, Zai Da'if).

**2. Jihad, martyrdom and ribat** (jihad 55 / 37; kinds of martyr 50 / 25; the martyr 45 / 31; ribat 12 / 9).

**Tirmidhi 1663** (from al-Miqdam b. Ma'dikarib; also ibnmajah:2799; grade: Ahmad Muhammad Shakir: Sahih; Al-Albani: Sahih; Bashar Awad Maarouf: Sahih; Zubair Ali Zai: Hasan; Tirmidhi: هَذَا حَدِيثٌ صَحِيحٌ غَرِيبٌ "this is a hadith sahih gharib")

> لِلشَّهِيدِ عِنْدَ اللَّهِ سِتُّ خِصَالٍ يُغْفَرُ لَهُ فِي أَوَّلِ دَفْعَةٍ وَيَرَى مَقْعَدَهُ مِنَ الْجَنَّةِ وَيُجَارُ مِنْ عَذَابِ الْقَبْرِ وَيَأْمَنُ مِنَ الْفَزَعِ الأَكْبَرِ وَيُوضَعُ عَلَى رَأْسِهِ تَاجُ الْوَقَارِ الْيَاقُوتَةُ مِنْهَا خَيْرٌ مِنَ الدُّنْيَا وَمَا فِيهَا وَيُزَوَّجُ اثْنَتَيْنِ وَسَبْعِينَ زَوْجَةً مِنَ الْحُورِ الْعِينِ وَيُشَفَّعُ فِي سَبْعِينَ مِنْ أَقَارِبِهِ
