"By Him in whose hand is the soul of Muhammad, no man fights them today and is killed, steadfast, seeking the reward (muhtasib), advancing, not turning back, but God admits him to the Garden." 'Umayr b. al-Humam, brother of the Banu Salima, with dates in his hand that he was eating, said: "Bakh, bakh! Is there not, between me and my entering the Garden, [anything] but that these kill me?" Then he threw the dates from his hand, and took his sword, and fought the people until he was killed.

'Ubayda b. al-Harith, his leg cut off, put it as a sale.

**ibnhisham:2:24** (from 'Ubayda b. al-Harith, on his leg cut off at Badr (Ibn Hisham: some who know poetry deny it to him); also Guillaume p. 349)

> فإن تقطعوا رجلي فإنى مسلم ... أرجي بها عيشا من الله دانيا مع الحور أمثال التماثيل أخلصت ... مع الجنة العليا لمن كان عاليا [2] وبعت بها عيشا تعرقت صفوه ... وعالجته حتى فقدت الأدانيا

"If you cut off my leg, I am a Muslim: / I hope by it for a life from God near at hand, / with the houris, like statues, made pure, / with the highest Garden, for whoever was high. / And I sold for it a life whose clear [part] I had tasted, / and I tended it until I lost the nearest [kin]."

**Uhud.** Ibn Hisham's Anas b. al-Nadr says only "die on what the Messenger of God died on" (ibnhisham:2:83). Ibn Sa'd has "ah, for the scent of the Garden" (ibnsad:4:328), as Bukhari 2805 and Muslim 1903 do. 'Umar answered Abu Sufyan, at the Prophet's word: "our dead are in the Garden, and your dead in the Fire" (ibnhisham:2:93).

The commentary on Uhud gives the state of the dead as a message to the living.

**ibnhisham:2:119:b** (from Ibn 'Abbas, from the Messenger of God; also Guillaume p. 400; abudawud:2520; cf. muslim:1887)

> لما أصيب إخوانكم بأحد، جعل الله أرواحهم في أجواف طير خضر، ترد أنهار الجنة، وتأكل من ثمارها، وتأوي إلى قناديل من ذهب، في ظل العرش، فلما وجدوا طيب مشربهم ومأكلهم، وحسن مقيلهم، قالوا: يا ليت إخواننا يعلمون ما صنع الله بنا، لئلا يزهدوا في الجهاد، ولا ينكلوا عن الحرب

"When your brothers were struck at Uhud, God put their spirits in the bellies of green birds, which come down to the rivers of the Garden, and eat of its fruits, and take shelter in lamps of gold in the shade of the Throne. When they found the goodness of their drink and their food, and the beauty of their resting place, they said: 'Would that our brothers knew what God has done with us, lest they abstain from jihad and shrink from war.'"

Ibn Ishaq sets the reward against the world.

**ibnhisham:2:116** (from Ibn Ishaq, glossing 3:156-158; also Guillaume p. 397)

> فلا تغرنكم الدنيا، ولا تغتروا بها، وليكن الجهاد وما رغبكم الله فيه من ثوابه آثر عندكم منها.

So let not the world deceive you, and be not deluded by it; and let jihad, and what God has made you desire of His reward, be preferred by you to it.

Men at Uhud are promised the Garden by name:
- al-Usayrim, who became a Muslim that day and never prayed: "He is of the people of the Garden" (ibnhisham:2:90:c);
- 'Amr b. al-Jamuh, excused for lameness: "I hope to tread with this lameness of mine in the Garden" (ibnhisham:2:90:b);
- Talha, who shielded the Prophet: "Talha has made [it] due" (ibnhisham:2:86).

**Bi'r Ma'una.** The three works differ on who cried "I have won", and on what he won.

**ibnhisham:2:187** (from Jabbar b. Salma, who was with 'Amir b. al-Tufayl at Bi'r Ma'una and later became a Muslim (from some of his family); also Guillaume pp. 434-435; cf. bukhari:2801, 4092; muslim:677)

> إن مما دعاني إلى الإسلام أني طعنت رجلا منهم يومئذ بالرمح بين كتفيه، فنظرت إلى سنان الرمح حين خرج من صدره، فسمعته يقول: فزت والله! فقلت في نفسي: ما فاز! ألست قد قتلت الرجل! قال: حتى سألت بعد ذلك عن قوله، فقالوا: للشهادة، فقلت: فاز لعمر والله.

"Among what called me to Islam was that I speared a man of them that day between his shoulders, and I looked at the head of the spear when it came out of his chest, and I heard him say: 'I have won, by God!' I said to myself: 'He has not won! Have I not killed the man?' Until I asked afterwards about his saying, and they said: '[He won] martyrdom.' So I said: 'He has won, by my life, by God.'"

**waqidi:1:349** (from Jabbar b. Salma [al-Kilabi], who killed 'Amir b. Fuhayra at Bi'r Ma'una; also ibnhisham:2:187; bukhari:4092, muslim:677 (Haram b. Milhan: "I have won, by the Lord of the Ka'ba"))

> سمعته يقول «فزت والله» ! قال، فقلت في نفسي: ما قوله «فزت» ؟ قال: فأتيت الضحاك بن سفيان الكلابي فأخبرته بما كان وسألته عن قوله «فزت» ، فقال: الجنة. قال: وعرض علي الإسلام. قال: فأسلمت،

"I heard him say: 'I have won (fuztu), by God!'" He said: "I said in myself: 'What is his saying, I have won?'" He said: "So I came to al-Dahhak b. Sufyan al-Kilabi and told him what had happened, and asked him about his saying 'I have won', and he said: 'The Garden.'" He said: "And he offered me Islam." He said: "So I became a Muslim."

Ibn Sa'd has it from 'Amir b. Fuhayra (ibnsad:3:212), and from Haram b. Milhan, as Bukhari 4092 does: "I have won, by the Lord of the Ka'ba" (ibnsad:3:476).

**The Trench.** The Prophet asks for a man to scout the enemy. He promises "that he be my companion in the Garden", and "Not a man of the people rose, from the severity of fear, and the severity of hunger, and the severity of cold" (ibnhisham:2:232).

**Khaybar.** A slave shepherd asks what Islam offers him.

**waqidi:2:649:b** (from Yasar the Abyssinian, black slave of 'Amir the Jew, and the Prophet (Khaybar); also ibnhisham:2:344)

> فقال: يا محمد، ما تقول؟ ما تدعو إليه؟ قال: أدعوا إلى الإسلام، فأشهد أن لا إله إلا الله وأني رسول الله. قال: فما لي؟ قال: الجنة إن ثبت على ذلك. قال: فأسلم.

He said: "O Muhammad, what do you say? To what do you call?" He said: "I call to Islam: that you bear witness that there is no god but God and that I am the Messenger of God." He said: "So what is mine?" He said: "The Garden, if you stay firm on that." He said: So he became a Muslim.

Killed in the fighting, he has "his two wives of the wide-eyed houris (al-hur al-'in)" (ibnhisham:2:345).

**Mu'ta.** Ibn Rawaha told the army that martyrdom was what they had come for.

**ibnhisham:2:375** (from 'Abd Allah b. Rawaha, to the army at Ma'an, before Mu'ta; also Guillaume p. 533)

> يا قوم، والله إن التي تكرهون، للتي خرجتم تطلبون الشهادة، وما نقاتل الناس بعدد ولا قوة ولا كثرة، ما نقاتلهم إلا بهذا الدين الذي أكرمنا الله به، فانطلقوا فإنما هي إحدى الحسنيين إما ظهور وإما شهادة.

"O people, by God, that which you dislike is that which you came out seeking: martyrdom. We do not fight the people by number, nor strength, nor multitude; we fight them only by this religion with which God honoured us. So go on: it is only one of the two best things, either victory or martyrdom."

When his own turn came, he hesitated.

**ibnhisham:2:379** (from 'Abd Allah b. Rawaha at Mu'ta, hesitating, after Ja'far was killed; also Guillaume p. 534)

> أقسمت يا نفس لتنزلنه ... لتنزلن أو لتكرهنه إن أجلب الناس وشدوا الرنه ... ما لي أراك تكرهين الجنه قد طال ما قد كنت مطمئنه ... هل أنت إلا نطفة في شنه

"I have sworn, O soul, you will go down [to it]; / you will go down, or you will be made to hate it. / If the people have shouted and raised the cry, / why do I see you hating the Garden? / Long have you been at ease; / are you anything but a drop in a worn water-skin?"

Ja'far's hands were cut off holding the banner, and "God rewarded him for that with two wings in the Garden" (ibnhisham:2:378). In the Prophet's vision, Ibn Rawaha's bed turned aside because he "hesitated somewhat, then went on" (ibnhisham:2:380).

**Hunayn.** Ibn Hisham names no Garden. The reward he records is "his spoils (salab)" for the killer of a man (ibnhisham:2:448). Al-Waqidi has the Garden for those who stood firm: "God has guaranteed (takaffala) for them their provisions and the provisions of their dependents in the Garden" (waqidi:3:901).

**Tabuk.** Ibn Ishaq closes the campaign with Q 9:111, "God has bought from the believers their selves and their wealth, that theirs is the Garden" (ibnhisham:2:554). For 'Uthman's gift to the army, Ibn Hisham has "O God, be pleased with 'Uthman" (ibnhisham:2:518), where Bukhari 2778 promises the Garden.

#### Those who fought but were not promised the Garden

**ibnhisham:2:88:b** (from 'Asim b. 'Umar b. Qatada; also Guillaume p. 383; cf. bukhari:2898, 3062, 4202, 4203; muslim:111, 112)

> كان فينا رجل أتى لا يدرى ممن هو، يقال له: قزمان، وكان رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم يقول، إذا ذكر له: إنه لمن أهل النار، قال: فلما كان يوم أحد قاتل قتالا شديدا، فقتل وحده ثمانية أو سبعة من المشركين، وكان ذا بأس، فأثبتته الجراحة، فاحتمل إلى دار بني ظفر، قال: فجعل رجال من المسلمين يقولون له: والله لقد أبليت اليوم يا قزمان، فأبشر، قال: بماذا أبشر؟ فو الله إن قاتلت إلا عن أحساب قومي، ولولا ذلك ما قاتلت. قال: فلما اشتدت عليه جراحته أخذ سهما من كنانته، فقتل به نفسه.

There was among us a stranger (ati), it was not known from whom he was, called Quzman. The Messenger of God used to say, when he was mentioned to him: "He is of the people of the Fire." When it was the day of Uhud, he fought a fierce fighting, and killed by himself eight or seven of the polytheists, and he was a man of might. The wound pinned him down, and he was carried to the abode of the Banu Zafar. He said: Men of the Muslims began to say to him: "By God, you were tested well today, O Quzman, so rejoice." He said: "In what shall I rejoice? By God, I fought only for the honour (ahsab) of my people; and were it not for that, I would not have fought." He said: When his wound grew hard on him, he took an arrow from his quiver and killed himself with it.

The Sahihs tell a like case without a name: at Khaybar (Bukhari 4203, 6606), in a raid not named (Bukhari 2898; Muslim 112) and at Hunayn (Muslim 111). In Bukhari 3062, Bilal then proclaims that none but a Muslim soul enters the Garden. Ibn Sa'd's notice gives Quzman's reason, "I did not fight, Abu 'Umar, for a religion", and the Prophet's "God surely supports the religion by the wicked (fajir) man" (ibnsad:4:269).

A second case turns on booty.

**ibnhisham:2:339** (from Abu Hurayra, on Mid'am, the slave of the Messenger of God, at Wadi al-Qura after Khaybar; also Guillaume p. 516; bukhari:4234, 6707; muslim:115; abudawud:2711)

> إذ أتاه سهم غرب [1] فأصابه فقتله، فقلنا: هنيئا له الجنة، فقال رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم: كلا، والذي نفس محمد بيده، إن شملته الآن لتحترق عليه في النار، كان غلها من فيء المسلمين يوم خيبر.

... when a stray arrow came to him and struck him and killed him. We said: "Congratulations to him: the Garden." The Messenger of God said: "No, by Him in whose hand is the soul of Muhammad, his cloak is burning on him now in the Fire. He took it by fraud (ghallaha) from the spoil (fay') of the Muslims on the day of Khaybar."

Al-Waqidi has three more denials:
- Karkara, "burning in the Fire for a cloak (shamla) he took by stealth" (waqidi:2:681);
- a man killed at Khaybar after fighting before leave was given, with the crier's "The Garden is not lawful for a disobedient one" (waqidi:2:649);
- a man thrown by his camel on the march to Tabuk, with "none enters the Garden who is disobedient" (waqidi:3:995).

#### Named persons and their deeds

**ibnhisham:1:320** (from Ibn Ishaq ("as has reached me"), on 'Ammar b. Yasir, his father and his mother; also Guillaume p. 145)

> وكانوا أهل بيت إسلام، إذا حميت الظهيرة، يعذبونهم برمضاء مكة، فيمر بهم رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم فيقول، فيما بلغني: صبرا آل ياسر، موعدكم الجنة. فأما أمه فقتلوها، وهي تأبى إلا الإسلام.

They were a household of Islam. When the midday [heat] grew fierce, [the Banu Makhzum] would torture them on the burning ground of Mecca, and the Messenger of God would pass by them and say, as has reached me: "Patience, family of Yasir: your appointment is the Garden." As for his mother, they killed her, and she refused [all] but Islam.

The others promised the Garden by name, and the deed stated:
- 'Ammar, at the building of the mosque: "He calls them to the Garden, and they call him to the Fire" (ibnhisham:1:497);
- 'Abd Allah b. Jahsh, whose house in Mecca was sold: "a house better than it in the Garden" (ibnhisham:1:499);
- 'Ukkasha, who asked to be of the seventy thousand: "You are of them" (ibnhisham:1:638);
- Haritha b. Suraqa, killed at Badr: "it is many Gardens, and Haritha is in the best of them" (ibnsad:3:473);
- Sa'd b. Mu'adh: "the napkins of Sa'd b. Mu'adh in the Garden are finer than this" (ibnhisham:2:526);
- Thabit b. al-Dahdaha, who gave an orphan a palm: "A palm in the Garden" (waqidi:1:281).

Some state no deed: Nu'aym al-Nahham (ibnhisham:1:259), Umm Ayman, "a woman of the people of the Garden" (ibnsad:10:213), and the ten of Quraysh (ibnsad:3:356).

One congratulation is questioned. When 'Uthman b. Maz'un's wife congratulated him on the Garden, the Prophet answered: "And what makes you know?" (ibnsad:3:369).

#### The Night Journey and the Ascension

Ibn Hisham's account names the Garden once, after the seventh heaven.

**ibnhisham:1:407** (from the Messenger of God, in Abu Sa'id al-Khudri's account of the Ascension; also Guillaume p. 186)

> ثم دخل بي الجنة، فرأيت فيها جارية لعساء ، فسألتها: لمن أنت؟ وقد أعجبتني حين رأيتها، فقالت: لزيد ابن حارثة، فبشر بها رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم زيد بن حارثة.

"Then he made me enter the Garden, and I saw in it a damsel with dark red lips (la'sa'), and I asked her: 'To whom do you belong?', for she pleased me when I saw her. She said: 'To Zayd b. Haritha.'" So the Messenger of God gave Zayd b. Haritha the glad tidings of her.

The Fire is shown at length: its keeper uncovers it, and the punishments of sinners are seen one by one (ibnhisham:1:404 to 406). The account ends with the prayer: whoever performs the five "for him is the reward of fifty prayers" (ibnhisham:1:408). Ibn Sa'd says the Garden and the Fire were shown to him, and does not describe them (ibnsad:1:181). Al-Waqidi has no Ascension.

#### The language of exchange

The words of trade run through the Sira:
- **the price asked**: "So what is ours", answered "The Garden" (ibnhisham:1:446);
- **the stipulation**: the Prophet "stipulated upon the people for his Lord" (ibnhisham:1:454);
- **the guarantee**: "when their guarantor (za'im) came to us" (ibnhisham:2:25);
- **profit**: "Suhayb has profited" (ibnhisham:1:477).

Ibn Ishaq reads Q 2:207 as a sale made by the dead of al-Raji'.

**ibnhisham:2:175** (from Ibn Ishaq, glossing 2:207 on the party killed at al-Raji'; then Ibn Hisham; also Guillaume p. 429)

> ومن الناس من يشري نفسه ابتغاء مرضات الله، والله رؤف بالعباد 2: 206- 207: أي قد شروا أنفسهم من الله بالجهاد في سبيله، والقيام بحقه، حتى هلكوا على ذلك، يعني تلك السرية. ### || (تفسير ابن هشام لبعض الغريب) : قال ابن هشام: يشري نفسه: يبيع نفسه، وشروا: باعوا.

"And of the people is he who sells (yashri) himself, seeking the good pleasure of God; and God is kind to the servants" (2:206-207): that is, they have sold their selves to God by jihad in His way, and standing by His right, until they perished on that; meaning that raiding party. Ibn Hisham said: yashri nafsahu: he sells himself; and sharaw: they sold.

Loss on earth is paid in kind in the Garden:
- a house for a house (ibnhisham:1:499);
- a palm for a palm (waqidi:1:281);
- a vine for each vine cut at al-Ta'if: "Whoever cuts a vine has a vine in the Garden" (waqidi:3:928).

#### Conditions outside battle

**The testimony and the pillars.**

**ibnhisham:2:574** (from Ibn 'Abbas, on Dimam b. Tha'laba of the Banu Sa'd b. Bakr; also Guillaume p. 635; cf. muslim:12; bukhari:63)

> فإني أشهد أن لا إله إلا الله، وأشهد أن محمدا رسول الله، وسأؤدي هذه الفرائض، وأجتنب ما نهيتني عنه، ثم لا أزيد ولا أنقص. ثم انصرف إلى بعيره راجعا. قال: فقال رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم: إن صدق ذو العقيصتين دخل الجنة.

"I testify that there is no god but God, and I testify that Muhammad is the Messenger of God; and I will perform these obligations, and avoid what you forbade me; then I will not add and not take away." Then he went back to his camel, returning. He said: The Messenger of God said: "If the man of the two plaits speaks truly, he enters the Garden."

**ibnhisham:2:590** (from the Messenger of God, instructing Mu'adh b. Jabal for the Yemen (from 'Abd Allah b. Abi Bakr); also Guillaume p. 643; cf. bukhari:1395, 1458; muslim:19)

> وإنك ستقدم على قوم من أهل الكتاب، يسئلونك ما مفتاح الجنة، فقل: شهادة أن لا إله إلا الله وحده لا شريك له

"And you will come to a people of the People of the Book who will ask you what the key of the Garden is. So say: The testimony that there is no god but God alone; He has no partner"

**Emigration** brings the house in the Garden (ibnhisham:1:499). Ibn Sa'd has the question "The Garden, none enters it but whoever emigrated", answered "There is no emigration (hijra) after the conquest of Mecca, but jihad and intention" (ibnsad:6:113).

**Patience** under persecution brings the Garden for the family of Yasir (ibnhisham:1:320). 'Uthman b. Maz'un, struck in the eye, held that "the bliss of the Garden does not cease" (ibnhisham:1:370).

**The bar** was proclaimed at the pilgrimage of 9 AH: "no unbeliever enters the Garden" (ibnhisham:2:545).

At the end, the Prophet is offered the world with the Garden after it.

**ibnhisham:2:642** (from Abu Muwayhiba, freedman of the Messenger of God, at the cemetery of al-Baqi' before the last illness; also Guillaume p. 678)

> يا أبا مويهبة، إني قد أوتيت مفاتيح خزائن الدنيا والخلد فيها، ثم الجنة، فخيرت بين ذلك وبين لقاء ربي والجنة. قال: فقلت: بأبي أنت وأمي، فخذ مفاتيح خزائن الدنيا والخلد فيها، ثم الجنة، قال: لا والله يا أبا مويهبة، لقد اخترت لقاء ربي والجنة

"O Abu Muwayhiba, I have been given the keys of the treasuries of the world and eternity in it, then the Garden; and I was given the choice between that and the meeting with my Lord and the Garden." I said: "By my father and my mother, take the keys of the treasuries of the world and eternity in it, then the Garden." He said: "No, by God, O Abu Muwayhiba; I have chosen the meeting with my Lord and the Garden"

#### The pattern in the Sira

**The base is the one the Qur'an and the Hadith give.** The testimony and the pillars bring the Garden, and the unbeliever is barred.

**The Sira adds the occasion.** The Garden is named when men were asked to give something: protection and war at 'Aqaba, a house left in Mecca, wealth paid to Quraysh, a life in battle. The Medinans ask the price, and the answer is the Garden.

**Other rewards are named beside it:**
- dominion over the Arabs (ibnhisham:1:417; ibnsad:1:184);
- victory (ibnsad:3:563);
- spoils, at Hunayn and al-Hudaybiyya (ibnhisham:2:448, 2:321).

The succession is not promised (ibnhisham:1:424).

**The battles are where the promise is made most often, and where it is denied.** It is denied to the one who fought for his people's honour, to the one who stole from the booty, and to the disobedient.

**The three works differ in detail.** Ibn Hisham does not name the man who cried "I have won" at Bi'r Ma'una, and the others name two men. Only Ibn Sa'd has "the scent of the Garden" at Uhud. Ibn Hisham names no Garden for the tribes, for Ridwan or for Hunayn, where one of the other two does.

### 2.14 Fear, hope and the guarantee: a claim checked

**The claim, as heard.** The author has heard Muslims say three things about the Garden:
1. entering it is *like walking on the edge of a knife*;
2. it is ultimately *upon the will of God*;
3. only certain ways are guaranteed, chiefly dying to bring Islam or in conquest, that is, death in jihad.

Each part was tested against the Qur'an, the Hadith swept in 2.9 to 2.12, the creeds and modern fatwas. The sources, editions and doubts are listed in `ext-ref/religion_ends/hadith_claim_check.md`.

| Part | Verdict | Main texts |
|---|---|---|
| 1. The edge of a knife | Corroborated with qualifications | The bridge: Qur'an 19:71 to 72; Bukhari 7439, 806; Muslim 183, 195. The ending: Muslim 2643; Bukhari 6493. Fear held with hope: Tirmidhi 983; al-Tahawi arts. 59 to 60; al-Ghazali. The wording: Abu Sa'id in Muslim 183; the reports gathered by al-Sakhawi |
| 2. Upon the will of God | Corroborated with qualifications | Qur'an 2:284, 3:129, 48:14, 76:31; Abu Dawud 1420; Bukhari 5673; al-Tahawi arts. 68 and 83; al-Nasafi; the al-Azhar textbooks. Dissent: the Mu'tazili 'Abd al-Jabbar; the Twelver commentaries on al-Hilli |
| 3. Only certain ways, chiefly jihad | Partly corroborated | The guarantee: Qur'an 9:111; Muslim 1876; Bukhari 2787; Tirmidhi 1663. Other guarantees: Bukhari 2790, 6474, 1237; Abu Dawud 1420, 2494, 4800; Ibn Majah 1837. The conditions: Bukhari 2810; Muslim 1905, 1885; Bukhari's chapter on naming a martyr |

#### Part 1: the edge of a knife

**The bridge.** The Qur'an says that every one comes down to the Fire:

**quran:19:71**

> وَإِنْ مِنْكُمْ إِلَّا وَارِدُهَا ۚ كَانَ عَلَىٰ رَبِّكَ حَتْمًا مَقْضِيًّا

And there is none of you but [is] coming down to it (wariduha); [that] was, upon your Lord, a fixed [decree], decided.

The next verse reads: "Then We will save those who feared [God], and leave the wrongdoers in it, kneeling (jithiyya)." The al-Azhar textbook takes the coming down as the crossing of the bridge (al-sirat): "the most evident [view] is that it is the bridge; so all creatures come down to Hell by passing over the bridge, even the prophets, and the martyrs and the righteous; and these are saved, and the hooks seize the disobedient and throw them into the Fire" (third secondary year, p. 75).

The two Sahihs give the bridge in the Prophet's words. In Abu Sa'id's report it is slippery and hooked:

**Bukhari 7439** (from Abu Sa'id al-Khudri; also muslim:183)

> مَدْحَضَةٌ مَزِلَّةٌ، عَلَيْهِ خَطَاطِيفُ وَكَلاَلِيبُ وَحَسَكَةٌ مُفَلْطَحَةٌ، لَهَا شَوْكَةٌ عُقَيْفَاءُ تَكُونُ بِنَجْدٍ يُقَالُ لَهَا السَّعْدَانُ، الْمُؤْمِنُ عَلَيْهَا كَالطَّرْفِ وَكَالْبَرْقِ وَكَالرِّيحِ وَكَأَجَاوِيدِ الْخَيْلِ وَالرِّكَابِ، فَنَاجٍ مُسَلَّمٌ وَنَاجٍ مَخْدُوشٌ وَمَكْدُوسٌ فِي نَارِ جَهَنَّمَ، حَتَّى يَمُرَّ آخِرُهُمْ يُسْحَبُ سَحْبًا

[We said: "O Messenger of God, and what is the bridge?" He said:] "A slippery [place], a place of slipping (madhada mazilla); on it [are] hooks and grapnels, and a broad spiked [thing] with a bent thorn, found in Najd, called al-sa'dan. The believer [passes] over it like the glance [of an eye], and like lightning, and like the wind, and like the finest horses and riding camels. So [one is] saved unharmed, and [one is] saved scratched, and [one is] heaped up in the Fire of Hell, until the last of them passes, dragged along."

Abu Hurayra's report says of the hooks: "they snatch the people according to their deeds; of them is one who is destroyed (yubaq) by his deed, and of them one who is cut to pieces (yukhardal), then is saved" (Bukhari 806). In the report of Abu Hurayra and Hudhayfa the speed is set by deeds, and the deeds give out:

**Muslim 195** (from Abu Hurayra and Hudhayfa)

> ثُمَّ كَمَرِّ الرِّيحِ ثُمَّ كَمَرِّ الطَّيْرِ وَشَدِّ الرِّجَالِ تَجْرِي بِهِمْ أَعْمَالُهُمْ وَنَبِيُّكُمْ قَائِمٌ عَلَى الصِّرَاطِ يَقُولُ رَبِّ سَلِّمْ سَلِّمْ حَتَّى تَعْجِزَ أَعْمَالُ الْعِبَادِ حَتَّى يَجِيءَ الرَّجُلُ فَلاَ يَسْتَطِيعُ السَّيْرَ إِلاَّ زَحْفًا - قَالَ - وَفِي حَافَتَىِ الصِّرَاطِ كَلاَلِيبُ مُعَلَّقَةٌ مَأْمُورَةٌ بِأَخْذِ مَنْ أُمِرَتْ بِهِ فَمَخْدُوشٌ نَاجٍ وَمَكْدُوسٌ فِي النَّارِ

[The first of you passes like lightning,] "then like the passing of the wind, then like the passing of the bird and the running of men, their deeds carrying them along; and your Prophet stands on the bridge (al-sirat), saying: Lord, keep safe, keep safe; until the deeds of the servants fail, until the man comes who cannot travel except by crawling." He said: "And on the two edges of the bridge are hooks hung, commanded to seize whomever they are commanded [to seize]; so [one is] scratched [and] saved, and [one is] heaped up in the Fire."

**The wording.** Neither Sahih gives a bridge "thinner than the hair" in the Prophet's own words. In Muslim the words come after the Prophet's words end, in the chain of al-Layth, which Muslim says he read to 'Isa b. Hammad. They are Abu Sa'id's, and he gives them as what reached him:

**Muslim 183** (from Abu Sa'id al-Khudri, in his own words)

> قَالَ أَبُو سَعِيدٍ بَلَغَنِي أَنَّ الْجِسْرَ أَدَقُّ مِنَ الشَّعْرَةِ وَأَحَدُّ مِنَ السَّيْفِ

Abu Sa'id said: It has reached me (balaghani) that the bridge is thinner than the hair and sharper than the sword.

Reports that carry such words back to the Prophet lie outside the two Sahihs. Al-Sakhawi (d. 902) gathered them and judged that their routes "strengthen one another". IslamQA quotes him:

**islamqa:249781** (from al-Sakhawi (d. 902), al-Ajwiba al-mardiyya 3/905-907, as quoted in IslamQA answer 249781 (27 Dhu al-Hijja 1446 / 23 June 2025))

> فأخرج البيهقي وابن المبارك وابن أبي الدنيا وغيرهم جميعًا من حديث عبيد بن عمير رفعه مرسلاً: (الصراط على جهنم مثل حرف السيف)، وكذا أخرج البيهقي وشيخه الحاكم عن ابن مسعود مرفوعًا: (والصراط كحد السيف) والبيهقي وحده من حديث زياد النميري عن أنس مرفوعًا: (الصراط كحد الشعرة أو حد السيف). ومن حديث يزيد الرقاشي عن أنس رفعه أيضًا: (إنه أدق من الشعرة، أحد من السيف) ولأبي يعلي وابن منيع في مسنديهما عن أبي هريرة مرفوعًا: (الصراط كحد السيف). ولأحمد بن حنبل في مسنده من حديث القاسم عن عائشة في حديث مرفوع ، أصله عند أبي داود من حديث الحسن البصري عنها: (ولجهنم جسر، أدق من الشعر، وأحد من السيف)، ولابن أبي الدنيا من حديث رجل من كندة عنها مرفوعًا في حديث: (أن الصراط يستحد حتى يكون مثل شفرة السيف، ويسجر حتى يكون مثل الجمرة). ومن حديث أبي عثمان النهدي عن سلمان الفارسي أنه قال: (يوضع الصراط يوم القيامة وله كحد الموس)، وحكمه الرفع

al-Bayhaqi, Ibn al-Mubarak, Ibn Abi al-Dunya and others all transmitted, from the report of 'Ubayd b. 'Umayr, raised [to the Prophet] in a mursal form: (The bridge over Hell is like the edge (harf) of the sword). Likewise al-Bayhaqi and his teacher al-Hakim, from Ibn Mas'ud, raised: (And the bridge is like the edge (hadd) of the sword). And al-Bayhaqi alone, from the report of Ziyad al-Numayri from Anas, raised: (The bridge is like the edge of the hair or the edge of the sword). And from the report of Yazid al-Raqashi from Anas, raised also: (It is thinner than the hair, sharper than the sword). And Abu Ya'la and Ibn Mani' in their Musnads, from Abu Hurayra, raised: (The bridge is like the edge of the sword). And Ahmad b. Hanbal in his Musnad, from the report of al-Qasim from 'A'isha, in a raised report whose origin is with Abu Dawud from the report of al-Hasan al-Basri from her: (And Hell has a bridge, thinner than the hair and sharper than the sword). And Ibn Abi al-Dunya, from the report of a man of Kinda from her, raised, in a report: (that the bridge is sharpened until it is like the blade (shafra) of the sword, and is heated until it is like the ember). And from the report of Abu 'Uthman al-Nahdi from Salman al-Farisi, that he said: (The bridge is set up on the Day of Resurrection, and it has [an edge] like the edge of the razor (al-mus)); and its ruling is [that of] a raised [report].

- The nearest wording to a knife is Salman's "edge of the razor".
- IslamQA's English version of the same answer renders Abu Sa'id's words: "I heard that the bridge is narrower than a hair and sharper than a knife".
- Al-Ghazali describes the bridge: "and it is a bridge stretched over the back of the Fire, sharper than the sword and thinner than the hair; so whoever was upright in this world on the straight path is light on the path of the hereafter, and is saved" (Ihya 4:524). He quotes Anas without a chain: "The bridge is like the edge of the sword, or like the edge of the hair." (4:525).
- A Twelver report gives the words to the Prophet, from Gabriel. Al-Majlisi grades it weak:

**kafi:8:312:486** (from the Prophet, from Gabriel (from Abu Ja'far [Muhammad al-Baqir]); grade: al-Majlisi: ضعيف (weak); Mirʾāt al-ʿUqūl fī Sharḥ Akhbār Āl al-Rasūl (16/410))

> ثُمَّ يُوضَعُ عَلَيْهَا صِرَاطٌ أَدَقُّ مِنَ الشَّعْرِ وَ أَحَدُّ مِنَ السَّيْفِ عَلَيْهِ ثَلَاثُ قَنَاطِرَ الْأُولَى عَلَيْهَا الْأَمَانَةُ وَ الرَّحْمَةُ وَ الثَّانِيَةُ عَلَيْهَا الصَّلَاةُ وَ الثَّالِثَةُ عَلَيْهَا رَبُّ الْعَالَمِينَ

Then there is set over it a bridge (sirat) thinner than the hair and sharper than the sword; on it [are] three arches (qanatir): the first, on it [are] the trust (al-amana) and the mercy (al-rahma); and the second, on it [is] the prayer; and the third, on it [is] the Lord of the worlds.

No Arabic report in the sources read has the word *knife* (sikkin).

**The ending.** The texts make the outcome turn on the last state of a life:
- "By Him besides whom there is no god, one of you does the deed of the people of the Garden until there is not between him and it but an arm's length, and the writing outstrips him, and he does the deed of the people of the Fire, and enters it; and one of you does the deed of the people of the Fire until there is not between him and it but an arm's length, and the writing outstrips him, and he does the deed of the people of the Garden, and enters it." (Muslim 2643);
- "The servant does, in what the people see, the deed of the people of the Garden, and he is of the people of the Fire; and he does, in what the people see, the deed of the people of the Fire, and he is of the people of the Garden; and deeds are only by their endings." (Bukhari 6493);
- "The Garden is surrounded by the things disliked (al-makarih), and the Fire is surrounded by the desires." (Muslim 2822);
- for the Prophet too: "His deed will never admit anyone to the Garden." They said: "Not even you, O Messenger of God?" He said: "No, not even I, unless God covers me with bounty and mercy. So aim right and come near." (Bukhari 5673).

Al-Nasafi states it as creed: "the happy one may become wretched, and the wretched may become happy" (p. 196). The fear reaches the doers of good. Qur'an 23:60 praises those who give "while their hearts are afraid", and 'A'isha asked who they are:

**Tirmidhi 3175** (from 'A'isha; grade: Ahmad Muhammad Shakir: Sahih; Al-Albani: Sahih; Zubair Ali Zai: Hasan)

> لاَ يَا بِنْتَ الصِّدِّيقِ وَلَكِنَّهُمُ الَّذِينَ يَصُومُونَ وَيُصَلُّونَ وَيَتَصَدَّقُونَ وَهُمْ يَخَافُونَ أَنْ لاَ يُقْبَلَ مِنْهُمْ أُولَئِكَ الَّذِينَ يُسَارِعُونَ فِي الْخَيْرَاتِ وَهُمْ لَهَا سَابِقُونَ

['A'isha asked about the verse "And those who give what they give while their hearts are afraid" (23:60): "Are they those who drink wine and steal?" He said:] "No, O daughter of al-Siddiq; rather they are those who fast and pray and give alms, while they fear that it will not be accepted from them. Those hasten in the good things, and they are foremost in them" (23:61).

The takhrij notes printed in the Ihya judge the chain otherwise: "al-Tirmidhi, Ibn Majah and al-Hakim transmitted it, and he [al-Hakim] said: sound in chain. I say: rather it is broken between 'A'isha and 'Abd al-Rahman b. Sa'd b. Wahb" (Ihya 4:162).

**Fear held with hope.** The texts set the fear beside a hope of equal weight:
- Qur'an 32:16: "Their sides forsake [their] beds; they call upon their Lord in fear and hope (khawfan wa-tama'an), and from what We provided them they spend.";
- of those whom the polytheists call upon: "Those [whom] they call upon seek the means [of access] (al-wasila) to their Lord, which of them [is] nearest; and they hope for His mercy and fear His punishment. Indeed the punishment of your Lord is ever [a thing] to be feared." (17:57);
- Bukhari 7405: "God, exalted, says: I am at My servant's thought (zann) of Me, and I am with him when he remembers Me.";
- the Twelver al-Kafi: "there is no believing servant but in his heart [are] two lights, a light of fear and a light of hope; if this were weighed it would not exceed this, and if this were weighed it would not exceed this" (2:71:13, good by al-Majlisi).

At death the two are joined:

**Tirmidhi 983** (from Anas b. Malik; grade: Ahmad Muhammad Shakir: Hasan; Al-Albani: Hasan; Zubair Ali Zai: Isnaad Hasan; Tirmidhi: هَذَا حَدِيثٌ غَرِيبٌ "this is a hadith gharib")

> لاَ يَجْتَمِعَانِ فِي قَلْبِ عَبْدٍ فِي مِثْلِ هَذَا الْمَوْطِنِ إِلاَّ أَعْطَاهُ اللَّهُ مَا يَرْجُو وَآمَنَهُ مِمَّا يَخَافُ

[A young man at the point of death said: "By God, O Messenger of God, I hope in God and I fear my sins." The Messenger of God said:] "The two do not come together in the heart of a servant in such a place as this, but God gives him what he hopes and makes him safe from what he fears."

Al-Tahawi makes the balance an article of creed:

**tahawi:59** (from al-Tahawi, al-'Aqida al-Tahawiyya, art. 59 (ed. al-Albani, Beirut: al-Maktab al-Islami, 1398/1978, p. 41))

> نرجو للمحسنين من المؤمنين أن يعفو عنهم ويدخلهم الجنة برحمته ولا نأمن عليهم ولا نشهد لهم بالجنة ونستغفر لمسيئهم ونخاف عليهم ولا نقنطهم

We hope for the doers of good among the believers that He will pardon them and admit them to the Garden by His mercy; and we do not feel secure for them, and we do not testify for them to the Garden. And we ask forgiveness for their wrongdoers, and we fear for them, and we do not make them despair.

His next article: "security and despair [both] carry [one] out of the religion of Islam, and the path of truth for the people of the qibla is between them" (art. 60, p. 42). Al-Nasafi has the same rule: "despair is unbelief, and security from the plan (makr) of God, exalted, is unbelief" (p. 198).

Al-Ghazali opens his book of fear and hope: "hope and fear are two wings by which those brought near fly to every praised station" (Ihya 4:142). He writes that "fear and hope are two remedies by which hearts are treated, and the merit of each is according to the illness present" (4:164). He gives 'Umar's saying as their balance:

**ghazali:ihya:4:165** (from 'Umar, as quoted without a chain by al-Ghazali, Ihya' 'ulum al-din, Kitab al-khawf wa-l-raja', 4:165)

> لو نودي ليدخل النار كل الناس إلا رجلا واحدا لرجوت أن أكون أنا ذلك الرجل ولو نودي ليدخل الجنة كل الناس إلا رجلا واحدا لخشيت أن أكون أنا ذلك الرجل وهذا عبارة عن غاية الخوف والرجاء واعتدالهما

['Umar said:] If it were called out, Let all the people enter the Fire except one man, I would hope to be that man; and if it were called out, Let all the people enter the Garden except one man, I would fear to be that man. [al-Ghazali:] And this expresses the utmost of fear and hope and their balance.

IslamQA answer 228924 gives the modern form: "The believer does not feel safe from the plan of Allah until he enters Paradise". It quotes Ibn Baz, that the Muslim "should be in a state between hope and fear". Ahmad b. Hanbal, asked when a man may feel secure, answered: "With the first step he takes into Paradise".

**The floor.** The texts that draw the bridge so narrow also set a low floor. The testimony brings the Garden: "[Someone came to me from my Lord and told me] that whoever of my community dies not associating anything with God enters the Garden. [Abu Dharr asked: And if he committed adultery and if he stole? He said: And if he committed adultery and if he stole.]" (Bukhari 1237). Whoever has an atom's weight of faith comes out of the Fire (2.9). The al-Azhar textbook reads the narrowness by the deeds of the one crossing: "the disobedient find the crossing hard, until the path appears before them narrow [and] sharp; and the righteous cross by a bounty from God, and see the path before them wide [and] easy" (p. 74).

#### Part 2: upon the will of God

**The Qur'an.** Forgiveness and punishment are stated by God's will in 2:284, 3:129, 5:18 and 48:14:

**quran:3:129**

> وَلِلَّهِ مَا فِي السَّمَاوَاتِ وَمَا فِي الْأَرْضِ ۚ يَغْفِرُ لِمَنْ يَشَاءُ وَيُعَذِّبُ مَنْ يَشَاءُ ۚ وَاللَّهُ غَفُورٌ رَحِيمٌ

And to God [belongs] what is in the heavens and what is in the earth. He forgives whom He wills and punishes whom He wills; and God is Forgiving, Merciful.

Of the Garden itself: "He admits whom He wills into His mercy; and the wrongdoers, He has prepared for them a painful punishment." (76:31). Of the sinner: "Indeed God does not forgive that [anything] be associated with Him, and He forgives what is below that for whom He wills. And whoever associates [partners] with God has fabricated a great sin." (4:48).

**The Hadith.** Abu Dawud 1420 puts the one who neglects the prayers in the will: "Five prayers God has written upon the servants. Whoever comes with them, not having lost anything of them from making light of their due, has with God a covenant that He will admit him to the Garden. And whoever does not come with them has no covenant with God: if He wills, He punishes him, and if He wills, He admits him to the Garden." In Bukhari 5673, quoted above, entry is by mercy, for the Prophet too. In Bukhari 4949 every seat in the Garden or the Fire is already written, and the command is still to work.

**The creeds.** Al-Tahawi puts the sinner of the community in the will:

**tahawi:68** (from al-Tahawi, al-'Aqida al-Tahawiyya, art. 68 (p. 45))

> وأهل الكبائر من أمة محمد صلى الله عليه وسلم في النار لا يخلدون إذا ماتوا وهم موحدون وإن لم يكونوا تائبين بعد أن لقوا الله عارفين مؤمنين وهم في مشيئته وحكمه إن شاء غفر لهم وعفا عنهم بفضله كما ذكر عز وجل في كتابه ويغفر ما دون ذلك لمن يشاء وإن شاء عذبهم في النار بعدله ثم يخرجهم منها برحمته وشفاعة الشافعين من أهل طاعته ثم يبعثهم إلى جنته

And the people of the great [sins] of the community of Muhammad are in the Fire, [but] do not abide forever, if they die professing [God's] oneness, even if they have not repented, after they have met God knowing [and] believing. And they are in His will (mashi'a) and His judgment: if He wills, He forgives them and pardons them by His bounty, as He, mighty and majestic, mentioned in His Book: And He forgives what is below that for whom He wills (4:48); and if He wills, He punishes them in the Fire by His justice, then brings them out of it by His mercy and the intercession of the intercessors among the people of His obedience, then sends them to His Garden.

Article 83 gives the ground of each end: "so whom He wills of them [He sends] to the Garden, as bounty from Him, and whom He wills of them to the Fire, as justice from Him" (p. 51). Al-Nasafi: "what is best (al-aslah) for the servant is not obligatory upon God, exalted" (p. 195). The al-Azhar textbook teaches al-Laqani's verse:

**azhar:y2:10** (from al-Laqani, Jawharat al-tawhid, as taught in al-Azhar, Taysir sharh Jawharat al-tawhid, 2nd secondary year (2019-2020), p. 10)

> فإن يثبنا فبمحض الفضل *** وإن يعذب فبمحض العدل

If He rewards us, then by pure bounty; and if He punishes, then by pure justice.

The textbook states the Mu'tazili rule as "the best is obligatory upon God; for doing the good and the best is what agrees with describing God by wisdom and justice" (second year, p. 23). It answers: "if an act or an omission were obligatory upon Him, He would not be choosing; for the one who chooses is he who, if he wills, acts, and if he wills, refrains" (p. 24). Of the major sinner it teaches that "whoever dies before repenting of the great [sins], his matter is entrusted to God: if He wills, He forgives him, and if He wills, He admits him to the Fire without abiding forever in it" (third year, p. 65).

**The promise.** The same texts bound the will by a promise. Qur'an 9:111 calls the Garden "a promise [binding] upon Him, true". Bukhari 2790 calls entry "a right upon God". The al-Azhar textbook: "the scholars of the Ash'aris and the Maturidis are agreed that His promise, exalted is He, to the believers of the Garden as an abode of reward is a promise that does not fail, by His bounty and His mercy" (second year, p. 15). In these texts the promise to the class of believers holds. What stays open is who dies a believer, and whether a sinner is punished before he enters.

**The dissent.** The Mu'tazila held reward to be owed in justice:

**jabbar:415** (from Qadi 'Abd al-Jabbar (Mu'tazili, d. 415), Sharh al-usul al-khamsa (Beirut: Dar Ihya' al-Turath al-'Arabi, 1422), pp. 415 to 416)

> فاعلم أنه تعالى إذا كلفنا الأفعال الشاقة فلا بد من أن يكون في مقابلها من الثواب ما يقابله ، بل لا يكفي هذا القدر حتى يبلغ في الكثرة حدا لا يجوز الابتداء بمثله ولا التفضل به ، وإلا كان لا يحسن التكليف لأجله. وإنما قلنا : إن هذا هكذا ، لأنه لو لم يكن في مقابلة هذه الأفعال الشاقة ما ذكرناه ، كان يكون القديم تعالى ظالما عابثا

Know that when He, exalted, charges us with hard acts, there must be, in return for them, reward that matches them; indeed this amount is not enough, until it reaches in abundance a limit such that its like may not be given unprompted (ibtida'an) nor as a favor (tafaddulan); otherwise the charge (al-taklif) would not be good for its sake. We said this is so because, if there were not in return for these hard acts what we have mentioned, the Eternal, exalted, would be unjust, acting in vain.

The Twelver commentaries on al-Hilli's creed say the same:

**arabshahi:215** (from Abu al-Fath b. Makhdum al-Husayni al-'Arabshahi (Twelver, d. 976), Miftah al-bab, commentary on al-'Allama al-Hilli's al-Bab al-hadi 'ashar, ed. Mahdi Muhaqqiq (Mashhad: Astan-i Quds, 2nd ed., 1370 Sh), p. 215)

> فقالت الأشاعرة انه سمعى. وقال أهل الحق الثواب واجب عقلا وسمعا ، أما عقلا فلما عرفت من أن انتفائه يستلزم الظلم ولاشتماله على اللطف

So the Ash'aris said it [is known] by revelation. And the people of truth said: reward is obligatory by reason and by revelation; by reason, because, as you have learned, its absence entails injustice (al-zulm), and because it contains grace (al-lutf).

Al-Suyuri gives the reward as a right: "what must be restored [at the resurrection] is of two kinds: one of them must be restored by reason and by revelation, and it is everyone who has a right (haqq) to reward or compensation, so that his right may reach him" (p. 53). The Twelver hadith still leave the sinner to the will. In al-Kafi 2:385:7 (2.12) no believer enters the Fire "except whom God wills".

#### Part 3: the guaranteed ways

**The guarantee for jihad.** Qur'an 9:111 states the Garden as the price of the believers' selves. In Muslim 1876 God says of the one who goes out in His way: "he is upon Me a guarantee, that I admit him to the Garden, or return him to his dwelling". Bukhari 2787 joins the same guarantee to a proviso:

**Bukhari 2787** (from Abu Hurayra; also bukhari:36, 3123; muslim:1876)

> مَثَلُ الْمُجَاهِدِ فِي سَبِيلِ اللَّهِ ـ وَاللَّهُ أَعْلَمُ بِمَنْ يُجَاهِدُ فِي سَبِيلِهِ ـ كَمَثَلِ الصَّائِمِ الْقَائِمِ، وَتَوَكَّلَ اللَّهُ لِلْمُجَاهِدِ فِي سَبِيلِهِ بِأَنْ يَتَوَفَّاهُ أَنْ يُدْخِلَهُ الْجَنَّةَ، أَوْ يَرْجِعَهُ سَالِمًا مَعَ أَجْرٍ أَوْ غَنِيمَةٍ

"The likeness of the striver (mujahid) in the way of God (and God knows best who strives in His way) is as the likeness of the one who fasts [and] stands [in prayer]. And God has taken it upon Himself (tawakkala), for the striver in His way, that if He takes him [in death] He admits him to the Garden, or returns him safe with reward or booty."

The martyr "is forgiven at the first gush" (Tirmidhi 1663). He is spared the trial of the grave: [A man said: "O Messenger of God,] what is [the matter with] the believers, [that] they are tried in their graves, except the martyr (shahid)?" He said: "The flash of the swords over his head suffices as a trial." (Nasa'i 2053). The Twelver al-Kafi: "Above every possessor of righteousness is [a further] righteousness, until he is killed in the way of God; and when he is killed in the way of God, there is no righteousness above him." (5:53:2, weak).

The guarantee covers the one who returns alive with reward or booty, as well as the one who dies.

Al-Ghazali ties martyrdom to the fear of the end: "for the sake of this great danger, martyrdom was envied, and sudden death was disliked" (Ihya 4:179). The danger is the evil ending (su' al-khatima), and he explains how martyrdom meets it:

**ghazali:ihya:4:179** (from al-Ghazali, Ihya' 'ulum al-din, Kitab al-khawf wa-l-raja', section on the evil ending, 4:179)

> وأما الشهادة فلأنها عبارة عن قبض الروح في حالة لم يبق في القلب سوى حب الله تعالى وخرج حب الدنيا والأهل والمال والولد وجميع الشهوات عن القلب إذ لا يهجم على صف القتال موطنا نفسه على الموت إلا حبا لله وطلبا لمرضاته وبائعا دنياه بآخرته وراضيا بالبيع الذي بايعه الله به إذ قال تعالى إن الله اشترى من المؤمنين أنفسهم وأموالهم بأن لهم الجنة والبائع راغب عن المبيع لا محالة ومخرج حبه عن القلب ومجرد حب العوض المطلوب في قلبه ومثل هذه الحالة قد يغلب على القلب في بعض الأحوال ولكن لا يتفق زهوق الروح فيها فصف القتال سبب لزهوق الروح على مثل هذه الحالة هذا فيمن ليس يقصد الغلبة والغنيمة وحسن الصيت بالشجاعة فإن من هذا حاله وأن قتل في المعركة فهو بعيد عن مثل هذه الرتبة

As for martyrdom, it is the taking of the spirit in a state in which nothing is left in the heart but the love of God, exalted, and the love of the world and family and wealth and children and all the desires has gone out of the heart; for no one rushes upon the battle line, resolved on death, except for love of God and seeking His good pleasure, selling his world for his hereafter, and content with the sale by which God bought from him, when He, exalted, said: Indeed God has bought from the believers their selves and their wealth, for that the Garden is theirs (9:111). And the seller is turned away from the thing sold, without doubt, and puts its love out of his heart, and keeps in his heart only the love of the price sought. A state like this may prevail over the heart at some times, but the spirit does not happen to depart in it; so the battle line is a cause of the spirit's departing in such a state. This is for one who does not aim at victory and booty and a good name for bravery; for whoever's state is this, even if he is killed in the battle, is far from such a rank.

**Not the only way.** The same words of guarantee are used of other deeds:
- Bukhari 2790: for faith, prayer and the fast "it was a right upon God that He admit him to the Garden, [whether] he strove in the way of God or sat in his land in which he was born". The same report adds "a hundred degrees which God prepared for those who strive in the way of God".
- Abu Dawud 2494: three are "in the charge of God, mighty and majestic": the raider, "a man who went to the mosque", and "a man who entered his house with [a greeting of] peace".
- Bukhari 6474: "Whoever guarantees to me what is between his two jaws and what is between his two legs, I guarantee to him the Garden."
- Abu Dawud 4800: "I am a guarantor of a house in the outskirts (rabad) of the Garden for whoever leaves disputing, even if he is in the right", with higher houses for leaving lies and for good character.
- Ibn Majah 1837: "Who will take upon himself for me one [thing], and I take upon myself for him the Garden?" The thing is "Do not ask people for anything."
- Abu Dawud 1420: the prayers bring "a covenant that He will admit him to the Garden".
- Bukhari 1237, above: the testimony.

Jihad is the largest category in the Sahihs (23 reports, 2.9), and it carries the highest degrees (Bukhari 2790; Qur'an 4:95). It is one guaranteed way among several. Al-Ghazali sets a limit to its rank: "martyrdom was a merit relative to what is below it, not relative to the degree of the God-fearing and the truthful (al-siddiqin)" (4:158).

**The conditions.** Each guarantee to the fighter is bound to conditions that only God sees. The first is intention. Bukhari 2810 defines the way of God by the fighter's aim:

**Bukhari 2810** (from Abu Musa al-Ash'ari; also muslim:1904)

> مَنْ قَاتَلَ لِتَكُونَ كَلِمَةُ اللَّهِ هِيَ الْعُلْيَا فَهُوَ فِي سَبِيلِ اللَّهِ

[A man came to the Prophet and said: "A man fights for booty, and a man fights to be remembered, and a man fights that his place be seen; so who is in the way of God?" He said:] "Whoever fights so that the word of God is the highest, he is in the way of God."

The second is the motive of show:

**Muslim 1905** (from Abu Hurayra)

> إِنَّ أَوَّلَ النَّاسِ يُقْضَى يَوْمَ الْقِيَامَةِ عَلَيْهِ رَجُلٌ اسْتُشْهِدَ فَأُتِيَ بِهِ فَعَرَّفَهُ نِعَمَهُ فَعَرَفَهَا قَالَ فَمَا عَمِلْتَ فِيهَا قَالَ قَاتَلْتُ فِيكَ حَتَّى اسْتُشْهِدْتُ ‏.‏ قَالَ كَذَبْتَ وَلَكِنَّكَ قَاتَلْتَ لأَنْ يُقَالَ جَرِيءٌ ‏.‏ فَقَدْ قِيلَ ‏.‏ ثُمَّ أُمِرَ بِهِ فَسُحِبَ عَلَى وَجْهِهِ حَتَّى أُلْقِيَ فِي النَّارِ

"The first of the people on whom judgment is given on the Day of Resurrection is a man who was martyred (ustushhida). He is brought, and He makes His favors known to him, and he knows them. He says: What did you do with them? He says: I fought for You until I was martyred. He says: You lied; rather you fought so that it would be said, [He is] bold (jari'); and it has been said. Then he is ordered, and dragged on his face until he is thrown into the Fire."

The third is the debt:

**Muslim 1885** (from Abu Qatada; also muslim:1886)

> نَعَمْ وَأَنْتَ صَابِرٌ مُحْتَسِبٌ مُقْبِلٌ غَيْرُ مُدْبِرٍ إِلاَّ الدَّيْنَ فَإِنَّ جِبْرِيلَ عَلَيْهِ السَّلاَمُ قَالَ لِي ذَلِكَ

[A man asked: "Tell me, if I am killed in the way of God, are my sins expiated from me?" He said:] "Yes, while you are patient, seeking [the reward] (muhtasib), advancing, not retreating; except the debt, for Gabriel told me that."

Muslim 1886: "For the martyr every sin is forgiven, except the debt." Ibn Baz adds that "the owner of the debt does not lose his right" (fatwa 10775).

**No martyr named.** Al-Bukhari heads a chapter against naming one:

**tarjama:bukhari:2898** (from al-Bukhari, Sahih, Kitab al-jihad wa-l-siyar, chapter heading before no. 2898 (ed. Zuhayr al-Nasir, Dar Tawq al-Najat 1422, 4:37))

> باب لا يقول فلان شهيد قال أبو هريرة، عن النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم: «الله أعلم بمن يجاهد في سبيله، والله أعلم بمن يكلم في سبيله

Chapter: one does not say, So-and-so is a martyr (shahid). Abu Hurayra said, from the Prophet: God knows best who strives in His way, and God knows best who is wounded in His way.

Its report is of the man who fought best that day. The Prophet said: "Lo, he is of the people of the Fire." The man then killed himself on his sword (Bukhari 2898). Ibn Hajar glosses the heading: "that is, by way of certainty about it, unless it is by revelation". He concludes: "so it is not said of everyone killed in jihad that he is a martyr, because he may be like this [man], even though he is, besides that, given the rule of the martyrs in the outward rulings" (Fath al-bari 6:90).

The creeds hold the same rule for every Muslim. Al-Tahawi: "and we do not place any one of them in a Garden or a Fire" (art. 70). He excepts the ten: "we testify for them to the Garden, as the Messenger of God testified for them" (art. 95; Abu Dawud 4649, Tirmidhi 3747). Al-Nasafi: "we testify to the Garden for the ten to whom the Messenger of God gave the good news of the Garden, and we do not testify to the Garden or the Fire for any one by himself" (p. 198).

**The Twelver texts** put the fighting itself under the Imam: "'I saw in a dream that I said to you: Fighting with other than the Imam whose obedience is made obligatory [is] forbidden, like carrion and blood and the flesh of swine; and you said to me: It is so.' So Abu 'Abd Allah said: It is so, it is so." (al-Kafi 5:23:3, unknown and broken chain). Without him one fights only for "the core of Islam" (5:21:2, sound).

**Modern teaching.** The fatwas keep the general promise and refuse the particular one.
- Ibn Baz:

**binbaz:22873** (from Ibn Baz, binbaz.org.sa fatwa 22873, "The ruling on the words martyr and the one shown mercy, and the like" (from an audio answer))

> إذا مات في المعركة يسمى شهيدًا؛ فإن كان صادقًا مخلصًا فله الجنة، وإن كان مرائيًا فإثمه عليه. وأما المرحوم المغفور له لا، يقال: رحمه الله، غفر الله له، أهل السنة والجماعة لا يشهدون لأحد لا بجنة ولا نار إلا من شهد له الرسول

If he dies in the battle he is called a martyr; then if he was truthful [and] sincere, for him is the Garden, and if he was showing off, his sin is upon him. As for the one shown mercy [and] the one forgiven: no; one says: May God have mercy on him, may God forgive him. The people of the Sunna and the Community do not testify for anyone, neither to a Garden nor to a Fire, except whom the Messenger testified for.

- He adds: "a martyr in the legal ruling; as for what concerns the Garden and the Fire, that is [left] to God, glorified and exalted". In fatwa 5702: "because testifying for a particular [person] to a Garden or a Fire, or the like, is not permitted, except for whom God testified to that in His noble Book, or His Messenger testified for him".
- IslamQA 151904 quotes Ibn 'Uthaymin: "we do not testify for any particular individual", and "Thus we differentiate between speaking in general terms and speaking in specific terms."
- IslamQA 731: naming a person for the Garden or the Fire "is not permissible except in cases where Allaah or His Messenger (peace and blessings of Allaah be upon him) have informed us of it".
- IslamQA 333383: "And the martyr is with the people at the scene of the reckoning, in the courts of the Day of Resurrection; there is no doubt about that."
- Dar al-Ifta al-Misriyya (fatwa 7855, 2023) names three kinds of martyr: "the first: the martyr of this world and the hereafter: the one who is killed in the battle and the defence of the homelands"; and "the second: the martyr of this world: he is one who was killed likewise, but was killed retreating, or fought for show and repute, and the like; so he is a martyr in appearance and in the rulings of this world".
- The Saudi Permanent Committee, of a woman who died in childbirth: "she is counted a martyr, God willing" (fatwa 19050).

#### Conclusion

- **Part 1 is corroborated with qualifications.** The narrow bridge over Hell and the uncertain end are standard teaching in the Qur'an, both Sahihs, the creeds and al-Ghazali. The words "thinner than the hair and sharper than the sword" are Abu Sa'id's report of what reached him in Muslim. They are the Prophet's only in reports outside the Sahihs. The nearest wordings to a knife are "the edge of the sword" and Salman's razor. The texts hold the fear against an equal hope, and condemn despair as they condemn security.
- **Part 2 is corroborated with qualifications.** In Sunni teaching the Garden is bounty, and a sinner's end lies in God's will. The same teaching holds the promise to believers as one that does not fail. Mu'tazili and Twelver theology state reward as owed in justice.
- **Part 3 is partly corroborated.** Death in jihad carries the most prominent guarantee and the highest rank. The guarantee is to a class, and it is bound to intention, to patience and to the debt. It is one guaranteed way among several. No text, creed or fatwa read lets any one fighter be declared in the Garden. The Twelver texts also require the Imam.

### 2.15 What is promised to men and to women, and how narrow the way is: a literal comparison

This section sets side by side, as literally as the texts allow:
- what the Qur'an and the Hadith promise men and women in the Garden;
- how they describe the women given to men there;
- the stated ways in, set against the texts on the bridge, the ending and the will of God.

The Qur'an comes first, then Bukhari and Muslim, then the Sunan with their grades. The classical commentators are used only to gloss words. The blocks are checked against their sources as in 2.8.

#### What is promised to men and to women

| | Men | Women | Source and grade |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Entry** | the same conditions: "from male or female, while he is a believer" | the same | Qur'an 4:124, 40:40; the paired list of 33:35; "the believing men and the believing women" (9:72) |
| **Spouses, in the Qur'an** | "purified spouses" (2:25); the *hur 'in* (44:54, 52:20); those "restraining [their] glances" (55:56); virgins (56:36); "swelling-breasted [maidens]" (78:33) | no counterpart named. The companions are all grammatically feminine. The "youths made everlasting" serve with cups (56:17 to 18) | Qur'an. *Zawj*, "spouse", is either sex, but al-Tabari reads 2:25 as "the wife of a man" (2.2) |
| **Earthly spouses** | "Enter the Garden, you and your spouses, made glad" (43:70); "whoever was righteous among their fathers and their spouses and their offspring" (13:23) | the same verses | Qur'an |
| **How many, in the Hadith** | two wives each (Bukhari 3245, 3254; Muslim 2834); seventy-two for the martyr (Tirmidhi 1663); seventy-two for every man (Ibn Majah 4337); seventy-two, with eighty thousand servants, for the lowest in rank (Tirmidhi 2562) | "there is not in the Garden anyone unmarried" (Muslim 2834). A woman married twice "chooses the better of them in character" (Shia, *Thawab al-a'mal* 688) | Sahih for the two wives; mostly Sahih for the martyr (Zai Hasan); Very Da'if for every man; Da'if for the lowest (Shakir, al-Albani; Zai Hasan). The Shia report has no grade, and its chain is raised with a gap |
| **Strength** | "the strength of a hundred" in intercourse (Tirmidhi 2536) | not stated | Hasan Sahih (Shakir, al-Albani); Zai Da'if |
| **Proportion** | | "the fewest of the inhabitants of the Garden are women" (Muslim 2738); of the Fire, "most of its people [were] women" (Bukhari 3241) | Sahih |
| **Age** | all the people of the Garden enter as "sons of thirty or of thirty-three years" (Tirmidhi 2545) | the companions are "age-mates [of one another]" (56:37, 78:33, 38:52), thirty-three years in four commentaries (below) | Tirmidhi 2545: Hasan (Shakir, al-Albani, Bashar); Zai Da'if |

The key reports in full:

**Muslim 2834** (from Abu Hurayra (through Muhammad b. Sirin))

> إِمَّا تَفَاخَرُوا وَإِمَّا تَذَاكَرُوا الرِّجَالُ فِي الْجَنَّةِ أَكْثَرُ أَمِ النِّسَاءُ فَقَالَ أَبُو هُرَيْرَةَ أَوَلَمْ يَقُلْ أَبُو الْقَاسِمِ صلى الله عليه وسلم ‏ "‏ إِنَّ أَوَّلَ زُمْرَةٍ تَدْخُلُ الْجَنَّةَ عَلَى صُورَةِ الْقَمَرِ لَيْلَةَ الْبَدْرِ وَالَّتِي تَلِيهَا عَلَى أَضْوَإِ كَوْكَبٍ دُرِّيٍّ فِي السَّمَاءِ لِكُلِّ امْرِئٍ مِنْهُمْ زَوْجَتَانِ اثْنَتَانِ يُرَى مُخُّ سُوقِهِمَا مِنْ وَرَاءِ اللَّحْمِ وَمَا فِي الْجَنَّةِ أَعْزَبُ

Either they boasted or they discussed: are men more [numerous] in the Garden, or women? Abu Hurayra said: Did not Abu al-Qasim say: "Indeed, the first group to enter the Garden are in the form of the moon on the night of the full moon, and the one following it upon [the form of] the brightest brilliant star in the heaven. Each man of them has two wives, two, the marrow of whose shins is seen from beyond the flesh; and there is not in the Garden anyone unmarried."

**Bukhari 3254** (from Abu Hurayra; also bukhari:3245, bukhari:3246; muslim:2834)

> أَوَّلُ زُمْرَةٍ تَدْخُلُ الْجَنَّةَ عَلَى صُورَةِ الْقَمَرِ لَيْلَةَ الْبَدْرِ، وَالَّذِينَ عَلَى آثَارِهِمْ كَأَحْسَنِ كَوْكَبٍ دُرِّيٍّ فِي السَّمَاءِ إِضَاءَةً، قُلُوبُهُمْ عَلَى قَلْبِ رَجُلٍ وَاحِدٍ، لاَ تَبَاغُضَ بَيْنَهُمْ وَلاَ تَحَاسُدَ، لِكُلِّ امْرِئٍ زَوْجَتَانِ مِنَ الْحُورِ الْعِينِ، يُرَى مُخُّ سُوقِهِنَّ مِنْ وَرَاءِ الْعَظْمِ وَاللَّحْمِ

The first group to enter the Garden are in the form of the moon on the night of the full moon, and those upon their traces are as the most beautiful brilliant star in the heaven in brightness. Their hearts are upon the heart of one man; there is no mutual hatred among them and no mutual envy. Each man has two wives of the white-eyed, wide-eyed [ones] (hur 'in); the marrow of their shins is seen from beyond the bone and the flesh.

**Tirmidhi 1663** (from al-Miqdam b. Ma'dikarib; also ibnmajah:2799; grade: Ahmad Muhammad Shakir: Sahih; Al-Albani: Sahih; Bashar Awad Maarouf: Sahih; Zubair Ali Zai: Hasan; Tirmidhi: هَذَا حَدِيثٌ صَحِيحٌ غَرِيبٌ "this is a hadith sahih gharib")

> لِلشَّهِيدِ عِنْدَ اللَّهِ سِتُّ خِصَالٍ يُغْفَرُ لَهُ فِي أَوَّلِ دَفْعَةٍ وَيَرَى مَقْعَدَهُ مِنَ الْجَنَّةِ وَيُجَارُ مِنْ عَذَابِ الْقَبْرِ وَيَأْمَنُ مِنَ الْفَزَعِ الأَكْبَرِ وَيُوضَعُ عَلَى رَأْسِهِ تَاجُ الْوَقَارِ الْيَاقُوتَةُ مِنْهَا خَيْرٌ مِنَ الدُّنْيَا وَمَا فِيهَا وَيُزَوَّجُ اثْنَتَيْنِ وَسَبْعِينَ زَوْجَةً مِنَ الْحُورِ الْعِينِ وَيُشَفَّعُ فِي سَبْعِينَ مِنْ أَقَارِبِهِ

"For the martyr (shahid) with God [are] six traits: he is forgiven at the first gush [of his blood]; and he sees his seat in the Garden; and he is protected from the punishment of the grave; and he is safe from the greatest terror; and there is placed on his head the crown of dignity, a ruby of which is better than this world and what is in it; and he is married to seventy-two wives of the white-eyed, wide-eyed [ones] (hur 'in); and he is made intercessor for seventy of his relatives."

**Ibn Majah 4337** (from Abu Umama; grade: Al-Albani: Very Daif; Muhammad Fouad Abd al-Baqi: Very Daif; Shuaib Al Arnaut: Very Daif; Zubair Ali Zai: Daif)

> مَا مِنْ أَحَدٍ يُدْخِلُهُ اللَّهُ الْجَنَّةَ إِلاَّ زَوَّجَهُ اللَّهُ عَزَّ وَجَلَّ ثِنْتَيْنِ وَسَبْعِينَ زَوْجَةً ثِنْتَيْنِ مِنَ الْحُورِ الْعِينِ وَسَبْعِينَ مِنْ مِيرَاثِهِ مِنْ أَهْلِ النَّارِ مَا مِنْهُنَّ وَاحِدَةٌ إِلاَّ وَلَهَا قُبُلٌ شَهِيٌّ وَلَهُ ذَكَرٌ لاَ يَنْثَنِي

ar: قَالَ هِشَامُ بْنُ خَالِدٍ مِنْ مِيرَاثِهِ مِنْ أَهْلِ النَّارِ يَعْنِي رِجَالاً دَخَلُوا النَّارَ فَوَرِثَ أَهْلُ الْجَنَّةِ نِسَاءَهُمْ كَمَا وُرِثَتِ امْرَأَةُ فِرْعَوْنَ There is no one whom God admits to the Garden but God, mighty and majestic, marries him to seventy-two wives: two of the white-eyed, wide-eyed [ones] (hur 'in), and seventy of his inheritance from the people of the Fire. There is not one of them but she has a desirous vulva (qubul shahi), and he has a member that does not bend. [The transmitter] Hisham b. Khalid said: "Of his inheritance from the people of the Fire" means men who entered the Fire, and the people of the Garden inherited their women, as the wife of Pharaoh was inherited.

**Tirmidhi 2562** (from Abu Sa'id al-Khudri; grade: Ahmad Muhammad Shakir: Daif; Al-Albani: Daif; Zubair Ali Zai: Hasan; Tirmidhi: هَذَا حَدِيثٌ غَرِيبٌ لاَ نَعْرِفُهُ إِلاَّ مِنْ حَدِيثِ رِشْدِينَ بْنِ سَعْدٍ "this is a hadith gharib; we do not know it except from the hadith of Rishdin b. Sa'd")

> أَدْنَى أَهْلِ الْجَنَّةِ الَّذِي لَهُ ثَمَانُونَ أَلْفَ خَادِمٍ وَاثْنَتَانِ وَسَبْعُونَ زَوْجَةً وَتُنْصَبُ لَهُ قُبَّةٌ مِنْ لُؤْلُؤٍ وَزَبَرْجَدٍ وَيَاقُوتٍ كَمَا بَيْنَ الْجَابِيَةِ إِلَى صَنْعَاءَ ‏"‏ ‏.‏ وَبِهَذَا الإِسْنَادِ عَنِ النَّبِيِّ صلى الله عليه وسلم قَالَ ‏"‏ مَنْ مَاتَ مِنْ أَهْلِ الْجَنَّةِ مِنْ صَغِيرٍ أَوْ كَبِيرٍ يُرَدُّونَ بَنِي ثَلاَثِينَ فِي الْجَنَّةِ لاَ يَزِيدُونَ عَلَيْهَا أَبَدًا وَكَذَلِكَ أَهْلُ النَّارِ ‏"‏ ‏.‏ وَبِهَذَا الإِسْنَادِ عَنِ النَّبِيِّ صلى الله عليه وسلم قَالَ ‏"‏ إِنَّ عَلَيْهِمُ التِّيجَانَ إِنَّ أَدْنَى لُؤْلُؤَةٍ مِنْهَا لَتُضِيءُ مَا بَيْنَ الْمَشْرِقِ وَالْمَغْرِبِ

The lowest of the people of the Garden is he who has eighty thousand servants and seventy-two wives, and there is set up for him a dome of pearl and chrysolite and ruby, as [wide as] between al-Jabiya and San'a'. And with this isnad from the Prophet, he said: "Whoever dies of the people of the Garden, small or great, they are returned [as] sons of thirty in the Garden; they never increase beyond it; and likewise the people of the Fire." And with this isnad from the Prophet, he said: "On them are the crowns; the least pearl of them would light up what is between the east and the west."

**thawab:688** (from the Messenger of God, answering Umm Salama (Ibn Babawayh, Thawab al-a'mal, chapter on the reward of good character); grade: none given; the chain is raised (rafa'ahu) with a gap)

> الْمَرْأَهُ یَکُونُ لَهَا زَوْجَانِ فَیَمُوتَانِ فَیَدْخُلَانِ الْجَنَّهَ لِأَیِّهِمَا تَکُونُ فَقَالَ النَّبِیُّ ص تَخَیَّرُ أَحْسَنَهُمَا خُلُقاً وَ خَیْرَهُمَا لِأَهْلِهِ

[Umm Salama asked:] "The woman has two husbands, and they die and enter the Garden: to which of them will she be?" The Prophet said: "She chooses the better of them in character and the better of them to his family."

A Sunni report on the woman with more than one husband is usually cited from al-Tabarani, a collection not swept here.

#### The women of the Garden, described literally

The Qur'an's own words are given in 2.2:
- "made them virgins, loving, age-mates [of one another]" (56:36 to 37);
- "swelling-breasted [maidens], age-mates [of one another]" (78:33);
- "whom no man before them has touched, nor jinn" (55:56, 55:74);
- "restraining [their] glances" (38:52, 55:56).

**"Swelling-breasted" (*kawa'ib*).** The word gives shape, not size, and no verse or report swept gives a size. Two commentators gloss it:

**tabari:78:33** (from Ibn Zayd, in al-Tabari (d. 923), on 78:33)

> الكواعب: التي قد نهدت وكَعَبَ ثديها

The kawa'ib: she who has become full-breasted (nahadat), and whose breast has become rounded (ka'aba).

**ibnkathir:78:33** (from Ibn Kathir (d. 1373), on 78:33)

> نواهد يعنون أن ثديهن نواهد لم يتدلين لأنهن أبكار عرب أتراب أي في سن واحد

Nawahid: they mean that their breasts are prominent (nawahid), not sagging, because they are virgins; loving, age-mates, that is, in one age.

**"Age-mates" (*atrab*, singular *tirb*).** The word is relational, but the verses do not say with whom. Five commentators read it of the women among themselves:
- **Four give the age as thirty-three.** These are Ibn Kathir (from Ibn 'Abbas), al-Qurtubi, al-Baghawi and al-Sa'di.
- **Two give another reading as well.** Al-Tabari and Ibn Kathir add that the women are sisterly, without the jealousy of co-wives.
- **None of the five says *equal to their husbands*.**

The age of thirty-three is also the age Tirmidhi 2545 gives all the people of the Garden. The commentators do not link the two, so the women's equality in age to the man is an inference from putting the two together. It is not the text.

**tabari:56:37** (from al-Tabari (d. 923), on 56:37)

> وقوله: ( أَتْرَابًا ) يعني أنهنّ مستويات على سنّ واحدة، واحدتهنّ تِرْب

And His saying "atraban" means that they are level, upon one age; the singular of them is tirb.

**ibnkathir:56:37** (from Ibn Kathir (d. 1373), on 56:37, citing al-Dahhak from Ibn 'Abbas)

> وقوله : ( أترابا ) قال الضحاك ، عن ابن عباس يعني : في سن واحدة ، ثلاث وثلاثين سنة

And His saying "atraban": al-Dahhak said, from Ibn 'Abbas, it means: in one age, thirty-three years.

**qurtubi:56:37** (from al-Qurtubi (d. 1273), on 56:37)

> أترابا على ميلاد واحد في الاستواء وسن واحدة ثلاث وثلاثين سنة . يقال في النساء : أتراب وفي الرجال : أقران

Atraban: upon one birth, in being level, and one age, thirty-three years. It is said of women: atrab, and of men: aqran.

**saadi:56:37** (from al-Sa'di (d. 1956), on 56:37)

> والأتراب اللاتي على سن واحدة، ثلاث وثلاثين سنة، التي هي غاية ما يتمنى ونهاية سن الشباب

And the atrab: those who are upon one age, thirty-three years, which is the utmost of what is wished for and the end of the age of youth.

**baghawi:38:52** (from al-Baghawi (d. 1122), on 38:52)

> مستويات الأسنان ، بنات ثلاثة وثلاثين سنة ، واحدها ترب . وعن مجاهد قال : متواخيات لا يتباغضن ولا يتغايرن

Level in ages, daughters of thirty-three years; its singular is tirb. And from Mujahid, he said: sisterly; they do not hate one another and are not jealous of one another.

**ibnkathir:56:37b** (from Ibn Kathir (d. 1373), on 56:37, citing al-Suddi)

> وقال السدي : ( أترابا ) أي : في الأخلاق ، المتواخيات بينهن ، ليس بينهن تباغض ولا تحاسد ، يعني : لا كما كن ضرائر [ في الدنيا ] ضرائر متعاديات

And al-Suddi said: "atraban", that is, in character: sisterly among themselves; there is no mutual hatred among them and no mutual envy; meaning, not as they were co-wives [in this world], hostile co-wives.

**tabari:38:52** (from al-Tabari (d. 923), on 38:52)

> وقال بعضهم: متواخيات لا يتباغضن, ولا يتعادين, ولا يتغايرن, ولا يتحاسدن.

And some of them said: sisterly; they do not hate one another, nor are hostile to one another, nor are jealous of one another, nor envy one another.

**Virginity restored.** The Qur'an says the women were made virgins and were untouched before. No text swept here says their virginity is restored. That covers the Qur'an, the seven Sunni books and the Shia texts. The belief is usually cited from later collections, chiefly al-Tabarani, which were not swept.

**Not the man's age, not a size, not a renewal.** These three readings are common in retelling, but they are not in the texts swept.

#### The stated ways in

Sections 2.3 and 2.9 to 2.13 list the ways in. In brief:
- **Belief with righteous deeds.** This is the base, in 69 verses of the Qur'an.
- **The testimony.** It is joined to the floor that whoever has "an atom's weight of good" comes out of the Fire (Bukhari 44).
- **Many particular acts,** each with a stated reward.
- **Jihad and martyrdom.** These carry God's explicit guarantee:

**Muslim 1876** (from Abu Hurayra; also bukhari:36, 2787, 3123, 7457, 7463)

> تَضَمَّنَ اللَّهُ لِمَنْ خَرَجَ فِي سَبِيلِهِ لاَ يُخْرِجُهُ إِلاَّ جِهَادًا فِي سَبِيلِي وَإِيمَانًا بِي وَتَصْدِيقًا بِرُسُلِي فَهُوَ عَلَىَّ ضَامِنٌ أَنْ أُدْخِلَهُ الْجَنَّةَ أَوْ أَرْجِعَهُ إِلَى مَسْكَنِهِ الَّذِي خَرَجَ مِنْهُ نَائِلاً مَا نَالَ مِنْ أَجْرٍ أَوْ غَنِيمَةٍ

"God has guaranteed, for whoever went out in His way, nothing bringing him out but jihad in My way and faith in Me and belief in My messengers: he is upon Me a guarantee, that I admit him to the Garden, or return him to his dwelling from which he went out, obtaining what he obtained of reward or booty."

The martyr's path is the only one that joins that guarantee to a counted reward, the seventy-two of Tirmidhi 1663, in a well-graded report. Other deeds carry the same guarantee wording (2.14).

#### How narrow the way is

**The bridge.** Every soul comes down to it (19:71). The Prophet's description in the Sahihs has the deeds carry men across, "until the deeds of the servants fail":

**Muslim 195** (from Abu Hurayra and Hudhayfa)

> ثُمَّ كَمَرِّ الرِّيحِ ثُمَّ كَمَرِّ الطَّيْرِ وَشَدِّ الرِّجَالِ تَجْرِي بِهِمْ أَعْمَالُهُمْ وَنَبِيُّكُمْ قَائِمٌ عَلَى الصِّرَاطِ يَقُولُ رَبِّ سَلِّمْ سَلِّمْ حَتَّى تَعْجِزَ أَعْمَالُ الْعِبَادِ حَتَّى يَجِيءَ الرَّجُلُ فَلاَ يَسْتَطِيعُ السَّيْرَ إِلاَّ زَحْفًا - قَالَ - وَفِي حَافَتَىِ الصِّرَاطِ كَلاَلِيبُ مُعَلَّقَةٌ مَأْمُورَةٌ بِأَخْذِ مَنْ أُمِرَتْ بِهِ فَمَخْدُوشٌ نَاجٍ وَمَكْدُوسٌ فِي النَّارِ

[The first of you passes like lightning,] "then like the passing of the wind, then like the passing of the bird and the running of men, their deeds carrying them along; and your Prophet stands on the bridge (al-sirat), saying: Lord, keep safe, keep safe; until the deeds of the servants fail, until the man comes who cannot travel except by crawling." He said: "And on the two edges of the bridge are hooks hung, commanded to seize whomever they are commanded [to seize]; so [one is] scratched [and] saved, and [one is] heaped up in the Fire."

The well-known "thinner than the hair and sharper than the sword" is Abu Sa'id's own report of what reached him, not the Prophet's words:

**Muslim 183** (from Abu Sa'id al-Khudri, in his own words)

> قَالَ أَبُو سَعِيدٍ بَلَغَنِي أَنَّ الْجِسْرَ أَدَقُّ مِنَ الشَّعْرَةِ وَأَحَدُّ مِنَ السَّيْفِ

Abu Sa'id said: It has reached me (balaghani) that the bridge is thinner than the hair and sharper than the sword.

**The ending.**

**Muslim 2643** (from Ibn Mas'ud; also bukhari:3208, 3332, 6594, 7454)

> فَوَالَّذِي لاَ إِلَهَ غَيْرُهُ إِنَّ أَحَدَكُمْ لَيَعْمَلُ بِعَمَلِ أَهْلِ الْجَنَّةِ حَتَّى مَا يَكُونَ بَيْنَهُ وَبَيْنَهَا إِلاَّ ذِرَاعٌ فَيَسْبِقُ عَلَيْهِ الْكِتَابُ فَيَعْمَلُ بِعَمَلِ أَهْلِ النَّارِ فَيَدْخُلُهَا وَإِنَّ أَحَدَكُمْ لَيَعْمَلُ بِعَمَلِ أَهْلِ النَّارِ حَتَّى مَا يَكُونَ بَيْنَهُ وَبَيْنَهَا إِلاَّ ذِرَاعٌ فَيَسْبِقُ عَلَيْهِ الْكِتَابُ فَيَعْمَلُ بِعَمَلِ أَهْلِ الْجَنَّةِ فَيَدْخُلُهَا

"By Him besides whom there is no god, one of you does the deed of the people of the Garden until there is not between him and it but an arm's length, and the writing outstrips him, and he does the deed of the people of the Fire, and enters it; and one of you does the deed of the people of the Fire until there is not between him and it but an arm's length, and the writing outstrips him, and he does the deed of the people of the Garden, and enters it."

**No one by his deed.**

**Bukhari 5673** (from Abu Hurayra; also bukhari:6463; muslim:2816)

> لَنْ يُدْخِلَ أَحَدًا عَمَلُهُ الْجَنَّةَ ‏"‏‏.‏ قَالُوا وَلاَ أَنْتَ يَا رَسُولَ اللَّهِ قَالَ ‏"‏ لاَ، وَلاَ أَنَا إِلاَّ أَنْ يَتَغَمَّدَنِي اللَّهُ بِفَضْلٍ وَرَحْمَةٍ فَسَدِّدُوا وَقَارِبُوا

"His deed will never admit anyone to the Garden." They said: "Not even you, O Messenger of God?" He said: "No, not even I, unless God covers me with bounty and mercy. So aim right and come near."

**Whom He wills.**

**quran:3:129**

> وَلِلَّهِ مَا فِي السَّمَاوَاتِ وَمَا فِي الْأَرْضِ ۚ يَغْفِرُ لِمَنْ يَشَاءُ وَيُعَذِّبُ مَنْ يَشَاءُ ۚ وَاللَّهُ غَفُورٌ رَحِيمٌ

And to God [belongs] what is in the heavens and what is in the earth. He forgives whom He wills and punishes whom He wills; and God is Forgiving, Merciful.

**Abu Dawud 1420** (from 'Ubada b. al-Samit; also malik:267; nasai:461; ibnmajah:1401; grade: Al-Albani: Sahih; Muhammad Muhyi Al-Din Abdul Hamid: Sahih; Zubair Ali Zai: Isnaad Hasan)

> خَمْسُ صَلَوَاتٍ كَتَبَهُنَّ اللَّهُ عَلَى الْعِبَادِ فَمَنْ جَاءَ بِهِنَّ لَمْ يُضَيِّعْ مِنْهُنَّ شَيْئًا اسْتِخْفَافًا بِحَقِّهِنَّ كَانَ لَهُ عِنْدَ اللَّهِ عَهْدٌ أَنْ يُدْخِلَهُ الْجَنَّةَ وَمَنْ لَمْ يَأْتِ بِهِنَّ فَلَيْسَ لَهُ عِنْدَ اللَّهِ عَهْدٌ إِنْ شَاءَ عَذَّبَهُ وَإِنْ شَاءَ أَدْخَلَهُ الْجَنَّةَ

"Five prayers God has written upon the servants. Whoever comes with them, not having lost anything of them from making light of their due, has with God a covenant that He will admit him to the Garden. And whoever does not come with them has no covenant with God: if He wills, He punishes him, and if He wills, He admits him to the Garden."

The Sunni creed taught at al-Azhar puts it in one line: "If He rewards us, then by pure bounty; and if He punishes, then by pure justice" (2.14). The creeds deny that God owes anyone reward. They join His will to wisdom and justice, though, and hold His promise to believers to be sure. So *arbitrary* is not their word.

**The martyr's guarantee has its own limits:**
- **Intention.** The man who fought "so that it would be said" he was brave is cast into the Fire:

**Muslim 1905** (from Abu Hurayra)

> إِنَّ أَوَّلَ النَّاسِ يُقْضَى يَوْمَ الْقِيَامَةِ عَلَيْهِ رَجُلٌ اسْتُشْهِدَ فَأُتِيَ بِهِ فَعَرَّفَهُ نِعَمَهُ فَعَرَفَهَا قَالَ فَمَا عَمِلْتَ فِيهَا قَالَ قَاتَلْتُ فِيكَ حَتَّى اسْتُشْهِدْتُ ‏.‏ قَالَ كَذَبْتَ وَلَكِنَّكَ قَاتَلْتَ لأَنْ يُقَالَ جَرِيءٌ ‏.‏ فَقَدْ قِيلَ ‏.‏ ثُمَّ أُمِرَ بِهِ فَسُحِبَ عَلَى وَجْهِهِ حَتَّى أُلْقِيَ فِي النَّارِ

"The first of the people on whom judgment is given on the Day of Resurrection is a man who was martyred (ustushhida). He is brought, and He makes His favors known to him, and he knows them. He says: What did you do with them? He says: I fought for You until I was martyred. He says: You lied; rather you fought so that it would be said, [He is] bold (jari'); and it has been said. Then he is ordered, and dragged on his face until he is thrown into the Fire."

- **Debt.** It is the one thing the martyr's death does not wipe out:

**Muslim 1885** (from Abu Qatada; also muslim:1886)

> نَعَمْ وَأَنْتَ صَابِرٌ مُحْتَسِبٌ مُقْبِلٌ غَيْرُ مُدْبِرٍ إِلاَّ الدَّيْنَ فَإِنَّ جِبْرِيلَ عَلَيْهِ السَّلاَمُ قَالَ لِي ذَلِكَ

[A man asked: "Tell me, if I am killed in the way of God, are my sins expiated from me?" He said:] "Yes, while you are patient, seeking [the reward] (muhtasib), advancing, not retreating; except the debt, for Gabriel told me that."

- **No one may be named.** Bukhari's chapter heading forbids naming any one man a martyr (2.14).

#### The contrast

1. **The reward is stated concretely, and for men it is counted.** The count rises with the deed: two wives for the first to enter in the Sahihs, seventy-two for the martyr in Tirmidhi.
2. **Women are promised the same Garden, on the same conditions, with their husbands.** They are given no counted companions, and the Sahihs say they are the fewest of its people.
3. **The ways in are many, but every one stands under the bridge, the ending, and "whom He wills."** The deed does not admit by itself.
4. **One path joins an explicit guarantee to a counted reward: death in the way of God.** That guarantee is to a class, it is bound to intention and to debt, and it cannot be claimed for any one man.

### 2.16 The pattern across the Qur'an, the Hadith and the Sira

**The base is the same.** The Qur'an joins belief to righteous deeds (2.3). The Sahihs and the Sunan carry the same base: the testimony and the pillars. The floor is low:
- the testimony brings the Garden. To the question "And if he committed adultery and if he stole?" the answer is the same (Bukhari 1237, 5827);
- whoever has "an atom's weight of good" with the testimony comes out of the Fire (Bukhari 44);
- the intercession "is for the people of the great [sins] of my community" (Abu Dawud 4739).

**The Hadith add many particular acts to the base**, and most carry a stated reward. Among them:
- the twelve rak'as and the mosque, which each bring "a house in the Garden";
- the date split between two daughters (Muslim 2630);
- water for a thirsty dog (Bukhari 173);
- the branch removed from the road;
- not asking people for anything (Ibn Majah 1837).

**The ground of entry is stated more plainly than in the Qur'an.** The Qur'an gives both "what you used to do" and "the bounty of God" (7:43, 57:21). The Sahihs add: "His deed will never admit anyone to the Garden ... not even I, unless God covers me with bounty and mercy" (Bukhari 5673; Muslim 2816 to 2817). This sits beside two other teachings:
- **Predestination.** "His seat in the Fire and his seat in the Garden have been written," with the command still "Work" (Bukhari 4949).
- **The arm's length.** The deed of the people of the Garden can be undone at "an arm's length" from it (Muslim 2643).

**The language of exchange changes form.**
- **The Qur'an** says God "has bought" the believers' selves (9:111).
- **The Sahihs** use guarantee, "a right upon God", and the pledge "for the Garden" (Muslim 1876; Bukhari 2790, 3893). No report in them prices or trades the Garden itself.
- **The Sunan** have: "the merchandise of God is costly ... the merchandise of God is the Garden" (Tirmidhi 2450; al-Albani Sahih, Zai Da'if).
- **The Shia texts** have: "The price of the Garden is: There is no god but God, and God is greater" (al-Kafi 2:517:1, marfu').

**Fighting is the largest single category in the Sahihs** (23 distinct reports):
- "the Garden is beneath the shades of the swords" (Bukhari 2818);
- the hundred degrees "for those who strive in the way of God" (Bukhari 2790);
- the man who died without raiding "died on a branch of hypocrisy" (Muslim 1910);
- the martyr's six traits, among them "seventy-two wives of the white-eyed, wide-eyed [ones] (hur 'in)" and intercession "for seventy of his relatives" (Tirmidhi 1663; Sahih by Shakir, al-Albani and Bashar, Hasan by Zai).

The Twelver texts make fighting lawful only with the obligatory Imam (2.12).

**Who is saved is drawn more narrowly in the Hadith than in the Qur'an's own verses.**
- **The Sunni Sunan** give the seventy-three sects, "seventy-two in the Fire and one in the Garden, and it is the community (al-jama'a)" (Abu Dawud 4597; Hasan). None of these reports is in Bukhari or Muslim.
- **The Twelver texts** make the wilaya of the Imams the condition of every deed's reward, and put the *nasib*, the one hostile to the Prophet's household, beyond intercession (al-Kafi 2:18:5, sound).

**The descriptions grow fuller.**
- The first group enters "in the form of the moon on the night of the full moon" (Bukhari 3245).
- Each man has wives, and "there is not in the Garden anyone unmarried" (Muslim 2834).
- The lowest in rank receives "the like of the world and ten times its like" (Muslim 186).
- Above all is the sight of God: "you will see your Lord as you see this moon" (Bukhari 554), and God's good pleasure, after which He "will not be displeased" with them ever (Bukhari 6549).

**The proportions.**
- "Most of its people [were] the poor" (Bukhari 3241).
- In the Fire, "most of its people [were] women" (Bukhari 3241).
- "The fewest of the inhabitants of the Garden are women" (Muslim 2738).
